# The Teaching Core of the Kephalaia

*Extracted from the Coptic Kephalaia of the Teacher (Gardner, Brill 1995).*
*Later editorial layers — hagiographic frame, pastoral instructions,*
*and explicit Christian overlay — have been removed. Mixed passages*
*have been repaired to recover the teaching content. The text is*
*presented in its original chapter order.*

---

**Chapters analyzed**: 123
**Chapters with core content**: 112
**Core text words**: ~50,789

## Chapter 0
### Introduction

*This introductory unit begins with a compact cosmogonic survey (eternal separation, assault of Darkness, First Man, Living Spirit’s construction, Third Ambassador, final fire and binding) in an impersonal, cosmological voice—likely preserving the oldest teaching substrate. After this, the register pivots sharply into self-authorization (Mani’s books), institutional/audience address, and comparative-apostolic polemic (Jesus/Zarathustra/Buddha), with strong late pastoral/application drift and a detected editorial extension.*

⟨p.1⟩

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Cosmogonic-cosmological exposition (separation of principles; aeons; heights) with no audience exhortation, institutional terms, or citation formulas; matches impersonal core survey style despite lacunae.
- ¶2: Binary-principle cosmology (Darkness vs Light) stated as ontological geography; no editorial anchoring to contemporary institutions.
- ¶3: Clear seam from meta-discourse about writing/teaching (‘written for you’) into a compact mythic event statement (attack of Darkness). The latter stays cosmos→cosmos and fits the old cosmogonic narrative.
- ¶4: Mythic-cosmological sequence (emanation/descent of First Man; armour of Light) without institutional application. ‘Crucified’ reads as Manichaean mythic metaphor here (no Gospel citation/NT narrative cues).
- ¶5: System-building cosmology (construction of universe; ordering of cosmic structures; light/dark mechanics) in impersonal expository voice—typical substrate material.
- ¶6: Architectonic cosmology with numbered/structured geography (three earths; four mountains; three vessels) and binding function—strong core teaching signature.
- ¶7: Named cosmic redeemer in functional role (purification/redemption) stated without Gospel/NT linkage; consistent with core cosmological hierarchy.
- ¶8: Eschatological cosmology (cosmic fire; ascent of Light; binding of Darkness; final reign) remains within the cosmic system; the second-person ‘You find’ reads as generic conclusion rather than institutional exhortation.
- ¶9: Brief doctrinal statement on primordial mixture/joining—core cosmological premise; no institutional or citation markers.
- ¶10: Canonical self-referential claim listing Mani’s named books (‘Great Gospel’, ‘Treasury’, etc.) functions as justificatory apparatus for scripture/authority, not impersonal cosmology (Gardner flag: canonical_source).
- ¶11: Authorial validation of ‘writer/teacher of truth’ and ‘I … have revealed’ is legitimating rhetoric (meta-discourse about authority), characteristic of later framing rather than substrate instruction.
- ¶12: Underlying numbered/spatial schematic (‘twelve’, ‘doors’, ‘four corners’) suggests an older correspondential lesson structure, but the paragraph is contaminated by autobiographical ‘my pains/afflictions’ and didactic evaluation, indicating later overlaying voice.
- ¶13: Seam-flagged institutional extension: disciples commanded to write; explicit community categories (‘teachers’, ‘leaders’, ‘elect’, ‘catechumens’, ‘free men/women’) and retrospective collection of homilies—editorial church-operational material.
- ¶14: Pure imperative/application voice (‘You must remember… write… gather…’) with no cosmological mechanism—pastoral exhortation.
- ¶15: Community-tier instruction and institutional categories (‘elect’, ‘suckling babes’) plus rhetorical self-reporting; not cosmos→cosmos mapping, but pastoral/adaptive teaching claims.
- ¶16: Ecclesial and apostolic discourse (‘apostles’, ‘fathers’, ‘church’) with application markers (‘Concerning this…’) and mission geography—late institutional reflection rather than core cosmology.
- ¶17: Explicit Jesus-historical narrative (west; disciples writing parables, signs, wonders) enters via Christian tradition/NT-world biography—classified as Christian overlay.
- ¶18: Hagiographic-comparative prophet material (‘apostle of light… Persia… Hystaspes… Zarathustra… they read today’) used to justify writing/authority; not impersonal cosmological teaching.
- ¶19: Comparative-apostolic narrative about Buddha and his churches/scriptures serves the same canonical justification theme; late framing rhetoric.
- ¶20: Polemic about institutional decay/adulteration when teachings aren’t written; focuses on ‘church’ persistence and leadership failure—pastoral-institutional argumentation.
- ¶21: Commands/commandments + exhortation to write after the speaker ‘so that it leads you not astray’—application voice and community safeguarding, not core cosmology.
- ¶22: Direct audience address and imperative (‘remember! And write…’) about recording oral wisdom—late pastoral instruction justifying the compilation.
- ¶23: Concluding encouragement about benefits of writing; practical exhortation, not cosmological exposition.

## Chapter 1
### Concerning the Ad[vent] of the Apostle.

*This chapter is dominated by later layers: dialogue framing, apostolic-succession instruction, and extensive New Testament-derived narrative (Jesus, Judas, Paul, Paraclete sayings). The only recoverable correspondential/cosmological substrate appears as brief embedded summaries of mysteries (Depths/Heights; Light/Darkness; construction of the Ships; purification mechanics; Adam myth), preserved inside Mani-autobiographical revelation rhetoric.*

He unveiled to me the hidden mystery, the one that is hidden from the worlds and the generations, the mystery of the depths and the heights. He unveiled to me the mystery of the Light and the Darkness; the mystery of the calamity of conflict, and the war, and the great [...], the battle that the Darkness spread about. Afterwards, he unveiled to me also: How the Light [...] the Darkness, through their mingling this universe was set up [...]

He opened my eyes also to the way that the ships were constructed; to enable the gods of Light to be in them, to purify the Light from creation. Conversely, the dregs and the effluent [...] to the abyss. The mystery of the fashioning of Adam, the first man. He also informed me about the mystery of the tree of knowledge, which Adam ate from; his eyes saw.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue-frame setup: disciples questioning the Teacher about his apostolate; biographical/topic-introduction voice rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Explicit speech-attribution formula (“The apostle says…”), a compiling/community frame marker.
- ¶3: Second-person address (“O my beloved ones”) and analogy applied to apostles/churches in historical time; not cosmos→cosmos mapping but institutional/applied instruction.
- ¶4: Continuation of agricultural/merchant imagery to explain apostolic work; remains an applied analogy, not systematic cosmological architecture.
- ¶5: Institutional categories (Elect, catechumens) and ‘church-formation’ language; this is application to community structure (editorial/institutional extension).
- ¶6: Apostle/church-cycle instruction; ‘flesh’/‘greatness’ language serves the institutional narrative rather than presenting impersonal cosmological mechanisms.
- ¶7: Farmer-cycle simile continuing the apostolic succession teaching; applied discourse rather than substrate cosmology.
- ¶8: Apostles from the ‘moulding of humanity’ in a salvation-history frame; not a degree-structured cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶9: Ecclesial succession and post-mortem help for the church; ‘support/leader/guardian’ roles are institutional-hagiographic rather than cosmological taxonomy.
- ¶10: Supportive analogies (farmer/merchant) reinforcing the apostolic-succession lesson; no substrate system exposition.
- ¶11: Tree/fruit propagation used to argue continuous revelation; applied ecclesiology (church/apostles) rather than cosmic correspondential registers.
- ¶12: Institutional cycle: another apostle sent as the former church ascends; despite ascent language, the subject remains church succession and salvation-history.
- ¶13: List of apostles across eras (Sethel…Buddha…Zarathustra…Jesus) is salvation-history/mission ideology, not impersonal cosmological teaching.
- ¶14: New-Testament vector: docetic Jesus language tied to apostolic preaching (Phil 2 echo) and Judas betrayal narrative; enters via Christian tradition.
- ¶15: Passion/resurrection sequence and Holy Spirit breathing are explicitly NT narrative material.
- ¶16: Continuation of the Jesus-apostles narrative frame; still part of the NT-derived overlay context.
- ¶17: Paul as apostle reinforcing others is NT-derived salvation-history.
- ¶18: ‘Narrow’ vs ‘broad’ road motif is Gospel-derived moralizing; late overlay application.
- ¶19: Ecclesial decline and a ‘righteous man’ in the last church; institutional history rather than cosmic system exposition.
- ¶20: Metaphor of tree stripped of fruit applied to church-history; not cosmological mechanics.
- ¶21: Explicit Gospel saying about sending the Paraclete (Johannine citation/paraphrase) marks NT entry vector.
- ¶22: Paraclete speech paraphrases John 16 (upbraid concerning sin/righteousness/judgement); NT overlay.
- ¶23: Paraclete-of-truth salvation-history (“from Jesus until now”) continues the Johannine/Christian synthesis frame rather than an impersonal cosmic taxonomy.
- ¶24: Embedded cosmological summary: impersonal ‘mystery of…’ catalogue focused on cosmic architecture (Depths/Heights; Light/Darkness; mingling; cosmic battle). It is preserved inside later autobiographical revelation rhetoric, suggesting reuse of an older catechetical kernel.
- ¶25: The opening items remain within Manichaean cosmic mechanics (Ships as purification apparatus; Light separation; abyss disposal; Adam’s fashioning). The latter half turns outward to institutional categories (Elect/catechumens), likely a later editorial graft extending a mystery-list.
- ¶26: Rhetorical self-testimony and ecstatic totalizing claim (“I have become a single body, with a single Spirit!”) belongs to hagiographic/self-authorizing revelation style, not substrate exposition.
- ¶27: Mission-travel autobiography (preaching, choosing an election) functions as apostolic vitae material.
- ¶28: Extended historical itinerary with kings and lands; biographical/mission frame.
- ¶29: Addresses ‘holy church’ and ‘good election’, claims superiority of Mani’s mission; institutional self-legitimation rather than cosmic system teaching.
- ¶30: Meta-textual remark about writing books and repeating a lesson ‘in brief’; editorial-authorial closure voice.
- ¶31: Formulaic closing: disciples rejoice, thank, and acclaim him as Paraclete; classic hagiographic closure.

## Chapter 2
### The Second, concerning the Parable of the Tree.

*The chapter opens in a Gospel-driven dialogue frame (Q&A + explicit Gospel citation) and then digresses into NT exempla (Judas/Paul) and sectarian polemic. The oldest teaching substrate emerges most clearly from ¶11 onward in systematic five-fold correspondences (five limbs/mental qualities mapped to cosmic hypostases and ships) and in the negative five-fold of the bad tree culminating in the "lump" and Darkness. Two strong institutional grafts occur as editorial seams (¶13, ¶16), where the correspondential pattern is extended to the holy church/catechumens/alms.*

[ ... ] how I will reveal to you [ ... ] the expound[er ... ] the trees [ ... ] the Father [ ... ] all of them, as he [ ... ] in the [ ... ] world [ ... ] this good tree, the [ ... ] the light of this good tree [ ... ] the Pillar [ ... ]

⟨p.20⟩ ... the intellectuals that are cloaked upon its body [ ... ] this good tree. They are the light elements that are mingled, being compounded in the totality.

Behold, this is the good tree [ ... ]

[ ... ] I reveal to you [ ... ] it has five limbs. [They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind.] [Its counsel] is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. [Its insight is the Fir]st Man who dwells in the ship of living waters. Its thought is the Third Ambassador [who dwells in] the ship of living fire, that shines in [ ... ] Also, the mind is the Father who dwells in greatness, who is perfect in the aeons of light.

It is consideration that shall [ ... ] they shall be raised up [ ... ] is counsel; they shall be raised up to insight, which is the First Man who dwells in the ship of the night; from insight they shall rise up to thought, which is the Ambassador who dwells in the ship of the day. And he too, great glorious thought, shall bring them in to mind; which is the Father, the God of truth, the great Mind of all the aeons of glory.

⟨p.21⟩ [ ... ] bad [ ... ] happen in [ ... ] from [ ... ] also [ ... ] the way that it is [ ... ] the good tree [ ... ] of the fire [ ... ] they are the ruling-power [ ... ] heaven and the wheel [ ... ] they are the five fleshes that walk [ ... ] fruit [ ... te]aching as of error, the mystery that [ ... wh]ich Satan appointed in the world.

⟨p.22⟩ [ ... I will] reveal to you and you [ ... ] The bad tree has five limbs. They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind. Its consideration is the law of death. Its counsel is transmigration [ ... ] in type after type. Also, its insight is the furnaces of fire [ ... ge]henna; which is full of smoke. Thought [ ... ] vessel. Its mind is the [ ... ] the lump, the last bond, the [ ... ], they that Satan cast away [ ... ] the great [ ... ] and they fall [ ... ] the darkness [ ... ] all [ ... ] and they take away [ ... ] are swallowed in to the lump, and they are bound [ ... ] the fruit of darkness, the one that they [ ... ] for ever and ever [ ... ] his face [ ... ] will arrive at that place; and they have no repentance therein.

[ ... ] this is the explanation of the bad tree, as I have explained to you, which you [ ... ] to it [ ... ] good fruit [ ... ] its fruit bad [ ... ] in it, in its existent days [ ... ] in it.

it being changed from [ ... ] which lies in its good earth [ ... life] and light; as death and darkness do not exist [ ... ] Also, the bad tree is Matter [ ... ] it, I understand it alone among the [ ... wh]ich occurs in its bad earth [ ... ] darkness and death.

⟨p.23⟩ [ ... ] these two trees, and separates them one from another. He understands that they did not arise out of one another, nor did they come from one another. They did not come from one another. They did not come from one! The person who can separate them out may go up to the aeon of light,

And he sees [ ... ] bad fruit did not arise from it [ ... ]; but whoever will not separate out [ ... ] from one another, he falls to the land of darkness [ ... ] until he arrives at the [ ... ]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Brief editorial lead-in; not cosmological exposition, functions as chapter header/frame.
- ¶2: Dialogue frame + explicit Gospel citation formula (“As it is written in the Gospel…”) anchors this paragraph as NT-derived overlay.
- ¶3: Continues the beseeching/request frame and appeals to “scriptures”; functions as citation-driven setup rather than cosmological system teaching.
- ¶4: Sectarian polemic (“some among the sects…”) applies interpretation to contemporary groups rather than mapping within the cosmic system.
- ¶5: Hagiographic/compilational attribution (“our master Manichaios, the apostle of greatness”) plus polemical discussion; not a self-contained systematic cosmological mapping layer.
- ¶6: Continuation of argumentative exemplum about composite/natural mixture; lacks stable cosmos→cosmos correspondential mapping and reads as interpretive/polemical development.
- ¶7: NT narrative exemplum (Judas among the twelve; Satan entered him; handing the saviour to the Jews; crucifixion) entered via the Gospel tradition.
- ¶8: Continuation of the Judas exemplum; NT-derived narrative application.
- ¶9: NT narrative exemplum (Paul persecuting the church, revelation) is Christian overlay material, even where used to argue mixture.
- ¶10: Introduces interpretation explicitly through “the saviour preached in the parable”; the entry-vector is the Gospel parable rather than autonomous cosmological teaching.
- ¶11: Begins the internal cosmological identification of the good tree (Father/light/Pillar) without citing an external authority; the content pivots to cosmos-internal architecture.
- ¶12: Cosmos-internal correspondential language (intellectuals/elements/mingling/totality) consistent with the systematic substrate.
- ¶13: Seam-extended correspondential pattern applied to institution (“holy church… teachers… catechumens”); by rule, entire paragraph is an editorial graft despite cosmic terms (Jesus the Splendour).
- ¶14: Brief transitional closure within the cosmological exposition; no citation or institutional application, though the deictic “Behold” is stylistically later-sounding.
- ¶15: Underlying core is a systematic five-fold (mental qualities ↔ cosmic hypostases/ships). The single identification with “holy church” is a later application crossing outside the cosmic system.
- ¶16: Seam-identified institutional material (alms, catechumens, purification in holy church); entire paragraph is pastoral graft onto the teaching.
- ¶17: Classic substrate move: mental-process ascent mapped stepwise onto cosmic stations/ships and hypostases (First Man/ship of night; Ambassador/ship of day; Father/Mind). Impersonal/systematic sequence.
- ¶18: Reiterates the parable’s Gospel phrasing (“good tree… good fruit”) as a proof-text style closure; likely entered through the Gospel-parable overlay even without an explicit citation formula here.
- ¶19: Returns to cosmos-internal mechanics (ruling-power, heaven/wheel, five-fold structure, Satan as cosmic agent). Fragmentary but in the systematic explanatory register.
- ¶20: Application of the “taste” to contemporary opponents (“evil people, the sects… their teachers… law after law”); correspondence is used to point outward.
- ¶21: Appeal to “scriptures” plus master-attribution; functions as proof-text validation rather than autonomous cosmic mapping.
- ¶22: Core substrate is the negative five-fold mapping culminating in the “lump”/final bond and Darkness. A brief sectarian application intrudes into the otherwise cosmos→cosmos structure.
- ¶23: Brief wrap-up of the systematic exposition; despite first-person pedagogy, it stays within the doctrinal explanation rather than institutional rule or citation.
- ¶24: Underlying content is a compact dualist cosmological definition (good tree/good earth vs bad tree=Matter), but the paragraph opens with audience-address markers indicating later homiletic framing.
- ¶25: Core doctrinal kernel: absolute dualism (no mutual arising; not from one). Later layer adds beatitude/exhortation and teacher-address closure.
- ¶26: Despite conditional language, the logic is cosmological-mechanical (separation vs descent to Darkness) and remains within the system (land of Darkness as eschatological destination).
- ¶27: Beatitude-style faith formula (“Blessed is whoever can believe!”) is characteristic of Christianized exhortation and likely entered through the Christian overlay stream rather than the impersonal cosmological substrate.

## Chapter 3
### The Interpretation of Happiness, Wisdom and Power; what they signify.

*The core teaching is the five-level cosmological identification of ‘happiness, wisdom, power’ across divine domains (¶5–¶8). The chapter opens with a clear hagiographic/dialogue frame (¶1–¶4). A strong editorial seam occurs when the patterned formula is extended to the institutional church (¶9), followed by a brief exhortatory/pastoral closure (¶10), consistent with the chapter’s pastoral drift signal.*

[The happin]ess of the glorious one is the Father, the God of [truth, who] is established in the great land of [light]. ⟨p.24⟩ His glorious wis[d]om is his Great Spirit that [ ... ] below, which flows through all his aeons, and t[h]ey float therein. His great power is all the gods, the rich ones and the angels who were summoned from him as they [ ... ] t]hey that are called aeons [ ... ]

[ ... ] which is called] the sun [ ... the ship of] living fire, [ ... the Third Amba]ssador, the second greatne[ss ... The] glorious [happiness] is the [Living] Spirit, [ ... , the wisdom] is the Mother of Lif[e ... and great power] is [all] the gods, [the] rich o]nes and the angels who are within the ship.

Again, [h]appiness, wisdom and power exist in the s[hip of liv]ing waters [ ... ] the happiness [ ... ] the Mind of the Father. Also, wisdom [is the Vir]gi]n of [Li]ght. And the power that is [i]n the ship is [all] the go[ds], the [ri]ch ones and the angels who are established i[n it].

Again, these three exist in the elements: happiness, wis[d]o]m and power. The happiness is [the Pillar of Glory], the Perfect Man. Wisdom is the [five sons of the] Living Spirit; and great powe[r is ... the fi]ve sons of the First Man [ ... who are encl]osed and compounded in the totality, that [ ... ] while he supports the totality.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic opening (‘Once again…’, ‘enlightener’, ‘disciples’, ‘assembly of the church’) is editorial frame language, not substrate exposition.
- ¶2: Introductory question/contrast with ‘the world’ functions as chapter setup rather than systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping; likely part of the compilers’ dialogue framing (though the explicit Q&A attribution is partly lost in lacunae).
- ¶3: Continues the rhetorical setup (‘People boast…’, ‘as I have told you’) in an address-like voice, without cosmological degree mapping; best read as introductory frame material.
- ¶4: Transitional didactic address (‘listen… I may reveal to you’) introduces the triad but still functions as a preface; the substrate proper begins when the triad is mapped onto specific cosmic beings/domains.
- ¶5: Systematic triadic identification (happiness/wisdom/power) mapped directly onto the highest Light-divinity and its emanated orders; impersonal cosmological architecture (Father, land of Light, Great Spirit, aeons) is characteristic of the core.
- ¶6: Continues the same triadic mapping, now to the solar ship and its indwelling deities (Third Ambassador, Living Spirit, Mother of Life); this is cosmos→cosmos identification in a repeated schema.
- ¶7: Formulaic iteration (‘Again… exist in…’) mapping the triad to the lunar/‘living waters’ ship and its hypostases; stable cosmological repetition indicates an older teaching template.
- ¶8: Extends the triadic schema into the mixed cosmos (‘elements’) and subordinate divine structures (Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man; five sons of Living Spirit/First Man), still purely internal cosmic mechanics (no institutional application).
- ¶9: Editorial seam: bridge connective ‘Now, moreover’ extends the patterned ‘Again… exist in…’ series into the contemporary institution (‘holy church’, Apostle of Light, leaders/teachers, elect/catechumens). This is cosmos→church pointing, not substrate mapping.
- ¶10: Exhortatory summary (‘Blessed… is every one who will know them’) and the institutionalizing phrase ‘in the five chur[ches]’ suggest a concluding pastoral/editorial application rather than an original cosmological degree statement.
