## Chapter 4
### Concerning the Four Great Days that have come forth from one another, together with the Four Nights

*The chapter is predominantly systematic cosmological enumeration (four days/four nights; twelve hours/shadows) and reads largely as an older correspondential taxonomy. The main later intrusions are (a) hagiographic/compilational framing and beatitude-style closings (¶1, ¶6, ¶17), and (b) application that maps a cosmic register onto contemporary religious institutions (“church”, “sects”) inside otherwise cosmological sentences (¶5, ¶14–¶15), producing MIXED paragraphs.*

The first great day is the Father, the God of truth, the fir[st ... ... m]idst the aeons of his greatness, in his [l]iving kingdom. The twelve hours of this great day are the twelve great rich Gods of greatness. These, who are the first evocations that he evoked (to mirror) his greatness, he spread out to the four climes, three by three before his face.

The second day is the Third Ambassador, the one who dwells in the light ship. His twelve hours are the twelve virgins that he evoked in his greatness.

[The third day is the Pillar of Glory, the great porter that is greater than] all [the po]rters; the one that supports all [ ... ], those above and those below. Its twelve hours are the five sons of the First Man; the five sons of the Living Spirit, who support all the weights of the universe; together with the summons and the obedience, which are counted to their ten brethren. These are the twelve light hours of the third day.

The fourth day is Jesus the Splendour. His twelve hours are the twelve wisdoms, which are his [light] h[o]u[rs].

⟨p.26⟩ [ ... ]: For just as four day[s ex]ist, s[o] too four nights exist.

The first night is the Land of Darkness. It has twelve black [shadows] therein, which are its dark h[o]ur[s]. The twelve shadows of the first night are the five elements of the Land of Darkness, [which] poured forth from its five sense organs; and [ ... ] which [ ... ] in its [fi]ve elements and its five [ ... ]; the five [sp]irits that dwell in its elements [ ... ]. These are the twelve shadows and spirits of the first night.

The second night is Matter, the sculptress [ ... ... ] who has sculpted [ ... ] and the entire ruling-power that is in the worl[ds of Darkness]. She has fashioned it in five sense organs, five [male] and five female, two per world; together with the fire and the lust [that] dwell in men and women, inflaming [them] inward to one another. These are the twelve spirits of this second night.

Indeed she, this Matter, the thought of death, ga[ve] the King of the realms of Darkness and his powers strength for the war and the fight, against the aeons of greatness. Yet, he was caught! They laid a snare for him through the Fi[rst M]an. [He] was ta[ken] t[o ... ] through the powers [ ... He] was brought up from the land of death and s[et ... ab]ove and below in the [whole] universe; in the five par[t]s in the heavens above, as well as the five [parts] in the earths below; together with the heat and the cold that are the father and the mother, their fire and their l[u]st.

This is the second night that is begotten from the first night. They laid a snare for her through the First M[a]n. She was brought up by the hand of the Living Spirit, and set in this composite univers[e ... ] he[r], above and below. The [upper side is the sense] organs of males; the lower side [ ... ] fe[mal]es.

⟨p.27⟩ The thir[d night is the five] worlds of flesh [ ... five] male, and five female; that were begotten from the powers [abo]ve. They have fallen upon the earth, being displayed in wh[at is d]ry and what is moist; together with the fire and the lust [that d]we[l]l in them, which drives them inward to one another.

[Mat]te[r] herself, the thought of death, is the [mother] of them all; as among them she is named 'the night'. [The worlds] of male and female fleshes, together with the fire and the l[ust], shall [ ... ] twelve hours of [ ... ... ] night. Again, [it] too, the third night that is in the worlds of fl[e]sh, was begotten from the second night; which the Living [Spir]it crucified in the uni[verse ... , ab]ove and below in heaven [ ... ... ].

The fourth night is the law of Sin, which is the dark spirit who speaks in the twelve spirits. They are the nakedne[ss]es, the twelve zodiacal signs of Matter. They are her thrones; she who is made public, as she sculpts and is displayed in the Old Man.

And, also, the hours of this fourth night that is the Old Man, are the twelve e[vi]l spirits; the [ ... ] of the Old Man, which [are ... ... ... ... di]fferentiate the day and night [ ... ... of] death.

This is the fourth night [ ... ] that have come forth of one another. One has [ ... ... ] just like the days of lig[ht ... ] they manifested one another. So als[o ... ] they did arise out of one another.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic attribution (“Once again the enlightener speaks”) functions as compilation frame rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal, schematic cosmology: Father/aeons + a fixed twelvefold hour-structure mapped to divine beings and spatial ‘four climes’.
- ¶3: Continues the same numbered-system mapping (day → cosmic being; hours → emanated twelvefold set) wholly within the cosmological system.
- ¶4: Structural, taxonomic exposition: Pillar of Glory + paired pentads + two salvific ‘calls’ (summons/obedience) to complete a twelvefold set; no citation, no exhortation.
- ¶5: Core is the day→being and hours→twelvefold ‘wisdoms’ mapping; the inserted ‘church’ anchor most naturally reads as later institutional application (even if ‘Jesus the Splendour’ itself is a cosmic entity).
- ¶6: Summary + beatitude (“Blessed is every one…”) reads as editorial closure/paraenetic tag rather than part of the systematic correspondential mapping.
- ¶7: Programmatic connective that introduces the matching fourfold/night schema; functions inside the structural exposition rather than hagiographic frame.
- ¶8: Pure cosmological correspondential schematization: night → realm; hours → shadows/spirits; twelve derived via pentads and ‘sense organs’—all internal to the system.
- ¶9: System-internal generative account: Matter as cosmic artificer; fivefold sensory structuring; male/female dualization; twelvefold totalization—no institutional address.
- ¶10: Mythic-mechanical cosmology (snare/rescue; above/below placements; pentadic divisions) presented as how the universe is structured—no later citation or church discipline.
- ¶11: Degree/descent language (“begotten from”) and cosmic engineering (upper/lower mapped to sexual ‘sense organs’) marks an older systematic layer.
- ¶12: Classic correspondential cosmology: worlds of flesh in pentads, rendered via elemental qualities (dry/moist) plus paired passions—still system-internal.
- ¶13: Internal derivation logic (‘begotten from’), and cosmic salvific mechanics (‘crucified in the universe’) without NT citation formulae—fits the teaching substrate.
- ¶14: Underlying core is a zodiacal/twelvefold cosmology (Sin-spirit → twelve spirits → zodiacal signs). The ‘sects’ gloss applies the scheme outward to the editor’s religious landscape.
- ¶15: Core is a continuation of the twelvefold ‘hours’ taxonomy within the ‘Old Man’ complex; the ‘sects’ phrase is an outward-pointing editorial application.
- ¶16: Structural recapitulation of the fourfold emanation sequence (‘come forth from one another’), consistent with the chapter’s schematic teaching voice.
- ¶17: Beatitude/paraenesis (“Blessed is he…”) functions as an editorial closing tag rather than a component of the cosmological mapping itself.

---

## Chapter 5
### Concerning Four Hunters of Light and Four of Darkness.

*This kephalaion is predominantly systematic cosmological-correspondential teaching: a fourfold “hunter / ship / sea / net” mapping for Light powers and a parallel fourfold mapping for Darkness powers. The main later layers are (a) a dialogue/frame apparatus (¶1, ¶7) and (b) beatitude-style exhortations (¶6, ¶12) plus a polemical/institutional application that identifies the Darkness-hunter with what “rules in every sect” (¶11). ¶4 largely preserves the same cosmic mapping style but includes an institutional identification (“holy church”) that appears to be a later application graft within an otherwise older correspondential unit.*

The first hunter is the First Man who was sent from the greatness. He threw himself down to the five storehouses of darkness, he caught and siezed the enmity [... ...] his net also [... ...] out over all the children of darkness [... ...]. His ship is his four sons who are swathed over his body. The sea is the land of darkness [... ...]; his net is [...] and his powers.

The second hunter is the Third Ambassador. This one, for by his light image, which he revealed to the depths below, he hunted after the entire light that is in all things; as it is established in them. His net is his light image, [...] the whole universe and took it prisoner, to this likeness [... His] ship is his light ship. [The sea] is the universe [...] which were hunted after by his net [...] his [glorious] image.

The third hunter is Jesus the Splendour, who came from the greatness, who hunts after the light and life; and he [...] it to the heights. His net is his wisdom, [the] light wisdom with which he hunts the souls, catching them in the net. The sea is the error of the universe, the law of sin [...], the souls that are drowning in it [...]. He catches them in his net. They are the souls [... th]em by his light wisdom.

The fourth hunter is the great counsel that [...] that lives in the circuit [...] ⟨p.29⟩ entire universe in it today. Yet, at the end, in the dissolution of the universe, this very counsel of life will gather itself in and sculpt its soul in the Last Statue. Its net is its Living Spirit, because with its Spirit it can hunt after the light and the life that is in all things; and build it upon its body. Its ship, in which it is established, is this light cloud whereby it itself travels in the five elements [...], the great fire that will burn all the buildings of [... ...] in its net is the light and the life. It can rescue and free it from all bonds and fetters.

The first hunter is the King of they who belong to the darkness, who hunted after the living soul with his net at the beginning of the worlds. His net is his fire and his lust that he has put upon the living soul, with which he has entangled it [...], through all his powers.

[The] second hunter is the evil counsel that lives in [...] that hunts after the light [...] and [...] up from [...] the earths to heaven. It binds them with its powers, which bring them to the heavenly worlds above. Its net is [...] whereby they are drawn up from the abyss [to the heig]hts.

The third hunter is lust [...] walks in every power of the flesh that walks [...] in the [...] the living souls [... ...] them in its bodies, which [... ...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (“Once again the apostle speaks to his disciples”) is compiling-community frame; it introduces the topic rather than expounding the cosmic system.
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural cosmology: named hypostasis (First Man), descent to the five storehouses, and tight correspondential identifications (ship/sea/net) wholly within the cosmic architecture.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmic mechanism (revelation of the Light Image; hunting the light “in all things”) with correspondential mapping (net/ship/sea) remaining cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶4: Core-like correspondential exposition (Jesus the Splendour as a cosmic redeemer hunting souls by Light-wisdom) is interrupted by an ecclesial insertion (“holy church”) that points outside the closed cosmic system, suggesting a later editorial application within the paragraph.
- ¶5: Highly systematic eschatological cosmology (dissolution of the universe; Last Statue; Living Spirit; five elements; great fire) with internal correspondential identifications (net/ship) and process-description rather than exhortation/citation.
- ¶6: Beatitude/exhortation directed to the reader’s deeds and personal escape; shifts from cosmological description to moral address (second-person implied), typical of later pastoral application.
- ¶7: Second formulaic attribution (“Once again he speaks…”) plus transitional promise to teach the counterpart list; this is editorial scaffolding around the teaching blocks.
- ¶8: Cosmic-historical placement (“at the beginning of the worlds”) and internal mechanism (fire/lust as net) keep the mapping within the Darkness-side cosmology, not contemporary polemic.
- ¶9: Impersonal cosmic mechanics (binding/drawing from abyss to heights; earths/heavens) presented as a structured “hunter/net” function; no audience address or institutional targeting.
- ¶10: Though fragmentary, the register remains within the cosmic-anthropological system (“powers of the flesh,” “living souls”) as a functional description of a Darkness-agent.
- ¶11: Editorial application/polemical identification: “rules in every sect,” “souls of people,” and the focus on “erroneous teaching” as contemporary institutional error shifts the mapping from cosmos→cosmos to cosmos→present religious landscape (diagnostic of later pastoral graft).
- ¶12: Closing beatitude aimed at the reader’s recognition/knowledge for salvation; hortatory conclusion rather than structural cosmological exposition.

---

## Chapter 6
### Concerning the Five Storehouses that have poured forth from the Land of Darkness since the [Beginning]; the Five Rulers, the Five Spirits, the Five Bodies, the Five Tastes.

*Core material dominates: a highly schematic pentadic cosmology of the Land of Darkness (storehouses → elements → trees → genera; and five worlds with kings/spirits/bodies/tastes), plus a detailed physiognomy and faculties of the King of Darkness. A strong editorial/application drift appears in the second half: several paragraphs keep the old correspondential mapping (face/metal/taste) but pivot to identifying the ‘spirit’ in contemporary institutions (principalities, leaders, temples, sects, soothsayers) and to direct exhortation.*

Five storehouses have arisen since the beginning in the land of darkness! The five elements poured out of them. Also, from the five elements were fashioned the five trees! Again, from the five trees were fashioned the five genera of creatures in each world, male and female. And the five worlds themselves have five kings therein, and five spirits, five bodies, five tastes; in each world, they not resembling one another!

The King of the world of Smoke [ ... ] who came up from the depth of darkness; this is he who is the head of all wickedness, and all malignity. The beginning of the spread of the war occured through him; all the battles, fights, quarrelling, dangers, destructions, fights, wrestling-contests! That is the one who first made arise danger and war, with his worlds and his powers. Afterwards, also, he waged war with the light. He pitched a battle with the exalted kingdom.

⟨p.31⟩
Now, regarding the King of Darkness, there are five shapes on him! His head is lion-faced; his hands and feet are demon- and devil-faced; his shoulders are eagle-faced; while his belly is dragon-faced; his tail is fish-faced. These five shapes, the marks of his five worlds, exist on the King of the realms of Darkness!

Now, there are five other properties in him. The first is his darkness. The second is his putridity. The third is his ugliness. The fourth is his bitterness, his own soul. The fifth is his burning, which burns like an iron () as if poured out from fire.

[There are] also three others in him! The first, that his body is hard and very tough, even as she has formed him [ ... ] cruel-hearted; namely Matter, who is the thought of death, [the o]ne who sculpted him from the nature of the land of darkness. This is the manner of the body of the ruler of Smoke. He is harder than every iron, copper and steel and lead; as there is no cleaver at all, nor any iron implement, can [ ... ] him and cut him. For Matter, his fashioner, has formed him [ ... ] strong and hard.

The second, that he wounds and kills by the word of his magic arts. His recitation and hearing, all his foolish instruction, make magic and invocations for him. When it pleases him, he can make an invocation over himself, and by his magic arts be hidden from his companions. Again, when it pleases him, he can be manifested over his powers and appear to them;

⟨p.32⟩
The third, that the King of the realms of Darkness knows the converse and language of his five worlds. He understands every thing he hears from their mouths, as they address one another; each one of them in his language. [Every] design they will consider against him, every snare they debate with one another to bring upon him, he knows them! He can also understand the gesturing they signal between one another. Yet, his powers and his rulers, who are subject to him, can not understand his wordy converse. While all these things are unveiled to him; still, their heart is not manifested to him. He knows not their mind nor their thought; he can not ponder their beginning and their end. Rather, he only knows and apprehends what is before his eyes.

Also, another different thing is found in the King of the realms of Darkness! For when it pleases him to move, he spreads all his limbs out and walks. When it comes to mind, he with[d]raws his limbs and takes them in, and is rolled (?) to his companions; and he falls to the ground like a grape and a great iron ball! He terrifies by his cry, he is frightful. He frightens his powers with his sound; because when he speaks, being like thunder in the clouds, he resembles the [ ... ] of the rocks [ ... ] When he cries out and [ ... ] and calls [ ... ] over his powers, they shall tremble and to[tter a]nd fall under foot; even as some birds would be [ ... ] the bird [ ... / ... ] and they fall down to the earth. Still, this thing only: he knows not what is far from him, he sees not who is at a distance, nor does he hear him. Rather, whoever is before his face he sees, hearing him and knowing him.

These signs and these evil marks are found in the chief of the demons and fiends, the King of all the mountains of Darkness [ ... the one] to [whom] the land of darkness has given birth, begotten in its cruelty, in its wickedness and its wrath [ ... ]

⟨p.33⟩
... more than all his fellow rulers, who are in all his worlds.

Gold is the body of the King of the realms of Darkness. The body of all the powers who belong to the world of Smoke is gold. And also, the taste of its fruits is salty.

In contrast, the King of the worlds of Fire is lion-faced, the foremost of all the beasts. Copper is his body. Again, the body of all the rulers who belong to Fire is copper. Their taste is the sour taste that is in every form.

Again, the King of the worlds of Wind is eagle-face. His body is iron. Also, the body of all they who belong to the Wind is iron. Their taste is the sharp taste that is in every form.

For his part, the King of the world of Water is fish-face. His body is silver. All the other rulers who belong to Water, silver is their body. Also, the taste of their fruit is the sweetness of water, the sweet taste that is in every (form).

Again, [the Kin]g of the world of Darkness is a dragon. His [body is lead] and tin. All the other rulers [who belong to the world of] Darkness, their body is lead and tin. And also, the taste of their fruits is bitterness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame attribution (‘Once again… speaks to his disciples’) is hagiographic container language added by the compilers.
- ¶2: Impersonal, schematic pentadic cosmogony (storehouses→elements→trees→genera; five-fold rulers/spirits/bodies/tastes) with cosmos→cosmos mappings and no audience address/citation.
- ¶3: Mythic-cosmological narration inside the system (world of Smoke; war with Light/kingdom), without contemporary institutional pointing.
- ¶4: Classic substrate physiognomic correspondences (five worlds marked on the King’s body) remain entirely within cosmic description.
- ¶5: Numbered five-fold property list; impersonal descriptive voice; no exhortation or institutional targets.
- ¶6: Degree/faculty enumeration continues (‘three others’); cosmic artisan ‘Matter’ and metal-comparison remain cosmological (cosmos→cosmos), despite possible redactional tension noted in scholarship.
- ¶7: Opens as substrate: enumerated ‘second’ property describing the King’s operative faculty within the dark cosmos. Mid-paragraph it turns to ‘nowadays… in this world’ and direct commands—editorial application voice.
- ¶8: Third faculty continues in impersonal system-description (knowledge/limits of perception) entirely inside dark-world hierarchy.
- ¶9: Physiology/sensation mechanics (limb-spreading, rolling, thunder-voice, perceptual limits) remain descriptive and cosmological, not hortatory.
- ¶10: Summary/identification within the mythic system (‘King… mountains of Darkness’; born from land of darkness). No institutional pointing.
- ¶11: Fragmentary continuation of the preceding cosmic comparison (‘fellow rulers… worlds’); still within the descriptive layer.
- ¶12: Starts with classic correspondential register (king/powers ↔ metal; fruits ↔ taste). Then pivots to present-world identification (‘reigns today’), signaling later editorial application.
- ¶13: Old pentadic mapping persists (face/metal/taste). The appended ‘spirit… leaders… sects’ is an editorial application using the mapping to point outward.
- ¶14: Clean substrate mapping (realm/king ↔ face; body ↔ iron; taste ↔ sharp). The ‘temple/idols’ clause breaks the cosmos-contained correspondences.
- ¶15: Substrate correspondences (fish-face/silver/sweetness) followed by an application clause aimed at contemporary ‘sects’ and ‘baptism’; no NT citation formula, so this is application/pastoral rather than NT overlay.
- ¶16: Begins as a pure correspondential description (dragon/lead-tin/bitter taste). The ‘till today… soothsayers’ pivot marks later editorial application to recognizable social roles.
- ¶17: Direct address to ‘my brethren… the holy elect’ with imperatives (‘Hold… stay away… Put behind…’) is institutional/pastoral exhortation, not substrate exposition.

---

## Chapter 7
### The Seventh, concerning the Five Fathers

*The opening half is a tightly ordered cosmological emanation/evocation schema (Father of Greatness → three emanations; Third Ambassador → three powers; Jesus the Splendour → three powers), in an impersonal expository voice typical of the older substrate. The second half shows strong editorial fatigue: the schema is redirected toward institutional identifications (apostles/churches/elect/catechumens) and includes a Johannine-style saying (Jn 8 vector), plus a closing beatitude/exhortation.*

Five fathers exist; they were summoned forth one of one. [Also], one did come out of another!

The first Father is the Father of Greatness, the blessed one of glory, the [on]e who has no measure to his greatness; who also is the first only-begotten, the first eternal; who exists with fiv[e fa]thers for ever; the one who exists before every thing that has existed, and that will exist.

Now he, the glorious Father, summoned from him three emanations. The [fi]rst is the Gre[at] Spirit, the first Mother, who came out of the Father. She appeared first. ⟨p.35⟩

The seco[nd i]s the Belo[ved of] the Lights, the great glorious Beloved, [the one w]ho is honour[ed; w]ho came out of the Father. [He ma]nifested ou[t of h]im.

The third father is the Thir[d Ambassador], the eldest of [a]]] the counsell[ors; who came out of] the first Father. He appeared.

This is the first Father, the first power, the one from whom the three great powers came out. This is the first Father, the first eternal, the root of all the lights; from whom the three emanations came out. They have humiliated the Darkness. They have brought its heart's desire to naught. They have given themselves the victory. They have also given the victory to their aeons.

The second father, who came out of the first Father, is the Third Ambassador, the model of the King of lights. And again, he too summoned and sent out of him three powers.

One is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man; the one who bears up under all things; the great Pillar of blessing; the great porter, who is greater than all the porters.

The second is glorious Jesus the Splendour.

The third is the Virgin of Light, the glorious wisd[o]m; the one who takes away the heart of the [rul]ers and the powers by her image, as she fulfils the pleas[u]re of the greatness.

The third father, who came out of the second father: he is glorious Jesus the Splendour. And, again, he too summoned three evocations after the pattern of the second father.

The first power whom he summoned is the Light Min[d].

The second power whom Jesus summo[n]ed is the gre[at Jud]ge, who gives judgement on all the souls of mankind, [his] dwelling being established in the atmosphere under [ ... / ... ] wheel [ ... ] stars.

The third power is [the Y]outh, the gre[at ... ] light in his two persons, in [ ... ], I am speaking about that which has been established in the summons [and] the obedience.

The fourth father is the Light Mind. And, again, he too summoned three powers after the pattern of Jesus.

And also the fifth father is this Light Form; the one who shall appear to everyone who will go out from his body; and the thr[ee] great glorious angels who are come with her.

These are the five fathers who have come out of one another. They have appeared and man[ifes]ted through one another!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic container: ‘Once again… apostle… sitting… disciples… revelation’. This is the editorial dialogue frame, not the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Programmatic numbered schema introducing an emanational chain; impersonal and system-internal (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶3: Cosmological definitional style (attributes of the primal deity; eternity/measurelessness) without citation or institutional anchoring.
- ¶4: Systematic emanation structure (‘three emanations’ with first/second/third ordering); purely internal cosmology.
- ¶5: Continues the ordered emanation list. Despite the pipeline ‘application’ hit, the mapping stays within the divine pleroma.
- ¶6: Cosmological hierarchy title (‘Third Ambassador’) in the same emanational sequence; no institutional application.
- ¶7: Summative cosmological proclamation; ‘Darkness’ is personified as a cosmic opponent (system-internal combat, not moral exhortation).
- ¶8: Recursive patterning (‘again… three powers’) is characteristic of the older systematic substrate.
- ¶9: Cosmic being defined by functional/correspondential epithets (‘pillar/porter/bears up’), not institutional offices.
- ¶10: ‘Jesus the Splendour’ functions here as a named cosmological entity (core), but the appended ‘life eternal’ phrasing is Christianizing/soteriological overlay diction.
- ¶11: Cosmos→cosmos action on ‘rulers and powers’ as cosmic ranks; correspondential language (‘by her image’) remains system-internal.
- ¶12: Ordered genealogical schema (‘third father… came out of…’ + patterned triad). Jesus is used as a cosmological node, not cited from Gospel narrative.
- ¶13: Core list-item (‘Light Mind’) is embedded in an editorial application to apostolic/church structures (‘holy church’), indicating later institutional grafting.
- ¶14: Cosmological-judicial function with explicit cosmic location markers (atmosphere/wheel/stars); no institutional address.
- ¶15: Begins as cosmological identification (‘the Youth… light in his two persons’) but pivots into Gospel-like quotation and exhortation, characteristic of NT overlay.
- ¶16: Underlying emanational schema is core, but ‘chooses all the churches’ is an institutionalization of the cosmic rank (editorial application within the same sentence).
- ¶17: Institutional office-language (‘Apostle of Light’, ‘assume the church of the flesh’) and community leadership terms indicate later church-layer material rather than impersonal cosmology.
- ¶18: Biographical/institutional support figure (‘counterpart… to the apostle… companion… dangers’) reads as ecclesial/hagiographic function, not systematic cosmic mapping.
- ¶19: Seam-flagged editorial extension: ‘elect and catechumens… renounce the world’ is explicit institutional categorization and exhortation; per rules, entire paragraph is late.
- ¶20: Death-escort/epiphany motif with ‘Light Form’ and attendant angels can belong to the cosmological system, but the explicit ‘pattern… to the apostle’ is an editorial anchoring to community authority.
- ¶21: Seam-flagged: while angelic regalia is cosmological, the closing identification ‘appear… to the elect and the catechumens’ is institutional targeting; treated as an editorial graft (whole paragraph late).
- ¶22: Formal closure of the emanational schema; internal recap without exhortation or citation.
- ¶23: Beatitude/exhortation promising ‘life eternal’ and addressing ethical categories (‘doers of good things’) is paraenetic and soteriological rather than structural cosmology; likely a closing pastoral addition.

---

## Chapter 8
### Concerning the Fourteen Vehicles that Jesus has boarded.

*The chapter’s core is a systematic cosmological listing of ten “vehicles/carriages” (¶2–8) in which Jesus the Son of Greatness journeys through the cosmic orders. A clear editorial seam then extends the pattern to the institutional church (¶9). The closing paragraph (¶10) begins as a structural arithmetic summary (likely closing the older list) but then shifts into later soteriological/praise material (beatitude-style blessing and polemical antithesis). Strong editorial fatigue signal aligns with the late ecclesial extension and closing exhortative tone.*

When Jesus the Son of Greatness came to these worlds, at the time that he unveiled the greatness, he boarded ten vehicles. He journeyed in the universe by them.

The first vehicle is the light ship, since he received instructions from the Ambassador there.

⟨p.37⟩ The second carriage is the ship of the First Man, since his dwelling is established there.

The third is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man, since he shone forth there. He came below, he appeared in the world.

The fourth is the summons that he clothed upon his body. The fifth is the obedience that he swathed upon the summons.

The sixth is the living air, and since he assumed it too, he received anointment there. The seventh is the living wind that blows. The eighth is the light that illuminates. The ninth is the living water. The tenth is the living fire that is above them all.

He was journeying in these ten carriages among all the orders and all the [...], those above and those below.

These ten carriages that he boarded in the zone, and the other four that are in the flesh, they complete and make fourteen carriages.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic discourse-frame (“Once again… speaks…”) introducing a public teaching scene; hagiographic/editorial container rather than systematic exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological mission statement introducing a numbered/ordered list of vehicles; no NT citation formula. “Jesus the Son of Greatness” functions as a Manichaean cosmic being-title within the system.
- ¶3: Degree/list structure (“first…”) mapping Jesus’ descent-movement to a cosmic station (“light ship”) and a named cosmic figure (“Ambassador”).
- ¶4: Continues systematic enumeration; internal cosmological geography and a principal Iranian-Manichaean entity (“First Man”).
- ¶5: Still within the cosmological itinerary: station-name + functional explanation. The voice remains expository, not hortatory or institutional.
- ¶6: Abstract cosmic process-elements (“summons/obedience”) treated as assumable ‘garments’ in a fixed sequence—typical core mechanism language.
- ¶7: Fivefold elemental run (air/wind/light/water/fire) embedded in the numbered vehicle scheme; cosmos→cosmos mapping with no institutional application.
- ¶8: Concluding cosmic scope statement for the tenfold sequence (“orders… above… below”); still internal to the system and closes the list-unit.
- ¶9: Editorial seam/extension: after the tenfold cosmic list is complete, the pattern is continued into institutional identifications (“holy church… brothers… sisters… catechumens”). By rule, the entire paragraph is a late ecclesial graft, not MIXED.
- ¶10: Opens with a structural arithmetic closure of the list (ten + four = fourteen), matching the core’s enumerative logic; then pivots into evaluative/praise language (“Blessed is he…”, antithesis of ‘his’ vs ‘strangers’) characteristic of later editorial conclusion rather than the impersonal mapping style.

---

## Chapter 9
### The Explanation of the Peace, what it is; the Right Hand; the Kiss; the Salutation.

*The chapter opens with a clear dialogue-question frame, then presents a largely self-contained cosmological archetype: five “signs/mysteries” enacted in the descent/ascent of the First Man (peace, right hand, kiss, salutation, laying on of hands). Mid-chapter the editor repeatedly pivots from archetype to human social ritual (“in the midst of mankind”, “here and now”, “today”), producing multiple MIXED paragraphs. The second half shows strong editorial fatigue: institutional church initiation sequence, exhortation, and clerical authority material dominate. One paragraph contains a likely biblical/psalmic quotation about sitting at the right hand/footstool, treated as OVERLAY inside an otherwise cosmological unit (handled as MIXED with the citation removed).*

So, these five signs are the mystery of the First Man. He came forth with them from the aeon of light. Also, when he finished his contest, he went up with these good signs. They received him into the aeons of light.

⟨p.38⟩ The first peace is the one that the gods and the angels in the land of light gave to the First Man; when he comes out against the enemy. The gods and the angels were walking with him; escorting him, giving to him their peace and power, and their blessing and fortification. This is the first peace that the gods and the angels gave to the First Man; as he comes forth from the aeon of light.

The first right hand is the one that the Mother of Life gave to the First Man; when he comes out to the contest.

The first kiss is this one with which the Mother of Life embraced the First Man; as he separates from her, coming down to the contest. Even all the gods and the angels who are in the aeons of light were making to embrace him! [ ... ] all of them also, and the church tutelaries (?), and the kinsfolk who belong to the household of his people. They were making to embrace him as [he] separates from them, accompanying him out, embracing him with the kiss of affection and love.

⟨p.39⟩ The first salutation is the salutation with which the First Man made obeisance; when he comes out [to the depths] below. He was bending his knees, making obeisance to the God of truth, and all the aeons [of light] who belong to the household of his people; entreating them for a power to escort him, as he comes forth.

The first laying on of hands is the one that the Mother of Life bequeathed upon the head of the First Man. She armed him and made him mighty. She laid hands on him, and sent him to the battle. He descended. He completed his struggle with the great powers and the enmity.

By the [se] five signs and these five myst[e]ri[es] the First Man came forth from the aeons of light against the enemy. He humbled him. He was victorious against him by them.

Also, when he ascended from the war, he came in to the kingdom of the household of his people by these five mysteries!

The peace with which [he ascen]ded is the peace that the summons gave to him, [w]hen it was sent forth from the [Fa]ther of Life. It gave him the peace in the world of darkness. Also, that peace with which he descended is th[e sam]e as the one with which he ascended from the struggle!

Again, this second right hand is the one that the Living Spirit gave the First Man, when he [brou]ght him upward out from the struggle.

Also, this second kiss is the one with which the [Father o]f Life and the Mother of the living embraced the First Man, when he ascended from the struggle.

⟨p.40⟩ [The] second salutation with which the First Man made obeisance [ ... this] time to the God of truth and [ ... ] the blessed aeons and al[l] the aeons [ ... the] whole [land] of light, whe[n] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living brought the Man upward from the abyss of the struggle.

The second laying on of hands is this: When the Living Spirit brought the First Man upward from the war, he saved him from all the surging waves. He brought him upward, he gave him ease in the great aeons of light, which belong to the household of his people. He set [him] firmly before the Father, the Lord of the totality. He received this great laying on of hands that he may become leader of his brethren in the new aeon.

These five mysteries and these five signs came about first in the divine. Mankind has been taught them; and they have instituted them in their midst [ ... but] these mysteries were not at the beginning amongst the powers of darkness.

Once again: At the time of their coming forth, the Light Form shall come forth before them; and she redeems them from the darkness to the light [ ... ] This Light Form calms the person, with the kiss and her quiet, from fear of the demons w[ho de]stroy his body. By her asp[ec]t and her image [the] heart of the elect one, who is come forth from his bo[d]y, shall be calm for [him]. Afterwards, the angel who h[o]lds the victory prize extends to him the right hand. And it draws him out of the abyss of his body, and accepts him in with the [k]iss and love. That soul shall make obeisance to its red[eem]er, who is this Light Form. And also, at the inst[ant when ... ] he shall be perfected and increased according to [ ... / ... ] in the household of the living ones, with the go[ds] and the angel[s]. [An]d he receives the crown [ ... ] glory in the life for ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic Q&A frame: disciples question the apostle; sets up topic in ritual terms. This is editorial apparatus, not substrate exposition.
- ¶2: Core kernel identifies the five signs as primordial mysteries of the First Man’s descent/ascent (cosmic-structural). Surrounding sentences are personal disclosure + ‘displayed in the world’ application, indicating later editorial mediation.
- ¶3: Impersonal cosmological narration establishing an archetype within the Light realm (gods/angels/aeon of light) without institutional targeting.
- ¶4: Compact archetypal mapping: divine being (Mother of Life) → action/sign given to First Man, entirely within cosmological system.
- ¶5: Cosmic household scene; despite the translator’s uncertain gloss “church tutelaries (?)”, the passage functions as intra-plērōmatic kinship/escort imagery, not church-institution instruction.
- ¶6: Archetypal obeisance described within the Light household; no ‘today/in the church’ pivot.
- ¶7: Mythic mechanism (arming/sending/struggle) presented as cosmic process; not a rule for the community.
- ¶8: Summative statement closing the first archetypal cycle; entirely cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶9: Completion of the descent/ascent arc; no later institutional anchoring.
- ¶10: Begins with cosmological causation (Summons from Father of Life; peace in world of darkness), then turns to ‘here and now’ etiology of human practice—classic archetype→social application seam.
- ¶11: Clean archetypal identification (Living Spirit → right hand) followed by explicit mankind-application in the full paragraph.
- ¶12: Cosmic embrace is core; the paragraph’s second half is an editor using it to explain ordinary human leave-taking/meeting customs.
- ¶13: Archetypal obeisance upon restoration to the Light is core; ‘today… in the midst of mankind’ is an editorial anchor to contemporary ritual.
- ¶14: Core cosmology (Living Spirit rescues/sets First Man before the Father) is interrupted by a recognizable ‘right hand/footstool’ scriptural voice, indicating later Judeo-Christian overlay insertion.
- ¶15: The cosmic priority claim (‘first in the divine’ / ‘not at the beginning among Darkness’) fits core origin discourse, while apostolic proclamation is a later legitimating frame.
- ¶16: Archetype is redirected to institutional election: “choose his church.” This is cosmos→church/institution mapping, characteristic of later pastoral extension.
- ¶17: Stepwise church initiation sequence (peace → child of peace → elected → right hand → approach church). Institutional catechesis rather than cosmological substrate.
- ¶18: Explicit ‘holy church’ and moral-programmatic elements (“good works”); not cosmic-system exposition.
- ¶19: Community-building promises tied to receiving ritual signs; although ‘Light Mind’ appears, the paragraph functions as institutional formation/assurance, not primordial cosmology.
- ¶20: Predominantly cosmological eschatology (Light Form, demons, angel with prize, ascent to household of living ones). A small late intrusion uses ‘apostles/chosen’ language, suggesting secondary church colouring.
- ¶21: Direct exhortation and audience address (“my brethren and my limbs… let you…”), plus self-authorization (“Father who sent me”): application voice typical of late homiletic/pastoral layer.
- ¶22: Fragmentary but oriented to hearers’ benefit (“you may part from the world… you have understood…”). Not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶23: Explicit clerical hierarchy (teachers/deacons/presbyters), obedience, and sin for rejecting laying on of hands—institutional enforcement layer.
- ¶24: Second-person exhortation (“my beloved ones, hasten… listen…”) and moral providential assurances; late application material, heavily lacunose.

---

## Chapter 10
### Concerning the Interpretation of the Fourteen [Great Aeons] of Light, about which Sethel has spoken in [his Prayer]

*Core teaching dominates from ¶3–¶5: a systematic fourteenfold cosmological enumeration (5+2+5+2) and its function in sustaining/establishing the universe. ¶1–¶2 are compilation frame (disciples’ question + dialogue attribution). ¶5 is more expansive and lacunose, but it remains within the cosmic system (Ambassador’s kingship among aeons) rather than pivoting to contemporary institutions or exhortation.*

⟨p.43⟩ Happen you know this, that the [ ... ] below abide in fourteen [great aeons ...]: the five elements, the sons of the First Man, together with the Call and the Hearing. They anoint them, living in them. These shall make seven, five plus two.

[ ... the] f[ive s]ons of the Living Spirit who [support] all [the wei]ght of the universe; together with the [Li]vi[ng] Spirit [and the Fir]st Man, their fathers. These are the fourt[een p]raises that he has proclaimed, the praise that is made of fourteen pra[yers], by which the universe is established.

[There are fourt]een aeons of light [ ... ] of Seth [e] ... ] about them in his prayer, for they are expl[ai]ned [ ... the la]nd that is not revealed. This land [ ... ... is estab]lished. They bore up under the Enemy [ ... ... equa]ls them. The hidden plenitude that [ ... ] the Ambassador who came from the Father, the [ ... ] He came and stood firm in the midst of these [ ... ]: he is called the good father of [all] the ae[ons and gods] who are holy and conjoined. He has become leader and a great king after the likeness of the first Father, the lord of all these cou[nse]ls. This universe is held together by their will [ ... ] to the end of all things.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame: disciples questioning, honorific address (“our master”), request formula, and quotation setup for Sethel’s prayer.
- ¶2: Dialogue attribution (“Then the enlightener speaks…”) and a programmatic promise to interpret; no actual cosmological exposition yet.
- ¶3: Systematic enumerative teaching in an impersonal explanatory voice; cosmic beings/degrees counted and summed (5+2=7) with no appeal to institutions or audience exhortation.
- ¶4: Completes the fourteenfold structure by adding another 5+2; remains cosmos→cosmos (sons, fathers, universe-support mechanics). The ‘fourteen prayers/praises’ language reads as interpretive but still internal to cosmological architecture.
- ¶5: Cosmological-redemptive exposition: the aeons as underpinning the (hidden/visible) universe and the Ambassador’s enthronement among them. Despite mention of Seth’s prayer, the paragraph does not pivot to community rules, polemic about contemporary cults, or direct address; it stays within the Manichaean cosmic hierarchy (Father/Ambassador/aeons). Lacunae make fine-grained stratification uncertain.

---

## Chapter 11
### [Concerning the Interpretation of] all [the] Fathers of [Light], who are distinguished from one another.

*This chapter is a catechetical-style catalogue of principal Light divinities. Most paragraphs are written in a repetitive “The beginning of X is Y” application register that maps cosmic beings onto worldly virtues/roles (grace, prayer, hunters, builders, etc.), suggesting a later didactic recasting of an older list. The recoverable core is the underlying enumeration of named Fathers of Light and a few brief cosmological functions (notably the Living Spirit and his five sons; the Light Mind as awakener/gatherer). The opening speech-attribution is frame, and the closing beatitudes are exhortational/pastoral.*

the great Father of the [ligh]ts

the Mother of [L]ife, the first Holy Spirit.

the T[h/i]rd Am[bassad]or, the king of the glorious realm that lies in this [wo]rld, [ ... ] of the King of the lights.

the Beloved of the Lights, who is honour[ed.

the First [Man.

⟨p.44⟩
[the] Living [Spirit], the Father of Life, who has distribu[ted about] his five sons from place to place.

the Great Builder, who is glorious.

the Pillar of Glory ... [ ... ]

[is Jesus the Splendour]; or the one who delivers, as he frees whoever belongs [ ... ] from his words.

the Virgin of Light, [.

the Light Mind; who is the awakene[r of they] who sleep, the gatherer in of the ones who are sc[att]ered.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula + direct address mark late compilation voice; only the divine title itself plausibly preserves older catalog material.
- ¶2: Underlying divine name is cosmological; the ‘beginning of prayer/blessing’ rubric reads like later instructional recasting. ‘Holy Spirit’ here functions as a Manichaean divine title rather than an explicit NT citation.
- ¶3: The remaining material is a straight cosmological identification (named being + realm/kingly function), consistent with an older catalogue; the ‘good counsel’ mapping is didactic.
- ¶4: Underlying divine title likely comes from an older list; the paragraph’s rubric (‘beginning of honour’) is application-style rather than mechanistic cosmology.
- ¶5: “First Man” is a stable Persian-Manichaean cosmological name; ‘hunters/trappers’ reads like later pedagogical analogy.
- ¶6: The five-sons distribution is characteristic core system-language (structural, cosmological, impersonal). The ‘fighters’ linkage is a didactic overlay on that content.
- ¶7: Cosmic title preserved, but the occupational mapping (‘architects/builders’) suggests later instructional framing rather than the oldest internal cosmic mapping.
- ¶8: “Pillar of Glory” is a core cosmological entity; the ‘porters of greatness’ rubric reads like a pedagogical metaphor applied to a known figure.
- ¶9: “Jesus the Splendour” here functions as a named Manichaean cosmic deliverer (core-compatible) rather than a Gospel citation; the ‘deliverers’ category is an application-style label.
- ¶10: Underlying divine name is part of the standard Light hierarchy; the ‘wisdom of truth’ rubric reads like later catechesis.
- ¶11: The functional pair ‘awakener’/‘gatherer-in’ is close to core expository language describing cosmic operations; the ‘honoured ones’ category is a didactic framing.
- ¶12: Beatitude/exhortation addressed to the hearer (“Blessed is he… lodge… fasten… in his heart”)—application voice rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶13: Closing beatitude promising inheritance of eternal life; exhortational conclusion to the catalogue rather than core system-description.

---

## Chapter 12
### Concerning the Interpretation of the Five Words that are proclaimed in the Universe

*The chapter is extremely fragmentary. Paragraph 1 is a clear hagiographic Q&A frame anchored in contemporary sectarian labels (Baptists, ‘Purified Ones’), while ¶2–¶3 preserve brief internal identifications of the “words” with Manichaean cosmological divinities (Father; Third Ambassador). The identifications themselves stay cosmos→cosmos, but the overall setup likely reflects a later syncretic/interpretive agenda (Mandaean/Elchasaite parallels flagged in the apparatus).*

[...] call it by this name is the Father, the l[ife ...] this unnameable, whom no one [...] his name.

⟨p.45⟩
The second [word is the Thir]d Ambassador [...] of life, whom they also call the father of [...] [...] the first Father.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue apparatus (“Once again… the disciples… questioned the apostle… Recount to us, our master”) plus explicit anchoring to contemporary groups (“sect of the Baptists… ‘Purified Ones’”). This is editorial framing, not substrate exposition.
- ¶2: Despite being a continuation of the framed question, the surviving clause itself is an internal cosmological identification (a “word/name” equated with the Father/Life/unnameable). No institutional exhortation or NT citation; reads like a bare teaching equivalence within the Manichaean divine hierarchy.
- ¶3: Cosmos→cosmos equation of a “word” with a named Manichaean entity (Third Ambassador) and related divine epithets. Fragmentary, but the register is classificatory/identificatory rather than pastoral or hagiographic.

---

## Chapter 13
### Concerning the Five Saviours, the Resurrectors of Those Who Are Dead, Together with the Five Resurrections

*This kephalaion is structurally pentadic (enumerating resurrectors) with a clear hagiographic opening frame and a likely late concluding beatitude. The central numbered sequence (¶3–¶6) is impersonal, cosmological, and process-oriented, consistent with the older teaching substrate, though heavily lacunose.*

The second resurrector is [...].

And the third resurrector is the Father, the First [...], because by his word shall all lives [...] gather in and go to him, and they receive [...] and rest.

The fourth resurrector is the Light Mind, because [...]. ⟨p.46⟩

The fifth resurrector to the good [...] is the Father, the First established one [...]. [He ...] all the powers, and he stands firm [...]. He shall unveil the [...] over them [...], and he draws them to him [...], his will [...], and he [...] them, and he stands them up [...] there, and they remain until [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue/frame opener (“Once again the enlightener speaks”), characteristic of the compiled Kephalaia hagiographic apparatus.
- ¶2: Too fragmentary to date internally, but it appears to continue the broken opening frame line begun in ¶1 rather than the pentadic teaching proper.
- ¶3: Bare enumerative item within a pentadic teaching list; no address, no citation, no institutional application—fits the impersonal schematic substrate despite the lacuna.
- ¶4: Cosmic mechanism stated as process (“by his word… gather… receive… rest”), remaining within the cosmological system (no contemporary institutions or exhortation).
- ¶5: Another pentadic list item naming a cosmological entity (Light Mind) in the same schematic style; manuscript page marker preserved.
- ¶6: Extended, impersonal description of a resurrection/ingathering operation (“unveil… draws… stands them up”), entirely cosmos→cosmos with no polemical ‘today/nowadays’ anchoring or church application.
- ¶7: Concluding beatitude/exhortation (“Blessed is every one who…”) shifts from descriptive cosmology to reader-facing valuation. Not clearly a Gospel citation, but it functions as a late moralizing/tag ending rather than the schematic core list.

---

## Chapter 14
### The Interpretation [of] the S[i]lence, the Fast, [the Peace], the Day, [and] the Rest; [what] they are.

*This chapter is largely an interpretive apparatus mapping named church “lessons” (silence/fast/peace/day/rest) onto cosmological archetypes. That mapping move (ritual term → cosmic entity) is later than the oldest substrate, but several paragraphs preserve clear cosmological fragments (First Man, ship of living waters, great aeon, Pillar of Glory). Frame material appears at the opening (disciples’ request) and in a brief doxological close.*

[...] the gatherer in [... ...] [...] is the Pill[a]r of Gl[or]y [...] because [...] ⟨p.47⟩ [...] up to the [...]

the [Fi]rst [M]an who dwel[ls i]n the ship of living waters [...] li]ght [...] he is c[alled ...] that they have [...] glorious [...]

[...] uni]verse as they shall [...] in the great aeon [...] are spread in the universe, it is [...] established in the [...] and the so[u]ls [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue frame with disciples questioning the enlightener; request/imploring formula and topic-setting list of ritual “lessons” identifies editorial/hagiographic apparatus rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Core signal: the Pillar of Glory (a fixed Manichaean cosmic transport/column of Light). Mixed because the surviving middle clause appears to apply the notion to bodily lusts/discipline (institutional-ritual interpretation of “fast”), even though it is preserved only in fragments.
- ¶3: Core cosmological exposition survives cleanly once the introductory identification formula is stripped: First Man + ship of living waters is internal to the cosmic system. The initial ‘they call …’ indicates later interpretive mapping from ritual term to archetype.
- ¶4: Core markers: “great aeon,” universe-spread/establishment language, and souls in a cosmic setting. Mixed because the surviving lead-in explicitly frames the description as an interpretation of what is called “day,” i.e., ritual term → cosmological referent.
- ¶5: Beatitude/doxological closure (“Blessed is he… remains for ever and ever”) is characteristic of editorial/hymnic wrapping rather than systematic cosmological mapping.

---

## Chapter 15
### [Concerning the ... ...] Five [Parts ...] Worlds of [...]

*Dominant old teaching layer: a systematic pentadic/decadic cosmology of Death’s five elements and five worlds, doubling into male/female to yield twelve parts, then correlated with zodiacal signs. Late hagiographic frame appears in ¶1 and a doxological close in ¶10. The main editorial graft is ¶8–¶9, where the cosmic scheme is explicitly applied to the “holy church” and contemporary “sects,” i.e., cosmos → present institution/polemic.*

Did Death arise and stand firm [...] up? Or else did it turn itself from [...] its armour [...] in its [...] but it finished [...] the two [...].

⟨p.48⟩ [...] it moved it and [...] they that are in the [...] of the earth of Darkness [...] first part.

Yet, after a long time, as there was not [...], it gushed and streamed up [...] the five elements of Death that gushed [...] which are smoke, fire, wind, water and Darkness [...] the second time [...] they were sculpted [...] those in which Death was established [...].

The fourth part is the evil fruits [...] in the five trees that are in the five worlds of the land of Darkness.

The fifth time it spread itself out to its creatures that exist in the five worlds; the ones that were formed and generated from the five fruits of Death. They are the five worlds of flesh, the five creations of Darkness in which Death has been established. It spread itself out to ten parts, five male parts and five female [...].

Now, like the land of Darkness, in that its spirit and its [...] they nourished the five elements [...]; this is also [how] the ten parts that are in male and female, the Fire and Lust of the flesh [...] them, as they yoke them with one another [...] they were established against the First Man. This is [...] of the Darkness, it was condemned for ever.

Alongside the mystery of these twelve parts that came about in the Darkness [...] against the First Man. The twelve [...] the twelve spirits of error that came about from the twelve signs of the zodiac [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Clear frame markers (“Once again…”, disciples in attendance) introduce a substantive cosmological question about Death’s arising within the two essences; the question itself likely preserves the older inquiry-topic.
- ¶2: Extremely lacunose, but what remains stays inside the cosmic geography (“earth of Darkness,” “first part”) with no institutional or citation language; tentatively treated as core continuation.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmological enumeration (five elements named) in an impersonal explanatory voice; this is classic substrate-style degree teaching.
- ¶4: Pure correspondential mapping internal to the dark cosmos (fruits/trees/worlds) with pentadic structure; no contemporary referents.
- ¶5: Pentad → decad (male/female doubling) is structural cosmology, not exhortation; remains wholly within the system of Darkness/Death.
- ¶6: Mechanistic explanation of how the doubled parts plus Fire/Lust operate against the First Man—cosmos → cosmos causality, no institutional pivot.
- ¶7: Twelvefold correlation (parts/spirits/zodiac) is a closed correspondential system. “Spirits of error” could be later polemical colouring, but here it still functions as an internal cosmic taxonomy tied to the zodiac.
- ¶8: Explicit cosmos→institution graft: the mythic pursuit is transferred to “the second living man, who dwells in the holy church,” anchoring the scheme in the editor’s ecclesial present.
- ¶9: Continues the application into the contemporary world (“light that dwells in the church,” “the twelve sects”); this is polemical/institutional use of the scheme rather than core cosmology.
- ¶10: Doxological closure formula (“for ever and ever”) belongs to the compiling community’s liturgical/hagiographic apparatus, not the teaching substrate.

---

## Chapter 16
### [Concerning the Five] Greatnesses who [went forth] against the Darkness.

*The chapter is largely a systematic cosmological pentad teaching (five greatnesses and their series) followed by process/simile expositions of the “advents” of key Light powers (First Man, Living Spirit, Third Ambassador, summons/obedience). A thin hagiographic opening formula is present (¶1). A distinct Christian/parabolic overlay appears in the Jesus the Splendour “trees/fig-garden” section and its move into Adam/Eve and “good news” language (¶23–25). The closing beatitude/exhortation (¶30) reads as later pastoral framing, consistent with mild end-of-chapter editorial drift.*

[...] the Darkness, it was defeated by five greatnesses.

The first greatness is the Father of Greatness who exists in calm and hiddenness; as he is established in his light-earth, in his own essence.

Four greatnesses were manifested and came forth of him. They were appointed to ten parts, and established in total twelve.

The second greatness poured out and was manifested. It was revealed from the first greatness. It is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, together with the First Man and his five sons; these are the second greatness.

⟨p.50⟩
The third greatness is the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder, the Living Spirit, and the five sons of the Living Spirit. These are the third greatness.

The fourth greatness is the Third Ambassador, and the Pillar of Glory, and all the light powers who were revealed from him. These are the fourth greatness.

The fifth greatness who came from the Father is Jesus the Splendour, and all his powers whom he summoned, his emanations who were [...] They poured out and were manifested from him.

This is, so that they were spread out and [...] fives: these five [sons] of the First Man [...] the entire building; another five are the five sons of the Living Spirit who are spread out to five places, supporting [...]

[...] total in twelve parts that [... There are] another two added to them who are the summons and the obedience. They are the father and the mother whom Jesus the Splendour by his advent has appointed as successors to his place [...] the great earth and the [...] in them.

[The advent ...] of the First Man who came [...] belonging to [...] existing in a kingdom [...] esteem before they who belong to his household [...] his enemies [...] and suffering by their wickedness; and they take from him [...] and a richness; and they imprison [him in] captivity and take from him their companies (?). Yet, he [...] by his humility; and he takes heart and restrains his enemy [...] in silence.

Just as with this man, this noble son whose enemies surround him, this also is how the advent of the First Man happened, [...] rulers, the enemies of the light.

In contrast, the advent of the Living Spirit and the commandment that he received, whereby he came upon all the rulers, it differs from that of the First Man. His advent [...] the advent of the First Man.

⟨p.51⟩
The advent of the Living Spirit is like this, when he was sent from the greatness that he might bring up the First Man. He resembles a judge the king would send to overturn the violence to that man, the noble son against whom his enemies sinned. And he, this judge, [...] hard and cruel [...] and he gives the means [...] by his righteousness and his strength; and he also judges all who are condemned according to their rebellious heart, and repays each according to his deserts. They who are worthy of blows he strikes with hard leather whips. Again, these who are worthy of cutting off their limbs, or indeed their [...] and he repays them accordingly. [...] and he binds and fetters them, and hangs by [...] and thus he does to them with a righteous judgement. And he kills them to flay their skin [...] so that they will be killed by a death [...] until they die. Again, [they for] whom it is fitting [...] to banish from their dwellings he may remove according to a righteous judgement; just as they had faulted and sinned against the noble son.

Also, the associates in the mystery and the assistants of this judge, these who belong to him in great strength; in an instant and haste they shall do the will of their master.

Now, just as this genuine judge, who was sent from the king to overturn the violence to this man, the noble son: this is also the case with the Living Spirit. He also was sent from the King of the lights to overturn the vengeance and violence to the First Man, against whom the King of the realms of Darkness had sinned. He faulted and sinned against him, and also his rebellious powers. Therefore, when (the Living Spirit) came, he bore up the First Man.

Also, all the rulers, the powers of Sin who had faulted and sinned against the sons of the First Man, he has judged them according to a righteous judgement. He has bound them in heaven and earth. He put each one to the place fitting for him; he weighed each of them according to his cruelty and oppression. While some of them he enclosed in the prison, others he hung head down. Some [...] are crucified, others are sat down all the time. Some among them are fixed beneath their associates, restricted by a strong chain; others he made the holders of authority over their companions beneath, so that they may do their pleasure amongst these below them.

⟨p.52⟩
[...] that when the Father of Life established the judgements [...] He was revealed to all the powers of Darkness. He judged them as a true giver of judgement [...] This is the first judgement that the Living Spirit gave the rulers [in respect of the] First Man. Indeed, he gave victory to the First Man corresponding to his victory; but he condemned the King of the realms of Darkness corresponding to that by which he had erred.

[...] this chain, of this sort, became [...] the earth till the end time when the universe will be dissolved; and it perishes in the great fire and is separated. While the light goes to its country, the Darkness remains in the chain and fetter for ever.

The advent also of the Third Ambassador, when he came to inspect they who are imprisoned in the zone: he is like a great king who comes and inspects craftsmen [...] and [...] and [...]

Also, they who are good, of the craftsmen and architects and artisans whom he has there in each place: he shall see that they have worked well, and have fallen to his works in their fashion. He shall rejoice with great joy [...] He shall thank the artisans who have constructed his works [and completed] his orders.

Now, just as this great king who comes and inspects his works and fortifications, so also is the advent of the Third Ambassador. For, when he too came, he saw [his works] established and prepared and [...] series of the rulers prepared and established there, above and below, outside and within, in the great pattern that his brothers had made. They had come before him to the contest, they who had fixed and created the universe before he came. He saw the gods and the angels established and controlling the fortifications [...] guarding the entire ruling-power [...] which his first powers had done. They are the First Man and the Living Spirit and the Great Spirit, the Mother of Life.

Therefore, when he saw them, that they had created everything with great wisdom, [...] as they looked he plundered (?) [...] in his advent, by which he came, he was unveiled to them. He says to the Living Spirit: “The judgement that you have [...] good, what you uttered [...] you gave to them correspondingly; so that they were moved and raised up to that of which they were not worthy”.

⟨p.54⟩
Then the summons and the obedience, the great counsel that came to the elements, which are set in conjunction: it mixed with them, it was established in silence. It bears up [...] until the end time when it can arise and stand firm in the great fire. It will gather to it its own soul, and sculpt it in the Last Statue. You will also find it sweeps out and casts from it the pollution that is foreign to it. However, the life and the light that are in all things it gathers in to it, and builds upon its body.

The time when this Last Statue will be perfect in all its limbs, then it can become free and ascend from that great struggle through the Living Spirit its father, the one who comes and brings a [...] limb. He brings it up from within this gathering (?), the melting down and destruction of all things.

Now, this counsel, that summons and obedience, resembles thus cooled butter. If a person will take and set it in hot milk, it melts down and soaks in with this hot milk. It melts in another time in silence and quiet to the midst [...] yet also, of the power and the taste of this milk [...] is drawn to (the butter), and (the milk) sets and becomes cold [...] in [...] and the [...] this milk it shall clarify and discard. However, the oil and the grease and the ‘beauty’ of the milk it shall gather in to it.

⟨p.55⟩
Again, this too is what the advent of that thought is like, the counsel of life, which is the summons and obedience that came to the elements. This is the one that at the end can arise and stand firm, and will gather in to it all the lives, and the light that has remained behind in everything. It can build them upon its body; it will gather them in to it [...] in its image for ever and ever [...] from this time.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: A classic Kephalaia frame formula introduces a short cosmological thesis. The frame is later, but the defeated-by-five-greatnesses statement is a compact teaching incipit.
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural cosmology: emanational identity + divine locale/quality (calm/hiddenness/light-earth) stated without citation or exhortation.
- ¶3: Numbered emanation arithmetic (4/10/12) in expository voice; internal to the cosmological system.
- ¶4: Systematic listing of hypostases and their pentad (five sons) as a defined ‘greatness’—a core emanational register.
- ¶5: Formal cosmological identification and pentadic structure; no institutional or paraenetic pivot despite a small application-voice score.
- ¶6: Emanational hierarchy and functional titles in the standard Manichaean cosmological inventory.
- ¶7: Still cosmos→cosmos (a named Light hypostasis and its powers), but the separate elevation of Jesus the Splendour as a ‘fifth’ reads like a potentially later doctrinal development within the tradition.
- ¶8: Explicit pentadic correspondential architecture (five-sons sets, ‘spread out’ to places, supporting structures).
- ¶9: Numerical completion logic (12 + 2) and hypostatic pairs (summons/obedience as father/mother) remain internal to the cosmological system, even if the calculation feels schematic.
- ¶10: Didactic simile/narrativized cosmology describing how an ‘advent’ functions; still explanatory rather than hortatory, and not applied to contemporary institutions.
- ¶11: Explicit simile-to-cosmos mapping (parable structure used to explain cosmic events) without citation formula.
- ¶12: Comparative typology of two cosmic missions; ‘commandment received’ is functional/structural, not ecclesial rule.
- ¶13: Extended judicial simile explaining cosmic retribution mechanics; violent imagery serves system explanation rather than moral exhortation.
- ¶14: Still within the simile’s cosmic court/retinue logic (assistants executing judgement); no institutional mapping to ‘church offices’ is made.
- ¶15: Direct cosmos-to-simile mapping; purely mythic/cosmological actors (Living Spirit, First Man, King of Darkness).
- ¶16: Systematic description of cosmic penal ordering (placements, chains, inverted suspension) presented as ‘how it is arranged’.
- ¶17: Core content is correspondential/juridical (‘corresponding to’) and cosmological; the initial second-person prompt sounds like later oral-homiletic shaping.
- ¶18: Eschatological mechanics stated as process (dissolution, separation, destinies of Light/Darkness) without exhortation or citation.
- ¶19: Begins a new simile cycle for another cosmic advent; still internal to the system (zone/prisoners).
- ¶20: Continuation of the inspection simile; explanatory, not paraenetic.
- ¶21: Cosmic architecture and hierarchy (‘above and below, outside and within’) presented as a finished pattern—typical structural teaching voice.
- ¶22: Still intra-cosmic evaluation and ‘correspondingly’ logic; direct speech is between cosmic beings (not a Kephalaia apostle/disciple frame).
- ¶23: Parabolic ‘trees/axe/fire’ complex and fig-garden imagery strongly echoes Gospel-style moral horticulture language; likely a Christianized didactic overlay attached to the Jesus the Splendour advent theme.
- ¶24: ‘Tree of life’ + ‘good fruit’ reads as biblicizing hortulan symbolism (Genesis/Gospel confluence) rather than the impersonal emanational register.
- ¶25: Soteriological vocabulary (‘saviour’, ‘good news’) and the move into Adam/Eve ‘flesh’ frames the advent in biblical-human history motifs characteristic of Mani’s Christian synthesis and later reception.
- ¶26: Core eschatological mechanism: mixing into elements, silent establishment, purification, gathering Light, building a body/Statue—classic system-process exposition (minor second-person aside does not shift to institutional application).
- ¶27: Degree/process teaching (completion of limbs → ascent) framed through cosmic kinship (Living Spirit as father) and end-time mechanics.
- ¶28: Correspondential simile explaining mixture/separation and selective gathering (beauty/oil) as cosmic process—no reference to contemporary practice.
- ¶29: Returns from simile to doctrinal statement; reiterates the core mechanism (gather Light/lives → build body/image eternally).
- ¶30: Beatitude-style exhortation (‘Blessed is every one who will believe…’) and salvation promise shifts to audience confirmation, typical of later paraenetic closure rather than cosmological exposition.

## Chapter 17
### The Chapter of the Three Seasons

*This kephalaion is dominated by systematic cosmological time-structuring (three seasons: descent/ascent of the First Man; ordering of the universe; final redemptive span from the Ambassador’s Image to the ascent of the Statue). The only clear later material is the dialogue frame apparatus ("apostle", "disciples" Q&A attributions), which wraps or punctuates otherwise core teaching. No NT citation formulae or institutional/pastoral grafts appear; “Jesus” here reads as the mythic/cosmic saviour-agent within the Manichaean system rather than a Gospel overlay.*

From the time when the Light came forth and was crucified in the Darkness, to the season when the Statue will ascend and the entire Light, which was conjoined with the Darkness, will be purified and cleansed. From that time on, from that time to the season of the end totals three seasons.

Three seasons: from the time when the First Man went down to the abyss, and waged the war and the struggle against the King of the realms of Darkness and his other powers. He was victorious, while his sons remain behind with them in the worlds [... ...] in the [...] until he shall catch all of them [...] he bound them all up, so that not one of them escaped his hand. He won a great victory [... ...] The First Man spent human lifetimes and generations in the land of darkness, until he prevailed [...] ⟨p.56⟩ he fulfilled the will of the greatness that was commanded of him.

Then they sent the Living Spirit, the Father of Life. He brought the First Man up from the struggle, he brought him up to the land of light. This is the first period, from the descent of the First Man to his ascent.

The second season is from the time when the First Man ascended. The Living Spirit came, and the Mother of Life. They set in order the things of the universe, they set up [...] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living set in order [...] the universe, up to the moment when they go out [...] it was reckoned to the second season.

[...] the universe [...] the Third Ambassador spread [...] that is in the [...] living; he [...] he separated beauty from all the powers and scooped it up to the heights, he swept the waste to the abyss, and he [... ...] Now, from the moment when the Ambassador revealed his image he purified the light. Sin spurted up towards him. He hid his image. Sin came [...] from the Darkness. It sculpted the trees and the abortions fell down. Afterwards they sculpted Adam and Eve in the flesh. Until the moment when Jesus was sent and he [...] as he performed there his will. He gave the hope to Adam, and ascended to the heights.

Now, from the time when the Ambassador displayed his image until the season when the Statue will ascend at the end, shall be reckoned to the third season.

[...] ⟨p.57⟩ [...] lasts long in the time that the First Man spent in the world of Darkness. It is a very great count of time, more than the (other) two seasons!

The first season, which the First Man spent in the world of Darkness, is how much longer than the time that this universe has been? While it is established?

If you should say that [... ...] it is small, if you should [... ...] to him [...] and you consult [...] you will tell him: Twenty times! It is greater again in this other one that you will consult: Thirty times! Less also is this other one [... ...] people will receive the {} of the trees [... ...] the years and the generations [...] earth to the heights. This is [...

This is the third period, from the time when the Ambassador revealed his image until the Statue will ascend from the lump. It again, that time, is greater than the intermediate; but it does not come equal to the time of the First Man. For this intermediate season, in which the Father of Life and the Mother of Life constructed the universe with its orders, is shorter than that of the First Man.

Great is the period of the First Man! It lasts longer than the season in which the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the world and the Statue ascends. From the time too when the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the universe [... ...] when the Father of Life and the Mother of Life shaped the universe.

These are the [...] three periods of time that occur. ⟨p.58⟩

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core content is a global three-time schema (Light–Darkness mixture → purification/end) stated impersonally; the only late signals are the explicit ‘apostle → disciples’ dialogue framing.
- ¶2: Systematic mythic chronology anchored in core cosmic actors (First Man, King of Darkness) and process (war/struggle, binding, victory) with no institutional ‘pointing-at’.
- ¶3: Classic core cosmological rescue sequence and explicit period delimitation; impersonal ‘this is the first period’ structuring marks the substrate.
- ¶4: Cosmos→cosmos exposition: divine hypostases ordering the universe; explicit temporal boundary (‘reckoned to the second season’) is characteristic of the systematic layer.
- ¶5: Mythic-cosmological sequence remains internal to the Manichaean system (Ambassador’s Image, Sin as a cosmic agent, creation of Adam/Eve, salvific sending/ascension). No Gospel citation or ecclesial application language appears.
- ¶6: Pure period-definition formula closing the three-season schema; no frame or pastoral intrusion.
- ¶7: Dialogue connector (‘disciples asked the apostle’) with no substantive teaching content preserved; typical compilational frame punctuation.
- ¶8: Core is comparative time-measure within the three-season system; the ‘apostle says’ tag is a later Q&A wrapper.
- ¶9: Question is about internal cosmological chronology (duration of First Man’s darkness-period vs. universe-time). The Q&A framing is later editorial apparatus.
- ¶10: Despite second-person rhetoric, the content functions as an internal cosmological comparison of durations rather than pastoral exhortation; the explicit reply tag is a later dialogue wrapper.
- ¶11: Impersonal comparative chronology across the three seasons; fully internal cosmic referents and explicit period labels are substrate markers.
- ¶12: Summative internal time-comparison; no institutional ‘application’ target—still cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶13: Brief closure of the systematic three-period teaching; no frame doxology or church application.
