## Chapter 18
### Concerning the Five War[s that the] Sons of [Li]ght waged with the Sons] of Darkness.

*Most of the chapter preserves a systematic cosmological sequence (five wars: First Man, Living Spirit, Third Ambassador, Jesus the glorious one, counsel of life). Two clear later intrusions occur: (a) a dialogue attribution framing the opening line (FRAME), and (b) a Gospel/"saviour" citation in the First Man section (NT entry-vector). The final lines shift toward ecclesial beatitude language ("elect") and generic moral victory formulae, consistent with the detected late pastoral drift.*

The sons of light waged five wars against the sons of Darkness. The sons of light humiliated the sons of Darkness in them all.

The first war is that of the First living Man, which he waged against the King of the realms of Darkness, and all the rulers that had come forth from the five worlds of [...]. He hunted them with his net, which is the living soul. He [...] in the snare and [...] the face of the [...] his virgin, the living fire [...] they did not find how to escape his net [...] he caught them like fish [...] the five counsels of the five elements [...]. He cut and dug out the root of the five trees, [...] root of the evil tree, like an axe.

The second war is that of the Living Spirit [...]. He has constructed the things and the worlds of light; he spread out the rulers and chained them in the middle [...] the stars in [...] the earths [...] them in the three vessels. For the rulers rebelled and were set against the Living Spirit. They wished to [...] since they knew and realised that he might bind and fetter them with a strong chain. Yet he, the Living Spirit, (appeared to them) with many aspects, with numerous [...], with many hands and great cruelties. He was revealed to them, he imprisoned them with much skill. Not one of them was able to save (himself), and he {...} [...]. Now, since he [...] he trapped them [...] ⟨p.59⟩ [...].

The third war is that of the Third Ambassador who has displayed his image [...] what is below [...] scooped [...] all light and the property [...] the ones that are consumed [...] the entire ruling-power [...] pride of the light [...] extending it [...]. A person comes, who is powerful [...]; he takes his riches [...], silver [...], his pride and wantonness.

This is what the ruling-power is like [...], all riches [...] as they travel [...] light [...]. He cleansed and purified it.

The fourth war is this that Jesus the glorious one waged against all the rebels who had rebelled above and below. When the light was purified from them by the image of the Third Ambassador, their chains loosened [...]. The glorious one in the fourth war; he came and brought [...] a rebellion of the entire ruling-power. He girded himself [...] all of it another time. He constructed the whole structure and he made [...] a great contest; he humiliated therein. [...] he fulfilled the will of the greatness, [he gave the] hope to Adam [...] to his light ship.

The fifth war is this that the counsel of life [...] which is the summons and the obedience [...] great [...] and it placed [...] upon the [...] and the [...] ⟨p.60⟩ [...] of death, which [...] with it. And it was victorious over [...] for ever, and it gave the kingdom [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The attribution to "the enlightener" is compilation-frame language; the remaining sentence is a programmatic cosmological heading that fits the impersonal teaching sequence.
- ¶2: Core is the First Man myth in correspondential/cosmological registers (net = living soul; five counsels/elements; five trees). The explicit "saviour" quotation marks later Christian overlay via Gospel tradition.
- ¶3: Impersonal process-description of cosmic construction/imprisonment (rulers, vessels, chaining) with no citation or institutional address—typical substrate exposition.
- ¶4: Cosmic-agent action (Third Ambassador, image, extraction of light) followed by an internal illustrative parable; no move to contemporary institutions or exhortation.
- ¶5: Continues the cosmological simile of the ruling-power and purification; still system-internal despite lacunae.
- ¶6: Uses named Manichaean cosmological actors and mechanisms (purification by the Image; chains loosening; rebuilding the structure). Mention of Adam may reflect syncretic mythic vocabulary, but there is no NT citation formula; the paragraph stays within cosmic-history narration.
- ¶7: Eschatological-cosmological closure in system terms (counsel of life; summons/obedience; victory over death), not institutional regulation.
- ¶8: Generic moral maxim (good vs wicked) without cosmological specification; likely a late gnomic/doxological tag appended as the sequence closes.
- ¶9: Beatitude addressed to "every elect one" introduces explicit church-category vocabulary (Elect) and exhortative reward framing—typical late pastoral/editorial addition, consistent with end-of-chapter drift.

## Chapter 19
### Concerning the Five Releases: what they are

*This chapter is largely a systematic five-fold cosmological enumeration of “releases/savings” (First Man → Living Spirit → Third Ambassador → Jesus the Splendour → Great Counsel/Summons-and-Obedience), a classic substrate pattern. A brief hagiographic dialogue tag opens the chapter (¶1), and there are later expansions where the mapping crosses from cosmic mechanics into institutional/Christian-historical reference (notably “churches” and “flesh” in ¶6, and “apostles/commandment” in ¶12). The closing beatitude/exhortation (¶13) reads as later pastoral/application voice rather than impersonal exposition.*

They [as]cend to him at the la[st].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core content is the impersonal five-fold schema statement; the speech attribution (“our father speaks”) is a compiling-community frame formula.
- ¶2: Systematic enumerative exposition (“The first…”) and cosmos→cosmos mechanics (First Man, Living Spirit, rulers, aeons) mark substrate teaching.
- ¶3: Impersonal process-description within the cosmic architecture (Father of Life/Mother of Life constructing worlds/earths/rulers; light extracted) indicates the old teaching layer despite lacunae.
- ¶4: Fragmentary continuation of cosmological war/ascension narrative; no institutional anchoring or citation formula appears.
- ¶5: Enumerative third item; correspondential-cosmic mechanism (image-display before ruling-power) stays internal to the system.
- ¶6: Jesus the Splendour as a cosmic revealer/purifier is consistent with substrate cosmology; the abrupt pivot to “churches” and descent “to the form of flesh” signals later Christian/institutional expansion grafted into the enumerative sequence.
- ¶7: Completes the five-fold list in impersonal definitional style; content remains within Manichaean cosmic soteriology (counsel/summons-and-obedience as a cosmic power).
- ¶8: Tabular/iterative recap of releases and their operations, ending with the Last Statue—cosmic eschatological machinery rather than exhortation.
- ¶9: Summative cosmological recap; flagged by application-voice scoring but the extant wording remains third-person and internal to the cosmic narrative (no audience-address or institutional targets). Uncertain due to lacunae.
- ¶10: Too fragmentary to date securely; retained as core by default because it reads like a connective within the cosmological sequence and lacks institutional/citation markers.
- ¶11: Cosmic causation statement tied to the (Third) Ambassador; no late frame/pastoral cues are visible.
- ¶12: Underlying material concerns eschatological operation of the Summons-and-Obedience/Last Statue/judgment/return-to-light (cosmos→cosmos), but an ecclesial insertion (“apostles/commandment”) anchors it to institutional authority, indicating a mixed transmission layer.
- ¶13: Beatitude/exhortation (“Blessed is every person…”) functions as audience-directed piety/application rather than impersonal cosmological mapping.

## Chapter 20
### The Chapter of the Name of the Fathers

*Chapter is dominated by systematic cosmological title→realm (“greatness”) correspondences for multiple divine figures. The only clear later material is the dialogue frame in ¶1–2 and the attribution tag in ¶3; no pastoral drift or NT citation overlay is present.*

Now, the Father, who is the first established thing, shall be called ‘the Father of Greatness’. His greatness is the great earth where he lives, he being established in it [...] the essence of the light that swathes all the rich gods and the angels and the dwellings [...] is set over it.

Furthermore, they shall call the Third Ambassador ‘father’. His greatness is the light ship of living fire wherein he lives, he being established in it.

Once again, Jesus the Splendour shall himself also be called ‘father’. His greatness is the ship of living waters where he lives, he being established in it.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame: “Once again the enlightener speaks…”; question-introduction apparatus rather than teaching exposition.
- ¶2: Petition/Q&A frame (“We beseech you, our master…”) typical of the Kephalaia’s hagiographic editorial container.
- ¶3: Core teaching is an impersonal, systematic mapping internal to the cosmological system: divine title (“Father”) ↔ his ‘greatness’ as a defined cosmic realm (‘great earth’), with hierarchical placement of light/inhabitants. The only late element is the introductory attribution tag.
- ¶4: Systematic extension of the same internal correspondence pattern (title ‘father’ ↔ specific cosmic ‘greatness’/dwelling). No institutional application, no citation formulas.
- ¶5: Uses Jesus the Splendour as a named cosmic entity within the Manichaean hierarchy, not as a Gospel citation; continues the same realm/dwelling correspondence pattern (father-title ↔ ship-realm).

## Chapter 21

*After a brief hagiographic/dialogue incipit (¶1), the chapter is almost entirely a systematic, impersonal cosmological exposition: the Father of Greatness established in five light limbs (storehouses/qualities) and also in twelve light limbs (wisdoms), then a sequential description of the five. No explicit institutional, exhortational, or NT-overlay material appears in the preserved text; the low teaching-density in ¶8 seems driven by damage/lacunae rather than a register shift.*

Very great is [land of] light in the presence of they who will hear and know it, due to the first Father of Greatness. How he ex[ists and is] established in his five light limbs, which are [ ... the store]houses that have neither limit nor measure to the[m ... ] mind, thought, insight, counsel and [consideration ... ] his twelve light limbs that are his twelve wisdoms.

Now, there are five great light lim[bs] in each one [ ... ] five grea[t] springs of praise gush for[th ... ] five great [ ... ] lig[ht ... ]

The first is his light that enlig[htens ... ] it gushed forth from him and pour[ed ... ] a[l]] aeons.

The second is the perfume of the [ ... ] and pervades all the aeons of greatness.

The third is his living voice of [ ... ... ] all his aeons [ ... ]

The fourth is his [ ... ]-ness [ ... ] gushes forth from him [ ... o]ut [ ... ]

⟨p.65⟩ The fif[th] is his great glorio[us ... ] that [ ... , w]hich [has been ma]nifested all the tim[e], whi[ch contin]ues [ ... ] all of them. It was sculpted in them [ ... ] sealed in the [m ... ] sealed [ ... ] as they send [ ... ] in [ ... ... ] they too receive nourishment [ ... ] immediately [ ... his] living voi[ce ... ] joy [ ... ] set up anew. It shall gush forth from it [ ... ] they shall [ ... ] and become rich in the [ ... ] too is beautiful [ ... ] portray[al ... ] adornment [ ... ] and they live [ ... ... ] for ev[e]r.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue/homiletic frame: "Once again the apostle ... to his disciples" + direct address "Hear this lesson"; this is compilation apparatus rather than the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Systematic cosmological structuring (five limbs/storehouses + twelve limbs/wisdoms) in an expository voice; both sides of the mapping remain internal to the Light-realm architecture.
- ¶3: Iterative numeration and internal cosmic parallelism (five limbs ↔ five springs of praise); no audience-address or institutional anchoring.
- ¶4: First item in a fivefold sequence describing emanative function within the aeons; impersonal, process description typical of the older teaching style.
- ¶5: Second item of the fivefold limbs, expressed as a cosmic quality (perfume) pervading aeons—correspondential register (scent/emanation) stays within the system.
- ¶6: Third limb as "living voice" (a standard Manichaean cosmological potency) in a purely intra-cosmic description; no frame markers.
- ¶7: Continues the enumerated limb-sequence; the verb "gushes forth" sustains the emanation/process register rather than exhortation or polemic.
- ¶8: Despite heavy lacunae, this reads as the fifth limb’s cosmic effecting/inscribing/sealing and nourishment/renewal within the aeons—no turn toward church practice, commands, or Gospel citation; low register score likely reflects damaged text rather than later overlay.

Also, at the time when it generated itself [ ... ] it formed the fruits, it still being set in them. They summoned the five garments, the sons of the Fir[st] Man.

And at the time when it generated its own self [f]rom the fruits through its offspring, they fell upon the earth and [ ... ] strength from the elements and were nourished by them. [ ... ... ] wickedness, together with cruelty [ ... ] in the ruler, the leader of all the demons. He hum[bled] his worlds and his powers; he gathered them all into him. He made himself ready for the battle; he set himself to the war with that Light earth, and the great King who dwells in it.

At that very moment the First Man approached his garments, the gods [who sh]ine forth. He spoke with them, that he might entrust to them a [ ... ] he [reve]aled to them and taught them about everything that [ ... ... ] them. He assumed them. He arranged them [ ... ... ] He draped them over one another upon his limbs [ ... ⟨p.70⟩ ... ] danger, it [ ... ... ] the heights of the earth [ ... ... ] with all its powers [ ... beg]inning, it being immeasurable [ ... ... ]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a formulaic narrative hinge (“Once again it happened …”), characteristic of Kephalaia editorial framing; the remainder is too lacunose to securely recover a clean core statement.
- ¶2: Impersonal negative-theological exposition about eternal essences and their (un)knowability; no institutional pointing, no citation apparatus.
- ¶3: Underlying content stays within cosmic ontology (Land of Death/essence existing ‘for ever and ever’), but the opening address signals a later dialogical presentation layer.
- ¶4: System-internal cosmogonic boundary: unknowable eternal kingdoms, then a dated threshold event (“from the time when it stirred…”) initiating the war; no institutional application.
- ¶5: Core cosmogonic action (First Man preventing intrusion; onset of Mixture) is followed by an editorially inflected reflection on teaching authority and recounting, marked by ‘apostle[s]’ and report-voice.
- ¶6: Purely cosmological mechanics (stirring in essence, Darkness/Light/abyss, nature-body strength ‘more than the Adamant’) with no outward-pointing institutional identifiers.
- ¶7: Fragmentary but remains within the war-cosmology register (Death, envy, land of Darkness, wounds/assault).
- ¶8: System-internal description of movement/compulsion in relation to the land of Darkness; no audience exhortation or institutional application.
- ¶9: Classic substrate-style cosmogony: five-element articulation of Darkness, generation/emanations, storehouses/abyss, bestial forms—cosmos→cosmos mapping without contemporary polemic.
- ¶10: Internal war-planning narrative of the Darkness ruler; no frame formulas or institutional targeting.
- ¶11: Cosmic counter-move by the Father of Greatness in response to war; remains within the emanational-conflict system.
- ¶12: Too lacunose for fine stratification, but nothing indicates NT citation, institutional regulation, or hagiographic apparatus; retained as core by default within the surrounding uninterrupted cosmogonic sequence.
- ¶13: Elemental fivefold structuring and depth/trees imagery—system-internal cosmogony consistent with the oldest teaching register.
- ¶14: Fivefold ‘garments’/sons of the First Man is a standard Manichaean cosmological structure; no editorial pointing beyond the system.
- ¶15: Continuous internal narrative: offspring/fall/elemental nourishment → demon ruler mobilization; ‘Light earth’ and ‘great King’ remain cosmological, not ecclesial.
- ¶16: Core emanational-war preparation in system terms (First Man, shining gods/garments, investiture ‘upon his limbs’). No later frame formulas (‘apostle says…’) are present in the paragraph as given.

## Chapter 24
### [... / ...]

*Despite the synopsis’s ‘composite’ character, most paragraphs preserve an older cosmological time-measure teaching: emanation durations are expressed by internal cosmic imagery (storehouses, aeons, borders, descent/ascent, Last Statue, chaining). A later editorial/meta-teaching layer appears where the text pivots to what ‘apostles/teachers/chosen’ can or cannot proclaim about the two eternities; these paragraphs are treated as non-core. Several paragraphs are MIXED where brief audience-address or transmission talk (‘listen’, ‘I recounted to you’, ‘disciples’) introduces otherwise core cosmological exposition.*

[...] a great ju[d/ge ...] now also a ti[me ...] that they sculpt[ed ...] he was revealed in the Father, the first established thing.

W[he]n [...] the two summonses that he called [...] the Mother of Life [...] a long duration in the first Father [...]

For someone, if he might set a measure and rule [...] the earth {particles} and the {pebbles} of the mountains and [...] of the sea and the body of the firmaments. Should they be poured [...] the body of the four mountains and the three vessels that surround the universe; if they shall all be poured out [...] these fine {granules}.

Happen you know that the grains of the earth {particles} are measurable, [and those of] the [entire] universe. It is possible to count the {particles} of the earth each year [...] the sand grains of the entire universe; but the duration of the time that the Great Spirit spent in the Father, [the] first established thing, one will not be able to make a count of it!

He first sculpted her like this. He established her in his inner storehouses in quiet and silence. When [they had] need of her she was called and came forth of the Father [of Greatness]. She looked at all her aeons of light!

Once again, from the time when she was called to [...] go to [...] she gave [...] upon his [bod]y [...] all the aeons [...]; she also [...] all of them. This [is also the case] with the First Man.

This is how the Man spent a long time being anointed in the Mother, set fast in her inner storehouses [...]; the extent of time—from when the First Man went down to the contest, till the time when the [Statue (?) co]mes i[n ...] this time [...] he appeared [...]

Again, from when he was called and came forth, till the time when he [himse]lf called the five shining [gods] who are his sons; they themselves also spent a long time existing in their father! It is the time that occurred from the coming down of the First Man till the going up of the Last Statue. The five gods spent this much duration being anointed in the First Man. Yet, [at the] time when they were awaited, they were called and came forth.

At the time when the Mother of Life was called from the Father of Greatness, when she expelled herself and came down by her own wish, from the heights of the Father to the earth, [she would spend thousands of] years and many ten thousands until she reached the earth. [Fo]r there is no measure nor rule to the heights of the Father. Rather, [just as] he had called her in his Great Spirit, so he set her upon [the] land of greatness through one of these three that are counted to the twelve hidden ones within the light veil, the ones [esta]blished after the [...] region of the south.

[The] matter of the Mother [of L]ife is like this. When [the Father called her he established her in the place he pleased,] and she examined everywhere that [...] a blink of an eye, or like a [...] bird [...]

This also is lik[e ... the] Mother of Life in [...] whenever [...] cast her out [...] upon her to the place [...] him. He perfected her in his Great Spirit and set her in [the place he pl]eased, according to [his will].

[Th]is [also is how] they called the [five g]ods from the [Fi]rst Man. They were set before him, as he wished that [...]-ness [...] down, that they would rule in them [...] one another. He ordered and distributed them [...] the son. He stripped himself of them [...] and he [...] he girt and bound himself to [...] in a [...] of silence upon the border of the earth [...]

Again, he too, the First Man, spent a long period and [time] until he gave power to his sons; until he assu[m]ed them. After he came to wear them he stood fast in the heights between the borders. Again, he spent a duration and time like this, corresponding to such amount and measure as the raindrops and dew and falls of mist that come down in the universe. This is how he spent this duration also, standing between the borders, until the hour when he cast himself down to the contest!

Whenever the Father of Greatn[e]ss called the Great Spirit and the First Man and the gods of the glorious one, who are provided to enable them to reveal him; at that very time [he] sculpted the Great Builder in the [land of] light, [and] the Living Spirit with the mighty and active gods who came [from] him. Also, at that time, he sculpted the [Amb]assador, with Jesus the Splendour and the Virgin of Light and the Pillar of Glory, and the gods who came from them. [All these the Father of Greatness sculpted at] a single time. They came forth of one another, one after one; the time when [...] and the work.

from the time when the Darkness raised itself up to [...] essence of the light [...] these three emanations [...] from the will of the Father against the enemy [...] he has [...] Darkness [...] according to the will [...] the universe [...] in it [...] separate out [...] rest him in which way, and he rejoices [...] in his kingdom; until [the time when the face] of the Father will be unveiled over [all] the gods of glory, and all the rich ones of [...] the em[anations] who came forth against the enemy.

[From] that time when he sculpted these three [...] till the time when the Father would unveil his face, [and they] go in to his hidden storehouses, and he calls them [...] establishes each one of them to his place. There is no measure nor rule to this period until that time.

about this Matter, this thought of death [...] ; for [this] is the way that [...] came up to its storehouses. It has [...] in its five elements.

Again, it also [...] the period and the time that it spent, until it revealed itself to its elements, before it came to the trees. It spent a great period and a long time established [...] before it came up to the trees. It was [...] in the trees. It spent a long time and duration [...] existing in them, before it sculpted the fru[its ...] and sculpted the fruits.

Again, it spent a long [time] in the fruits. Not little is the period and the time that it spent existing in the fruits, before it sculpted [...] after a long time [...] the d[emon]s of its five worlds in various forms [can not (?) ] be measured [...] in p[erfec]tion of the fruits in the demons and fiends. They fell upon their earth and [...] were nourished. Not little is the period that they [spent in the wo]rlds, until they were nourished amongst [...] to make [...] that land which shines, and the great [King] who dwells there.

[You] know that [...] that in [...] at the time when he [...] its essence. It assumed [...] Matter [...] to the war [...] the Perfect Man. [From the time] when the war occurred [...]

[The first is ...] he came to be in some [...] when they ensnared a [...]

[The second is ...] that he built [...] the universe was established. The zone [...] down until the time when it will be dissolved.

The third is the [...] the things and the universe will [...] from the time when they were established there, till the time when their end will be complete and their existence ceases [...]

[The fourth is the dissolution when they will dissolve the universe] there, and all things are destroyed. Obliteration reaches them in that great fire, [which will] burn them for fourteen hundred and sixty-eight years.

[The fift]h is the way that the Last Statue will be sculpted [from] the remnant of all things. The light separates out to its place, and goes up and rules in its kingdom. However, [the da]rkness [...] and they take it in to the tomb [...] and they are bound with it in an eternal chain.

Now, from [the tim]e when this first war occurred, till the time of this chain [...] these five things [...] light.

However, before [...] from it out of the powers [...] and measure it; because it is eternal.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Lacunosely formulates a primordial ‘first established thing’ revealed in the Father; no citation or institutional/application hook is visible.
- ¶2: Emanation/‘summons’ language in a purely cosmological register (Mother of Life, first Father, duration).
- ¶3: Old ‘measurement’ analogy: cosmic architecture (firmaments, four mountains, three vessels) used to express immeasurable duration—cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: Contrasts measurable created quantities with uncountable divine duration; Great Spirit ‘in the Father’ is internal cosmological exposition.
- ¶5: Pure emanation mechanics (sculpted/established/called; storehouses; aeons of light). No institutional pointing.
- ¶6: Continues the emanation-duration schema and parallels it with the First Man; still internal cosmological description.
- ¶7: Core temporal span is given by descent/ascent markers (contest; Last Statue), but a first-person delivery aside suggests later compilation voice.
- ¶8: Systematic, internally bounded timing of emanation (father→sons; anointing; descent/ascent; Last Statue) with no external application.
- ¶9: Core is a highly systematized cosmological timing/topography (twelve hidden ones; light veil; south region). The ‘listen/how I teach’ is later delivery framing.
- ¶10: Core describes instantaneous perception/agency of the Mother of Life (blink of an eye / bird simile), but an inserted ‘instruction of the people’ clause shifts to contemporary-aimed didactic purpose.
- ¶11: Emanation ‘perfected/set’ within Great Spirit; purely internal divine action language.
- ¶12: Old systematic ‘calling/ordering/distributing’ of the five gods; border-of-earth motif stays within cosmic architecture.
- ¶13: Characteristic substrate ‘measure-by-cosmos’ similes (raindrops/dew/mist) and degree-like staging (between the borders → contest).
- ¶14: Systematic emanation catalogue in a fully internal cosmological hierarchy (including Jesus the Splendour as a cosmic being, not a Gospel citation).
- ¶15: Core contrasts Light vs Darkness and frames history between uprising and final unveiling—cosmic timeline; the ‘recount to you’ is later delivery voice.
- ¶16: Impersonal temporal measurelessness plus storehouse/placing mechanics: classic structural cosmology.
- ¶17: Meta-discourse about ‘teachers/saviours/apostles of greatness’ and what they can proclaim; institutional transmission register rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶18: Continues institutional ‘apostles and teachers’ limitation language; focuses on proclamation rather than describing cosmic mechanisms.
- ¶19: Fragmentary question-form about an ‘eternal end’ suggests an editorial/Q-style transition rather than core exposition.
- ¶20: Core resumes internal evil-process mechanics (Matter; storehouses; five elements). The surrounding ‘who asks / disciples / I told you’ is later delivery framing.
- ¶21: Evil emanation staged through elements → trees → fruits; entirely within the cosmological process register.
- ¶22: Highly characteristic internal cosmology: five worlds; demons/fiends; nourishment; emergence of a shining land and its King (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶23: Though lacunose, it stays inside the mythic-cosmological conflict schema (Matter vs Perfect Man), not institutional application.
- ¶24: Begins a numbered/list structure about cosmic history; despite damage, it reads as internal cosmological sequencing.
- ¶25: Internal cosmogonic stage (‘built’; universe established; dissolution horizon).
- ¶26: Temporal span of created existence framed cosmologically (established → end complete).
- ¶27: Eschatological ‘great fire’ within the system; the specific year-count may reflect later calculation, but it remains internal to cosmic process (no institutional mapping).
- ¶28: Classic Manichaean end-process: Last Statue; separation; Light enthronement; Darkness chained—fully cosmological.
- ¶29: Summarizing temporal bracket (war → chain) for the preceding five items; remains an internal cosmic chronology.
- ¶30: Core remnant asserts an eternal, immeasurable pre-stage; the excised clause applies the teaching to church roles (‘apostles/chosen/teachers’), indicating later editorial reflection.
- ¶31: Fragment continues the meta-epistemic limitation theme (‘No person is able ...’), not advancing cosmological correspondences/mechanics.
- ¶32: Explicit institutional roles (‘the apostle and the teacher’) in a statement about what can be spoken; late frame/meta layer, not substrate exposition.

## Chapter 25
### [Concerning the Advent of Five Fathers from the Five Limbs of the Father]

*This chapter is dominated by a compact five-fold correspondential mapping: five divine/cosmic hypostases are said to “come from” five internal faculties/limbs of the Father (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration). ¶1 is a later dialogue/frame header (“the apostle speaks”); ¶2–¶3 read as impersonal, systematic cosmological exposition with no institutional or Gospel anchoring.*

The [Ambas]sador [came] from his mind; the Beloved of the Lights [from his thought]; the Mother of Life from his insight; [Jesus] the Beloved from his counsel; the Virgin of Lig[ht f]rom his consideration.

So in this way were summone[d these five] fathers. These five advents came from the five limbs [of the Father]. One came from another and was manifested in its season.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic opening and dialogue attribution (“Once again the apostle speaks… He speaks thus:”). ‘Apostle’ is a compilation-frame marker rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶2: Five-fold, degree-like mapping entirely within the cosmic system: hypostasis → internal faculty/limb of the Father. No citation formula, no exhortation, no institutional referent; ‘[Jesus] the Beloved’ functions here as a named cosmic being within the hierarchy, not a Gospel overlay.
- ¶3: Continues the same impersonal, structural exposition: reiterates the five-limbs origin and gives a process statement (“manifested in its season”) rather than applying it to a community or ethical instruction.

---

## Chapter 26
### Concerning the First Man and the Ambassador and the Ship; what they resemble.

*This chapter is largely a set of internally-cosmological similes (First Man, Living Spirit, Great Spirit in the Ship, Ambassador) describing redemption within the Manichaean system. Only the final paragraph pivots into direct exhortation to disciples (application voice), marking a later pastoral graft. Paragraph 1 contains a clear hagiographic/frame attribution that must be stripped to recover the teaching.*

The First [Man] is like a great distinguished man whose treasure has been taken away from him. He came to pursue his treasure. This is how the [First] Man too came after his sons from the heights, so that he might redeem them and retrieve his treasure, which had been scattered amongst his enemies.

Once again, [he is like a man] whose two sons were taken away from him by [ ... He] came out toward them to redeem them. Again, this is how the First Man [ ... ] shines in the heights because of his two sons, who were separated from him. They are his living soul that exist in two powers, which makes four.

Once again, the ship [of the day] is like a great spear [ ... ... The Great Spirit too, which is in it, is like a wise [crafts]man who might beautify and set aright [all] the armour for war.

Once again, the Living Spirit is like a combatant who might go towards a king's son who has been taken prisoner of war, and he redeems him from the hands of his enemies.

[The Amba]ssador too is like a great man [ ... ] because of the treasure [ ... ] hand of his prisoners [ ... ] these light powers [ ... ... the] sons and their soul, so that they would [ ... ... (the soul) might] be brought forth in the land wherein it was imprisoned.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The opening speech-attribution is a compiling-community frame marker; the remaining content is a cosmological simile mapping First Man’s descent and recovery of the Living Soul/treasure entirely within the Light–Darkness economy (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶2: System-internal cosmological accounting (sons/living soul/powers) presented as a simile without institutional address, citation, or biographical praise; the mapping stays within the mythic-cosmic architecture despite the unclear numeration.
- ¶3: Pure correspondential simile: Ship of the Day and Great Spirit described by crafted-object/war-gear imagery (cosmos→cosmos), with no turn toward church practice or audience exhortation.
- ¶4: Impersonal, mythic-redemptive mechanism stated as a simile (Living Spirit as rescuer) with no overlay citations or institutional application; reads as core teaching style.
- ¶5: Despite lacunae, the content remains fully within the redemption-myth register (Ambassador, Light powers, imprisoned soul), not pointing outward to contemporary institutions; likely part of an older simile-collection.
- ¶6: Direct second-person exhortation (“You yourselves must be purifiers…”) addressed to disciples, aiming at membership/eschatological ‘counting’ and communal identity; this is application voice (cosmos→audience conduct) rather than system exposition.

---

## Chapter 27
### Concerning the Five Forms that exist in the Rulers of Darkness.

*The chapter is dominated by a systematic correspondential body→world mapping (five forms from the five worlds of darkness) followed by additional triads of ruler-qualities. The final two paragraphs pivot into direct second-person exhortation (“my beloved ones…”)—an application/pastoral add-on consistent with the chapter’s strong second-half drift signal. The opening contains a hagiographic seating/speaking frame that must be stripped to recover the core teaching introduction.*

⟨p.77⟩
Consider the ruler, the leader of all the powers of darkness. Now, five forms exist in his body, corresponding to (the archetype) of the five creations that exist in the five worlds of darkness.

Indeed, his head is lion-faced, which came about from the world of fire. His wings and his shoulders are eagle-faced, after the likeness of the children of the wind. His hands and his feet are daemons, after the likeness of the children of the world of smoke. His belly is dragon-faced, after the likeness of the world of darkness. His tail is the form of the fish, which belongs to the world of the children of water. These five shapes are in him, and they came from the five creations of the five worlds of darkness.

⟨p.78⟩
Should he wish, he shall walk on his two feet [ ... ] world of smoke. Also, when he wants, he shall [ ... ] four [ ... ] on his hands and feet [ ... ] like the children of fire. Should he wish, he shall rise up on his wings, like the children of the wind. Also, should he wish, he shall plunge down to the waters like the children of water. Again, should he wish, he shall crawl on his belly like the children of darkness. These five shapes are in him.

Also, there are three other (qualities) in him.

The first: [ ... ] his powers [ ... ]

The second: Shall a [ ... ] his magic arts. When he wishes, he shall make an invocation over himself, and hide from his powers. When he wants, he shall show himself to them and strike a death-blow by his magic arts. Any word he might utter at that moment, he shall [wreak magic] by it.

The third: His body is strong, a[1]] [ ... ] nails and claws of his powers are not able to penetrate it. No iron nor copper body will equal him [ ... ]; they will not be able to destroy him, because he was formed and fashioned from the hard-hearted counsel of Matter, the mother of demons and fiends.

Again, there are three others in him.

When he wants, his fire shall {blaze), and his entire body be made like the [ ... of] fire.

Also, when he wishes, he shall send out frost, and his entire body freezes like a [ ... of] snow.

The third: Whenever his powers are stood before him, he shall look at them and understand; for [whatever ex]ists in their heart he shall understand, what is in their heart and their face so long as they are stood before him. When they retreat before him and distance themselves from him, he shall not understand what is in their heart. There is no life in him whatsoever, but his 'life' is the bile of anger that is apparent on his face and in his fear. Shall a [ ... ] destruction that is establ[ished be]fore him.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core teaching begins with impersonal structural exposition (five forms ↔ five creations ↔ five worlds). The paragraph’s initial ‘apostle among the congregation’ is a late framing device, hence MIXED with frame stripped.
- ¶2: Pure cosmos→cosmos correspondential mapping (body parts/animal forms ↔ the five dark worlds and their ‘children’), in a systematic expository voice.
- ¶3: Continues the internal cosmological description of the ruler’s five-form mobility, each motion keyed to a dark-world type; no audience-address or institutional application.
- ¶4: Numbered/iterative structural marker typical of the core’s schematic presentation (introducing a triad of qualities).
- ¶5: Fragmentary, but preserves the schematic ‘first/second/third’ enumeration style of the core teaching.
- ¶6: Describes an intrinsic faculty/operation of the ruler within the dark cosmos (concealment, appearance, lethal speech/arts), not a warning about contemporary practitioners.
- ¶7: Cosmic-material constitution and hierarchy language (‘formed and fashioned… Matter’) in impersonal explanatory mode; still fully internal to the system.
- ¶8: Another schematic triad-introduction, indicating continued core enumeration.
- ¶9: Internal elemental transformation ability attributed to the ruler; correspondential/physical-cosmic description without exhortation.
- ¶10: Pairs with the previous line as another intrinsic transformation (fire/frost), continuing the core’s descriptive list.
- ¶11: Faculty-description (bounded perception/knowledge; ‘life’ as anger-bile) presented as cosmic mechanics, not moral instruction—typical core voice despite lacunae.
- ¶12: Direct second-person exhortation with application voice (“Behold… my beloved ones… Hold yourselves secure…”) applying the ruler-description to audience conduct; likely later pastoral/application graft.
- ¶13: Continues exhortative second-person register (zeal/perfection, ‘inherit life for ever and ever’) rather than cosmological mapping/mechanics; fits end-of-chapter editorial drift.

---

## Chapter 28
### [Concerning the T]welve Judges [of] the Father

*This chapter is largely a systematic cosmological catalogue of twelve judging powers emanated from the Father of Greatness (CORE). The opening dialogue tag is a late hagiographic frame (¶1), and the closing exhortation to the community is pastoral/application (¶16), consistent with the chapter’s strong second-half drift. Two list-items show later institutional/Manichaean-church coloring inside an otherwise cosmic sequence: (a) Jesus the Splendour labelled “father of all the apostles” (¶10), and (b) Light Mind “choos[ing] the holy church” (¶13); these are best treated as MIXED with the ecclesial identifiers removed.*

Twelve judges exist; the great mighty givers of judgement! They were sent and came from the exalted greatness to this place. They were assigned to different works according to the command that the Judge, who belongs to the greatness, entrusted to all these judges. Now he, the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges, who exists and is established in the aeons of greatness; he is the Father of Greatness.

The first judge is the First Man, he who humiliated the ruler of Darkness in the beginning. He judged him according to a judgement of righteousness; for he had exalted himself against a kingdom that was not his own.

The second judge is the Living Spirit, he who came and bore up the Man. He too made a judgement on all the powers of darkness; which had wounded the garments of the First Man.

The third judge is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, she who beautified and set up the heavenly part of the universe, according to her pleasure; which [...] her. She judged the rulers above; she fettered and set them firmly in the place that is fitting for them.

The fourth judge is the Great Builder, he who built the new aeon of joy. He judged, according to a judgement of righteousness, the storehouses of the enemy; that death may not well up from this time on. He has constructed a prison for the enemy. Also, upon the crown of the building he has constructed a throne for the First Man and all the fathers of light; they who engaged the struggle with the evil one and were victorious over him.

⟨p.80⟩
The fifth judge is the great King of Honour, he who dwells and is established in the seventh firmament. He is the judge of all the firmaments who gives a true judgement, according to a judgement of righteousness, upon all the powers and all the kingdoms of the firmaments.

The sixth judge is [the Th]ird Ambassador, he who came and displayed his image. He purified the light [...] which his own [...] also of the enemy, he has [...]

The seventh judge is ... ... the worlds [... as he gives the victory to] they who are steadfast; but he condemns the ones who are convicted ... ... he shall become for them the steps of the way (?) [...]; but the convicted he sweeps and casts out to the depths.

The eighth judge is Jesus the Splendour, because after the Ambassador had displayed his image and the light was taken [...] a mass of rulers rose up from above and below. Then they sent Jesus the Splendour. He came ... ... he descended and made a judgement in the firmaments. He separated the steadfast from the convicted.

The ninth judge is the Virgin of Light, [she who took the heart] of the powers by her image, gathering her own ones into her. She makes a judgement on the ruler of the moist, and the ruler of the dry.

The tenth judge is the Judge who dwells in the atmosphere, judging all mankind. He makes a separation between they who are good and they who are evil, setting apart the righteous from the sinners.

⟨p.81⟩
The eleventh judge is the Light Mind, he who shall come and appear in the world. And he separates light from darkness, and sets the truth apart from lawlessness.

The twelfth judge is the great counsel, which is the summons and the obedience, which is appointed in the elements. It is the one that makes a separation between the good and the evil ones. At the end also it can gather itself together and sculpt its own self in the Last Statue. And it separates light from darkness.

These are the twelve great and mighty judges who were sent. They came from the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges. He is the Father that is hidden and immeasurable, the one who exists; who is established in the aeons of greatness upon the great throne of honour, which is the King of Honour [...] in his kingdom.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again… disciples”) characteristic of the Kephalaia’s editorial/hagiographic apparatus.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological exposition: emanation/sending from Greatness; no audience address or institutional identification; establishes the cosmic hierarchy of Judge and judges.
- ¶3: Cosmic-historical function of a named emanation (First Man) within the Light–Darkness conflict; “righteousness” here serves as a cosmic judicial quality, not pastoral exhortation.
- ¶4: System-internal mythic mechanics (rescue/raising; judgement on powers) tied to core Manichaean beings.
- ¶5: Cosmic architecture and binding of rulers; the entire mapping stays within the cosmological system (heavenly part, rulers above, fitting place).
- ¶6: Cosmic construction (new aeon, prison, throne) and containment of “death”; still system-internal despite slightly heightened rhetorical tone.
- ¶7: Firmament-level hierarchy and jurisdiction; structured cosmic geography (“seventh firmament… all the firmaments”).
- ¶8: Named emanation (Third Ambassador) in a functional cosmic role (image-display; purification of light); lacunae obscure details but register remains cosmological.
- ¶9: Eschatological sorting expressed as cosmic judgement (victory/condemnation; casting to depths). No direct audience address or institutional target.
- ¶10: Core is a cosmological mission of Jesus the Splendour within the firmaments; the “apostles” label is a later institutional vocabulary intrusion typical of Mani’s/community framing.
- ¶11: Virgin of Light acting through image/attraction; rulers of moist/dry are cosmic divisions (system-internal correspondential structuring).
- ¶12: Though ethically worded, it still describes a cosmic station (“dwells in the atmosphere”) and its judicial function; no exhortation or institutional identification.
- ¶13: Underlying item fits the cosmic list (Light Mind; light/dark separation), but the “holy church” insertion is a later editorial application within the list-form.
- ¶14: Highly systematic, system-internal eschatological mechanism (elements; end; Last Statue; separation of light/darkness) with impersonal explanatory voice.
- ¶15: Cosmic recapitulation/closure returning to the transcendent Father of Greatness; no behavioural address; fits a core summary colophon to the list.
- ¶16: Direct audience address and imperatives (“my beloved ones… you yourselves may walk… Judge…”) with moral exhortation and communal conduct: classic pastoral/application layer, matching the chapter’s second-half drift.

## Chapter 29
### Concerning the Eighteen Great Thrones of all the Fathers

*This chapter is largely a systematic, numbered cosmological catalogue (thrones across aeons/ships/firmaments), characteristic of the older teaching layer. A later compiler has wrapped it with a dialogue opening (¶1) and appended/extended the sequence with ecclesial-apostolic identification (¶17) and summary/doxological material (¶11–12, ¶18). One paragraph (¶10) shows a pivot from cosmic identification to institutional reference (“churches”), suggesting editorial application grafted onto a core throne-item.*

Eighteen [great thrones for all the fathers] exist and are appointed, which are established in eighteen places! While there are nine among them [appointed in] the outer aeons, they exist [i]n all the worlds of the damage.

The first throne is the throne of the Father, the God of [trut]h, the King of the aeons of greatness, the one who exists; who is established [...] he alone, in his own essen[c]e, [...] apart. This is the first throne [...] the one that surpasses all the thrones.

⟨p.82⟩ The second throne is that of the [gl]orious Mother of Life. She is the origin of all the emanations that have come to this world.

The third throne is constructed for the father, the First Man. He is the one who humiliated the entire first enmity, which existed in the worlds of darkness.

The fourth throne is that of the Beloved of the Lights, the great honoured Beloved of the aeons of greatness.

The fifth throne is that of the glorious Great Builder; the great architect who built the new aeon for a newness, for [a ...] for [the] fathers of light; also for a place of binding and a prison for the enemy and his powers.

[The sixth thro]n[e] is that of the Living Spirit, the [glorious] mighty one, he who bore up the First Man out from the [land of darkness]; who also set in order and constructed the world [s ...] both for a cleansing of the light, [and] for a binding [of the] powers of the enmity.

The seventh th[rone is] that of the Third Ambassador, the king of the zone, also the master of all advices.

The eighth throne is that of Jesus the Splendour, who [is] the releaser and redeemer [of] all souls.

The ninth throne is the throne of the great M[ind ...]; and all the [life] that will be purified from the world returns to him.

Three thrones in the ship of [the d]ay: one is that of the Ambassador, the second i[s that of] the Great Spirit, the third is that of the Living Spirit.

⟨p.83⟩ Once again, there are another three in the ship that [belongs to the night]: the first is the throne of Jesus the Splendour, [the second is that of] the First Man, the third is tha[t of the Virgin of] Light. These six thrones are established in the two ships.

Also, the seventh throne is established in the seventh firmament. The great King of Honour sits upon it.

The eighth throne is established in the atmosphere. The Judge of truth sits upon it, he who judges all mankind. Three paths shall be distinguished before him: one to death, one to life, on[e] to the mingling.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core catalogue statement is impersonal and structural (numerical scheme + aeons/worlds); the opening speech-to-disciples formula is a later compiling frame.
- ¶2: Numbered, impersonal throne-listing with standard Manichaean divine titles; no citation, exhortation, or institutional application.
- ¶3: Systematic emanational cosmology (Mother of Life as source of emanations) in catalogue form; stylistically consistent with core teaching lists.
- ¶4: Classic mythic-cosmological role statement (First Man vs. enmity/Darkness worlds) presented as part of a numbered structure.
- ¶5: Throne-item within the enumerated cosmic hierarchy; “Beloved of the Lights” functions as a personified cosmological figure/title within the system.
- ¶6: Cosmic mechanics and architecture (new aeon; prison/binding of enemy powers) stated as ontology/function, not exhortation or church regulation.
- ¶7: Systematic cosmological function (rescue + world-construction for purification/binding) in the same enumerative register.
- ¶8: Catalogue identification of a named cosmological being with titles internal to the cosmic administration (“zone”, “advices”).
- ¶9: Despite “Jesus” vocabulary, this is a standard Manichaean cosmic hypostasis (“Jesus the Splendour”) presented as a throne-item without Gospel citation formulas; thus it reads as internal cosmology rather than NT overlay.
- ¶10: Opens as a normal throne-item within the cosmic list (“great Mind”); then pivots to institutional reach (“churches”), which breaks the cosmos→cosmos register and suggests editorial application.
- ¶11: Summary + doxological cadence (“for ever and ever, amen”) characteristic of editorial closure rather than core catalogue items.
- ¶12: Editorial transition marker (“In contrast… other nine”) functioning as structural stitching between two sub-lists.
- ¶13: Systematic triadic sub-structure located in a cosmic vehicle (“ship of the day”); pure internal mapping.
- ¶14: Parallel triad in the complementary cosmic ship; “Once again” here is iterative catalogue rhetoric, not a disciple-speech frame.
- ¶15: Cosmic geography (“seventh firmament”) + enthroned divine title; continues the internal cosmic placement scheme.
- ¶16: Eschatological-cosmological mechanics (judgment + three destinies including “mingling”) expressed as system-description, not moral exhortation.
- ¶17: Institutional/hagiographic identification (“apostle in the holy church… has come to you at this time”) is a classic editorial self-location of the compiler’s present and not part of the timeless cosmic catalogue.
- ¶18: Fragmentary concluding summary of the nine thrones ‘in this world’; functions as closing stitching rather than a fresh cosmological item, and appears in the drifted second-half register.

## Chapter 30
### Concerning the Three Garments

*This is a short, largely continuous cosmological instruction about the Living Spirit’s three ‘garments’ (wind, fire, water) and their spatial deployment across the zone. The only clear later material is the formulaic dialogue opening and direct address in ¶1; otherwise the chapter stays within cosmos→cosmos description without institutional or NT overlay.*

⟨p.83⟩
that when the Living Spirit came he brought the three garments; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He assumed them from [ ... ] he has [ ... ] in them in those three garments [ ... ] he established [them] for all the things of [this univers]e, both above and below. He arranged them in the [ ... ] he was displayed. Afterwards [ ... ] his works [ ... ] the [ ... h]e stripped them off before [the ri]ghteous ones.

⟨p.84⟩
Now, when he was about to loose [n them, ... he] divested himself of them from their (clasp) and he [ ... i]n them, and thus these three g[arme]nts are arranged [ ... ] from below the edges of the zone as they clothe i[t al]l [ ... ] up [ ... ] above [ ... ] up [ ... ] above them strips them of the zone from that place at its topmost point; and it is placed below them and ra[ised u]p to the heights above, from which it had been sent o[u]t at their beginning.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The paragraph begins with a formulaic speaking-scene plus second-person address (frame/application), then shifts into impersonal cosmological mechanics: the Living Spirit’s three garments (wind/fire/water) established ‘for all the things of this universe, both above and below’ and later ‘stripped… before the righteous ones’.
- ¶2: Continuous, technical cosmological exposition (no citation, no institutional application): divestment and stretching of the three garments across the zone from lower edges to the ‘topmost point’, staying entirely within the cosmic architecture.

## Chapter 31
### Concerning the Summons, in which Limb of the Soul it descended to the First Man.

*The chapter is overwhelmingly cosmological-correspondential teaching about the First Man as Head and the scattered Living Soul as limbs, with systematic body↔cosmos analogy and a fivefold faculty list. It is embedded in a late Q&A dialogue frame (teachers/enlightener) and contains one citation-like appeal to “scriptures” in the question, but no clear NT/Gospel entry vector. The second-half “drift” here reads more as narrative elaboration than institutional/pastoral graft.*

“Indeed, the First Man dispersed himself and he [...] limb of the living soul [...] of the living soul; he has [...] he did not know [... ...] but the blessed, glorious Man came [in] secret; in his image; in his shape; in his love; in his holy virgin, she who is the virgin of light, the soul of the father.

“After the fashion of this fleshly body: as the root of all the limbs hang upon the head, so that should one of the person's limbs be cut off, while the head exists he has hope for [...] but if his head should be cut off, the entire body will die and he is lost.

“This is also the case for the Firs[t] Ma[n]. He is the head, while his sons attach to it the limbs [of] his soul. Or, conversely, like the living air, on which all flesh entirely lives as it breathes and [...] therein. His head is placed on the body of the Pillar of Glory in the heights of the universe.

“[This i]s also the case [for] the father, the blessed Man; as he is like the head that is upon the body and the air that upon the Pillar is set i[n ...] being made strong by his light virgin, [she] who is his soul that he clothes [...]

“[ ... ] t[h]ose [...]: the mind, thought, insight, counsel and c[on]sideration that he produced and sent forth from hi[m to] do his will. He sprang and travelled behind them. [...] of his living soul, which is entwined among the rebels; as they are like the limbs of its body, and [...] universe.

“And when they were sent, at [that ti]m[e] he was found with the Virgin of [Lig]ht and [he] stood up, asking and entreating for a p[o]wer. [ ... he] gave him peace and a ki[ss ...] he [gave] him goo[d] tidings [...] Man. The Man him[s]elf gave [his ...] his limbs and gathered his soul i[n ...] he built it in its place like this tower [...]

“[...] shaped it and beautified it skillfully [...] for[m ...] the voices that he sent [...] he might sink in and quench [... ...] so[u]l that was crushed by the enemy. They were gathered in. They came, set firm once more, in the image of their father.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/compilation frame: “Once again… questioned the enlightener… He says…” + a question. The clause “it is written in the scriptures” is a citation posture, but not clearly NT-derived here; the whole paragraph functions as dialogue apparatus, not substrate exposition.
- ¶2: Core cosmology (First Man dispersal; Virgin of Light; ‘soul of the father’) is embedded inside a late dialogue attribution. The ‘I have written for you’ gesture breaks the impersonal, system-internal explanatory voice and looks like compilation-era cross-reference.
- ¶3: Impersonal correspondential instruction: body anatomy (head/limbs) as the fixed image for a cosmic dependency relation. No citation, exhortation, or institutional targeting; mapping stays cosmos↔body-image.
- ¶4: System-internal mapping: First Man=head; sons/limbs=soul; living air analogy; Pillar of Glory located ‘in the heights of the universe’. This is classic cosmological correspondential exposition, not application.
- ¶5: Continues the same correspondence chain (head/body/air/Pillar) with the Light-Virgin/soul motif. Despite lacunae, the register remains cosmic and structural.
- ¶6: Fivefold, degree-like faculty list (mind/thought/insight/counsel/consideration) in an impersonal ‘produced and sent forth’ mechanics. The ‘rebels’ remain internal cosmic actors; the paragraph explains process, not ethics.
- ¶7: Mythic-cosmic narrative remains system-internal (Virgin of Light; bestowal of power/peace; gathering of soul; building as ‘tower’). No institutional referents or audience-address pivot.
- ¶8: Cosmic restoration mechanics (voices sent; soul ‘crushed by the enemy’; re-gathering; re-formation ‘in the image of their father’) continue the internal salvation architecture; the one pastoral-density blip likely reflects vocabulary noise rather than an institutional graft.

---

## Chapter 32
### Concerning the Seven Works of the Living Spirit

*The chapter is dominated by a compact, enumerated cosmological teaching: the Living Spirit’s “seven works” (plus an added eschatological work) described in impersonal mythic/architectonic terms (rescuing First Man, constructing zone/ships, appointing powers, etc.). Only the opening dialogue attribution (“Once again the enlightener speaks”) and the final beatitude read as later framing/devotional material rather than the older systematic substrate.*

The Living Spirit has done seven [wo]rks with his strength.

[The fi]rst: He brought the First Man up from the contest, the way a pearl is [brought] up from the sea.

[The second: He spread about the one who had rebelled, he crucified [h]im in every body.

The third: He [tr]ampled, piled up, and pierced the essences of death.

[The f]ourth: He constructed the ships of light.

The fi[f]th: He evoked his five sons, and distributed them about. They [took] possession of the zone, they bore up under all the universe's weight. Also, he evoked three living words. He set [them] upon the th[re]e vessels, another one upon the giant. [Again, he evoked the] summons, that it might be mixed with the five shining men.

⟨p.86⟩ The sixth: When he had constructed the zone he aro[se] and s[e]nt forth a multitude of powers and many [angel]s; so that they would bound the circuit of the z[o]ne on every si[de], un[t]il he finished [co]nstructing the works. And at the end [of] all the [wor]ks he to[ok s]ome of them into his storehouses, and he appointed others at the watch-posts.

The [sev]enth: A[t the] time when the Ambass[ador dis]pl[aye]d his glorious imag[e], the L[iving] Spirit [established] many [go]ds and angels. They [ ... ] Th[ey to]ok hold of it, that the entire building [ ... ] not [ ... / ... ] one another against [the] light, which he had [ ... ]

Also another gr[ea]t and glorious work he will enact at the e[nd] is [the] Last [Statue], which he will bring up to the aeons of [light]; and he [ ... ] in, and [he ta]kes hold, and he smites [ ... ], and [he] builds, and he [ ... ], and he [ ... t]hem.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening formula with the speaker label “the enlightener” is a classic Kephalaia frame layer; the remaining sentence begins the old enumerative teaching topic.
- ¶2: Enumerated work within the cosmic drama (Living Spirit/First Man), using impersonal correspondential simile (pearl/sea) without institutional or NT citation markers.
- ¶3: Continues the numbered mythic sequence; “crucified…in every body” functions here as internal cosmological language (binding a cosmic rebel in bodies), with no Gospel-citation vector.
- ¶4: Mythic-cosmic action verbs in a numbered list; no audience address or institutional application.
- ¶5: Pure cosmological architecture (“ships of light”), matching the old teaching register.
- ¶6: Strong core markers: systematic numeration plus five-/three-fold structuring (five sons; three living words; three vessels), cosmic engineering (zone/universe weight), and internal process language (“summons…mixed”).
- ¶7: Impersonal logistical/cosmic-administrative description (zone circuit, storehouses, watch-posts) that stays within the universe’s architecture; no church/pastoral targets.
- ¶8: Still within the cosmic system (Ambassador, Living Spirit, gods/angels, safeguarding the building against assault on the light). Lacunae prevent finer segmentation but no clear late institutional/NT signals appear.
- ¶9: Eschatological-cosmic closure motif (“Last Statue,” “aeons of light”) remains internal to Manichaean cosmology; no ecclesial application or Gospel citation formula.
- ¶10: Beatitude-style devotional closure (“Blessed is he…”) shifts from impersonal exposition to pious valuation/reward language; functions as a later framing/doxological ending rather than the enumerative substrate.

## Chapter 33
### Concerning the Five Things that he constructed with the hard Bodies of the Rulers

*A short, systematic five-item construction list attributed to the Living Spirit. The only clear later layer is the formulaic dialogue/frame attribution in the opening clause of ¶1; the rest reads as impersonal cosmological-architectural instruction within the mythic system (no institutional application, exhortation, or NT overlay).*

The Living Spirit constructed five great things from veins and skull[s in the] hard [bodies].

The first is the disc that is placed upon [the sh]oulder of the Porter.

The se[con]d are the arches of [the] pillar that is before him.

[The th]ird is the [mou]ld of the fo[r]m for the gehennas.

The fourth are the [seven (?)] pillars that he has erected in the great sea, in [the] seven parts of the universe.

The fifth are the four walls that surround the vessels.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening frame formula (“Once again… speaks”) marks the compiler’s hagiographic wrapper; the remaining sentence is impersonal cosmological exposition about the Living Spirit’s demiurgic construction within the cosmic system.
- ¶2: Numbered, schematic list item (1/5) describing cosmic architecture (disc/Porter) with no address, citation, or institutional reference—fits the old systematic teaching register.
- ¶3: Continues the five-fold construction list in the same impersonal, architectural-cosmological mode; entirely internal to the cosmic scene (pillar/arches).
- ¶4: Third item of the systematic list; remains within cosmological/eschatological mechanics (“gehennas”) rather than moral or institutional application.
- ¶5: Explicit cosmic geography (“great sea,” “seven parts of the universe”) and enumerative structure signal the teaching substrate; no seam into church practice or polemic.
- ¶6: Fifth item concludes the structured construction catalogue; cosmological-architectural description (“walls… vessels”) with no later framing or application voice.

## Chapter 34
### Concerning the Ten Things that the Ambassador began by his Advent

*A compact numbered schematic of ten cosmological operations triggered by the Third Ambassador’s advent. Aside from the single dialogue attribution in ¶1 (hagiographic frame), the chapter stays entirely within the cosmic system (ships, wheels, doors, separation of Night/Day, Sin, abortions, transmigrations) and reads like an old structural list rather than institutional application or NT overlay.*

The Ambassador performed ten works by his advent.

⟨p.87⟩
The first: The Great Builder came forth to build the new aeon.

The second: The ships moved through the heights in the heavens.

The third: The wheels begin to send the life to earth and the upper worlds.

The fourth: [ ... ]

The fifth is the doors of the ships, that they were opened up [ ...

The sixth is the doors too [ ... ... that they were opened to receive the waste.

The seventh is [the Sin], which he had separated from the rulers by cause of the Third Ambassador; it fell upon the earth.

The eighth: Night and day were separated [ ... ... ]

The ninth: The abortions [fell] to the ground.

The tenth: Transmigrations came about in the zone.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The attribution "the enlightener speaks" is a classic Kephalaia frame marker; the remaining clause functions as a schematic heading introducing a cosmological ten-fold list.
- ¶2: Impersonal enumerated cosmological sequencing ("The first...") with named cosmic functionary (Great Builder) acting within the aeonic architecture; no institutional pointing.
- ¶3: Cosmos→cosmos description of the ships’ motion in the heavens; numbered schematic list style characteristic of teaching substrates.
- ¶4: Mechanistic cosmological process language (wheels sending life) within the purification machine; no exhortation/citation.
- ¶5: Though lacunose, it is a continued numbered item in the same cosmological ten-work sequence.
- ¶6: Continues the internal cosmic machinery register (doors of the ships opening); no contemporary/institutional mapping.
- ¶7: Still a functional description within the cosmic system (doors opened to receive waste), consistent with purification mechanics.
- ¶8: Cosmic event narration within the system: a personified entity (Sin) separated from rulers due to the Third Ambassador’s action; no moral exhortation or institutional polemic.
- ¶9: Cosmological ordering (separation of Night/Day) as a stage in the machine; remains entirely within cosmic process language.
- ¶10: Brief schematic notice of a mythic-cosmological consequence (abortions falling), not a pastoral warning; continues the numbered operations.
- ¶11: Completes the ten-fold cosmic sequence with a structural result (transmigrations in the zone), still non-institutional and non-NT.

## Chapter 35
### Concerning the Four Works of the Ambassador

*This chapter is essentially a compact, systematic fourfold exposition (image; doors of firmaments; purification/release of light; manifestation of the light ship and living soul). The only clearly later material is the brief dialogue/frame attribution in ¶1 (“Once again the enlightener speaks:”), which wraps an otherwise core, impersonal cosmological statement.*

At the time when the Ambassador displayed his image, four great works were revealed that are very excellent and outstanding.

The first is his image that he displayed in a glorious image.

The second is all the doors of the firmaments that were opened at that time. They open [...] and shut behind him.

The third is the light and the releasing that was purified by his image. That light is more than the light that he shall [...]

The fourth: He has [made apparent], by the perfection of people, his light ship. He has [...] the living soul that shone forth in perfection and fullness over the worlds of enmity.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening frame formula with a named speaker (“the enlightener”) is characteristic of the Kephalaia’s editorial/hagiographic apparatus; the remaining clause shifts to impersonal, structural cosmological exposition (a numbered set of “four works”).
- ¶2: Numbered, impersonal listing of salvific/cosmic mechanisms (the Ambassador’s image) without audience address, citation, or institutional application.
- ¶3: Continues the systematic fourfold sequence; purely cosmological architecture (“doors of the firmaments”), with no polemical or pastoral extension.
- ¶4: Process-mechanistic, cosmological purification language (“light,” “releasing,” “purified”) anchored to the Ambassador’s image; no institutional referent despite a slight comparative turn.
- ¶5: Completes the fourfold structure with core Manichaean cosmological entities/processes (“light ship,” “living soul,” “worlds of enmity”). The phrasing remains explanatory rather than hortatory; no explicit contemporary-world identification.

## Chapter 36
### Concerning the Wheel that exists in front of the King of Honour

*This chapter is overwhelmingly systematic cosmological exposition about the Wheel before the King of Honour in the seventh firmament, functioning as a binding/diagnostic device over the rulers and powers. Only the opening dialogue attribution (“apostle speaks”) is a later framing tag; the rest maintains an impersonal, structural voice and stays within the cosmic architecture (firmaments, rulers, zone, fetter, seals, shining men). Paragraph 5’s simile (chain/guard) is a generic illustrative comparison and does not clearly anchor to institutional or NT material, though it may be a secondary explanatory gloss.*

⟨p.88⟩
[Agai]n the [...] the wheel that exists in front [of the King of Honour who dwells in] the seventh firmament [...], the root of the rulers above and below in a great and cruel fetter; as the entire will of the great King of Honour is placed in [it]. Also, their entire enmity, which is in the firmaments, is [...] and ensnared in it.

It exists in a blaze of splendour [with] ornamentation and great beauty. Now, there are twelve seals in it. The seal between i[s] every firmament and the rulers who are there [... ...] are in it; for should one of them wish to escape, he will be bound [...]. If he should wish to cause a trick in his bond [... will be] apparent and known in [that] wheel [...]. The spheres and stars and [the] leader of all the powers exist there, and can be known by it, in front of the King of Honour.

The twelf[t]h seal that is in it is its five shining men that are conjoined and enmeshed through the entire zone; because every place where they are cramped, and every place where they have space, is displayed by that wheel. For the root of all the firmaments above and below, and that of their rulers, is ensnared and fixed in it.

The observation [...] is set right at all times opposite the great King o[f Honour ...] so that fear, wh[ich ...] will assume all the powers of mixture [... ...] their root affixed there, so that they cannot make an esc[a]pe from the fetter of their bond.

That wheel rese[m]bles, in this respect, a chain in front of the chief guard, for when [...] in th[ose ...] in the [... cha]in.

This is also the case for all the rulers who are in the firmaments, and every power of the zone: If they should wish to escape, they shall be recognised and revealed by that wheel. [As] the wheel is like a great mirror, for [the di]scrimination of all things [... is] in it.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening contains a formulaic attribution (“Again … the apostle speaks”), but the remaining clause-set is impersonal, cosmological mechanism: wheel–seventh firmament–root of rulers–fetter/ensnaring; no audience address or institutional targeting.
- ¶2: Systematic description of the wheel’s structure (twelve seals) and its cosmic function (detecting escape/trick of rulers; relation to firmaments/spheres/stars) in an expository voice—no citations, no pastoral address.
- ¶3: Numbered/structural cosmology (twelfth seal; five shining men; zone) with functional mechanics (display/ensnare/fix). This is characteristic of the older teaching substrate’s degree-mapping style.
- ¶4: Continues impersonal mechanism: “observation” set opposite the King of Honour; “powers of mixture” constrained; no shift to exhortation or contemporary institutions.
- ¶5: A brief simile (wheel ~ chain/guard) used to clarify the binding function. It does not invoke church structures, polemic ‘today’, or NT citation; however, because it uses a worldly analogy rather than intra-cosmic mapping, it could be a secondary explanatory gloss.
- ¶6: Generalizing conclusion in the same cosmological register (rulers/firmaments/zone) with correspondential analogy (mirror) to describe cognition/discrimination—still internal to the cosmic system and not framed as moral instruction.
