## Chapter 46
### Concerning the Ambassador.

*The chapter is largely an old, impersonal cosmological sequence of foreordination (“before …”) describing predetermined moments in the Light’s plan: Ambassador, ships, Judge, astrological fate, destroyers, and the Builder. It is lightly framed by an introductory exhortation (¶1). One paragraph (¶5) crosses from cosmic architecture into contemporaneous polemic (“sects”), indicating an editorial/institutional application graft rather than substrate teaching.*

Know that before night and day were differentiated in the universe, the Third [Am]bassador was summoned. And afterwards, he was sent to this void and outer waste that he might purify the light, and separate night and day from each other.

⟨p.117⟩ Before the light was released and freed and ascended through this zone, the Father of Life made beautiful for it the ships of light. He constructed them so that it might be purified in them, and be sculpted and fashioned in good order.

[Bef]ore Sin had multiplied and had made a kingdom in the flesh, before the acquitted and the condemn[ed] were set apart from each other, the Judge [w]as sent forth and revealed. His throne was created and placed [i]n the living atmosphere, so that he would be a just Judge, so that there the righteous and the sinners would be set apart and tested and separated from each other.

Again, [befo]re mankind had been engendered, some were named ['rich' and] others 'poor' on earth [ ... ] upon them. ⟨p.118⟩ Before they die, they who will die are marked out by the stars and the signs of the zodiac in the sphere. They are appointed for them; in them are their births. And their root is bound up with their zodiacal signs; and they are compelled by them and brought to an equal judgement in accordance with their deeds and their sins.

Again, before the universe is dissolved, [and the] entire earth, the destroyers were appointed and instructed for its dissolution.

And before the ne[w] aeon was [built and] made be[auti]ful and marked out, [the] Great Builder [w]as summoned, the first architect and the angels who are with him, who would build and make beautiful the new aeon.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic introductory/exhortative voice (“Once again, hear…”) functioning as editorial framing rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmic chronology and function of a named aeonic being (Third Ambassador) within the salvation mechanics; cosmos→cosmos mapping (void/outer waste; separation of night/day).
- ¶3: Systematic salvific mechanism stated as process (release/ascend/purify) with standard Manichaean hypostases (Father of Life; ships of light), without institutional address or citation.
- ¶4: Cosmic adjudication architecture (Judge, throne, living atmosphere) expressed in the same pre-temporal deterministic mode. ‘Sin’ here behaves as an active power (‘multiplied… made a kingdom’), consistent with cosmological personification rather than a later ethical exhortation.
- ¶5: The correspondence leaves the cosmic system and points into a historical-religious landscape (“the sects”); this reads as institutional/polemical application of cosmic Christ-language rather than the older substrate’s self-contained cosmography.
- ¶6: Astrological-deterministic cosmology (stars, zodiac, births, compulsion) stated structurally and impersonally; the register stays within cosmic fate mechanics rather than exhortation or church discipline.
- ¶7: Brief but purely cosmic eschatological mechanism (appointed destroyers for dissolution) in the same ‘before…’ foreordination sequence.
- ¶8: Self-contained cosmological construction motif (new aeon; Great Builder; first architect; angels) characteristic of systematic Manichaean cosmogony, not framed as moral instruction or citation.

---

## Chapter 47
### Concerning the Four great Things

*This chapter is overwhelmingly the systematic cosmological layer: a fourfold division of the ruling-power across cosmic structures (ten firmaments, eight earths, walls/vessels, and the wheel/sphere of the stars), including a mechanical rationale for binding and balancing hostile powers. Only the opening dialogue attribution is hagiographic frame; no NT citation or institutional/pastoral extension appears.*

The wheel of the stars, which is displayed to you, is a great mighty thing! Also, it is one among the four great things; since the whole ruling-power is assigned to four parts.

The first part is the powers who occur in the ten firmaments that exist above the wheel, and the ruling-power that is bound in them, as it is imprisoned within them in the heavens that are above.

The second part is the eight earths that are below; the four that are composite, and the four places of darkness. The second part extends from this earth, upon which mankind walks, towards the underside.

The third part is the walls and the vessels that surround the worlds. They are the four mountains and the three vessels; with what is in them, and the ruling-power that is trapped within them, and the powers who are masters over them.

The fourth part is the sphere of the stars that is appointed with great diligence, as it is ordered in a [ ... m]any [ ... ]; that of the Father of Life, [and that of the Mother of] the living.

⟨p.119⟩ Its [ ... ] very [ ... ] with diligence, because of the great and cruel powers, and the titanic figures, [who are] in it; they being suspended from it. So, all the first-born and the leaders of all the rulers, they who were existing in the three parts, these were garnered. They were collected together, seized, bound and assigned on this wheel. Each was apportioned by these three parts. They were distributed, entwined, and weighted against their companions of the same size.

Still, for its part, the wheel is equal to them in its strength; the powers of the enmity who are bound on it. Indeed, they were suspended on the circling wheel because of their cruelty; so that, due to the wind and the motion in which they go round, they comprehend not the place wherein they are set. For if they had been bound in the heavens, the heavens would not have had the strength to support them. Again, if they had been assigned on the earths, on the fixtures that are below, they also would not have had the strength to support them. Rather, they were suspended from the wheel of the circling stars; so that the power and the light that should come up from below can be received, and given to the heavenly ones above them. Conversely, whatever will fall from above, whether cold or hot, the wheel of the sphere will also take it and cast it out upon the entire earth, upon all the wood.

Behold, the ordinance of the wheel is in this form. For the powers who are entwined on the wheel are like this: a king mastering his kingdom by this mighty legionary force of all the leaders of the kingdom; and with all the armour of the soldiery that is collected together for him; and also with the mass of the property of the kingdom; and the beautiful lustful aspect of the women who are collected together for him; together with [ ... ] property [ ... ] and those cattle [ ... ] that have been distributed about among his retinue. Yet, if you ask about that king, he is matched with all his retinue. His retinue, for its part, is matched against his entire kingdom. Judgement shall come forth from him, vindication and condemnation, to his entire kingdom; by his wisdom, skill and strength.

Now, just as for this king, who is matched against the measure of his entire kingdom by his wisdom, skill and strength; so also is it for the powers who are bound on the sphere as compared to the ruling-power, and the powers who exist in the entire zone. Like the king towards his retinue; so too is it that this totality is gathered to the sphere, to seven leaders, to twelve signs of the zodiac. As all the cities are gathered to the king, again they gather to the leaders of the universe. All that is done in the universe above and below: the battles and the disorders and the captivity and the hunger and the lust and the property; so shall they increase and diminish through these leaders. They are the motivaters of the whole created order; the totality being gathered in to them the way that I have told you. I have opened your eyes!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening formula marks the Kephalaia’s later dialogue-frame layer; the remaining sentence immediately shifts into impersonal structural cosmology (fourfold partition of the ruling-power).
- ¶2: Impersonal, enumerative cosmological partitioning ("first part"; firmaments; bound/imprisoned powers) with no citation or institutional reference.
- ¶3: Degree-structure enumeration (eight earths in two fours) and cosmic geography; "mankind" functions as locative orientation, not pastoral address.
- ¶4: Systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping by numbered sets (four mountains/three vessels) with containment mechanics (trapped within).
- ¶5: Cosmic construction/ordering language tied to named cosmological beings (Father of Life; Mother of the living) without hagiographic or institutional markers.
- ¶6: Mechanistic cosmology (seizing/binding/apportioning/weighting) describing how the system is engineered; no address to church life or external institutions.
- ¶7: Pure explanatory voice giving cosmic-functional reasons (support/strength; circulation; transfer upward/downward) — a hallmark of the older systematic layer.
- ¶8: Extended structural analogy (king ↔ retinue ↔ kingdom) used to explain cosmic ordering; still does not pivot to contemporary institutions or exhortation.
- ¶9: Cosmological determinative schema (seven leaders; twelve zodiac) with universe-wide causation; the closing direct-address flourish does not introduce church discipline or NT citation, so it remains within the teaching exposition.

---

## Chapter 48
### Concerning the Conduits.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly a continuous cosmological-mechanical teaching about three cosmic conduits/roots binding heavenly structures to earthly bodies, governing ascent of Life and descent of waste, and explaining astral ‘authority’ via the Ambassador episode. Later material is limited to dialogue/frame attributions ("Once again…", "the apostle says", disciple entreaties), which are stripped where present. Despite a mild second-half drift signal, there is no clear institutional/church graft; the late layer is mainly hagiographic Q&A packaging.*

There are three conduits that exist through the entire zone, from the abyss to its heights.

The first conduit is the root of all the powers above, they that exist in all the heavens. They are cast off and fettered on the earths below; because the earth below is the ‘stripping off’, the garment and the body of the powers that are above the heavens.

For when the Father of Life constructed the heavenly worlds, he divested them of their bodies, [he] threw them to the earths below.

So, the upper worlds are of the soul and the spirit; but the worlds below are of the body and the carcass [...] ⟨p.121⟩ Now as for this, he [...] the powers of heaven; he sealed it on their bodies and their carcasses that are on the earths, so that when life comes up from the fullness of these earths, it can be entirely drawn up to the heights in the roots of what is heavenly, that are fastened in their bodies. And also, all life can be purified in that place.

However, the waste that will be swept from the purified one who ascends to the heights will be let fall to the ground through the conduits of these conduits, and will be poured down [...] and thrown to the underside.

This is the first conduit, which comes from the powers of the heavens to their bodies and their carcasses that are upon the earth; also, from their bodies and [their] carcasses that are on the earths to the powers of the heavens.

The second conduit is the one that comes from the temples and dwellings and cities that exist in the heavens down to the ground, to the five shapes of tree that grow upon the earth. The life comes up from the trees to the temples and cities. Life too, and the residue of the heavenly ones, comes down to the trees in a conduit.

And the third conduit is all the powers and householders who dwell in all the firmaments, whose roots come down from them to the five worlds of flesh that creep upon the earth, being fixed to one another. Now, the power and the life that shall be gathered in from the worlds of flesh are dispersed among them in various shapes. The powers above shall draw them to the upper side through conduits.

[The was]te too, and the lust and the evil-doing and the anger that will be greater in the powers of [heave]n, shall be poured to the ground through their various conduits. They shall be discharged upon mankind and the other remaining animals. When what is heavenly will wash the waste and the stench and the poison down on the creations of the flesh below, in their turn the creations shall be greater in lust and anger and evil-doing against each other through the action of their fathers who are on high.

Still, look at the wheel of the stars! It has no [root in this earth], but its roots are bound in the totality. The wheel of stars, then, receives life through the power and the life that ascend from the earths through conduits and the firmaments above. It (also) takes life from those conduits that receive up to the firmaments and the heavenly worlds above it, it takes life out from that place.

Look, in that the wheel has no root in this earth, from where did the stars and signs of the zodiac find this authority? They became masters over these five fleshes and five trees. Even though you have told us that the wheel has no root there! For you said to us that every thing, if it can be reduced from the earth, the heavenly powers above draw it through a conduit. Also, we see that every thing that a person will attain, whether it be wealth that comes into his possession or poverty that will accrue to him, or his sickness and health; he attains it through the signs of the zodiac and the star under which he shall be born.

Nevertheless, what I proclaimed to you, namely that the wheel has no root therein, I have recounted to you properly. It is set aright in its position. For when the Living Spirit, the Father of Life, constructed the heavens and the firmaments above, he bound their roots into the earths below; in their carcasses that he had stripped from them. Yet, when he constructed the wheel, [he] did not bind its root into the earths, but he bound the root of the wheel by the sea giant.

And when [the] Ambassador came, he displayed his image and purified and brought the light out from all [the] heavens. Matter, however, which is present in all the rulers, spurted out against the image of the Ambassador. It went up in order to reach it, but reached it not. It dropped back and fell to the [under]side. Now, when it had fallen from above, it separated out into three parts.

The first part fell to the wheel; another part fell to the earth; an[other part fell to the se]a. ⟨p.123⟩ [By means] of the part that fell to the sphere, [the r]oot was bound in the trees and flesh. Therefore, because of this, they received authority and became masters in the five worlds of the flesh and the five worlds of the tree.

Through the nature that came down [upo]n the wheel the root was bound in the trees. Also, by means of the abortions that came to the ground, another root was bound in the fleshes.

Therefore, because of this, the stars and signs of the zodiac received authority over the trees and the fleshes: through the root of sin that fell down; and [the ro]ot of the abortions that were aborted there, they fell to the ground.

However, the[se] other conduits that [the] Living Spirit has bound in the e[a]rths, they that the soul of the elements comes up through to the heavens; those (conduits) are not bound to the wheel. Rather, they are plundered from them by the powers on the wheel, that hang from it.

The first is the one that comes from the firmaments to the earths, and the one that comes from the wheel to the sea giant. That is only one. So, the other root is the one that comes [fr]om [the temples] to the trees. The third is bound by means of the abortions in the f[leshes]; also in the fleshes the fathers of the abortions who are in the heavens [...] are bound and twined, and the [y] that [...]

that you might instruct us [...] about the conduits, the one that is greater than its [partn]er [...] as more life and soul is drawn [...] than its partner.

You have inquired about a [...] and you have asked about this lesson in superiority, great [...] the earth from which much life ascends [...] the Father of Life has drawn them. ⟨p.124⟩ He has fastened them from the powers [...] he has bound them in the earths. (These conduits) are the roots of the ent[ire] ruling-power that the life of the living soul is drawn up through, it coming up from the whole earth, from all the mountains, fields and islands.

After that first great root is the root of the trees, which is bound to the temples and the buildings above. For there also is much great life coming up from the tree and the entire Cross, being drawn up from them to the upper side like a great host.

What, however, is lesser [...] of their conduit [...] he takes up [...] the roots [...] the earths, they [...] the whole earth in all the worlds. Again, these of the trees are present in the whole earth, whether small or great; but the root of mankind [...] is not present in the whole earth, other than only this world of the south. Therefore, even now these fleshes do not make [...] in the entire world of the south [...] there is a small part of them in [...] of the upper side of the world of the south in [...] in number than the [...]

I have instructed you about the roots [...] them, [I] have taught you that they are bound by their [roots ...] with one another. They stir one another, they draw life from one another through the action of the powers above, which stirs the nature that dwells in them all.

Now, when the Matter that is in them will be crushed and despoiled, then that Matter too shall [...] its members and overturn them and [...] each other [...] stir [...] ⟨p.125⟩

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core content is a numbered cosmological structure (three conduits spanning abyss→heights). The introductory speech formula is a later compilation frame.
- ¶2: Impersonal, mechanical cosmology: heaven–earth binding via ‘root/conduit’ and ‘stripping off’ bodies; maps cosmic domains onto bodily ‘garment/body’ correspondences.
- ¶3: Cosmogonic process explanation (construction/divestment/casting down) in the native mythic-cosmological register; no citation or exhortation.
- ¶4: Degree/correspondence pairing (soul/spirit vs body/carcass) plus a purification mechanics of ascent through roots; strongly systematic and internal to the cosmic architecture.
- ¶5: Complements the ascent mechanism with a symmetric ‘waste-descending’ mechanism; still cosmos→cosmos process language.
- ¶6: Definition-style recapitulation of conduit 1 as a two-way binding (above↔below), characteristic of older schematic teaching.
- ¶7: Numbered conduit 2 in a closed cosmological circuit (heavenly cities↔five tree-shapes). ‘Temples/cities’ are explicitly ‘in the heavens’ (cosmic, not institutional).
- ¶8: Numbered conduit 3; structured correspondences (firmaments↔five worlds of flesh) and ascent dynamics; impersonal expository voice.
- ¶9: Still a cosmological ‘downflow’ account (waste/stench/poison as cosmic efflux), not moral exhortation; causal mechanism remains within the system (powers above → creations below).
- ¶10: The content is schematic astral cosmology (wheel/roots/life-flows). The label ‘apostle’ is a later Mani/community framing device per the dialogue apparatus.
- ¶11: Substantive cosmological question about ‘roots’ and astral authority/fate belongs to the teaching discourse, but its presentation is via later dialogue framing.
- ¶12: Core cosmogonic mechanics (binding roots, exception of the wheel, sea giant linkage). The introductory ‘he says to his disciples’ is later frame.
- ¶13: Mythic-cosmological episode used to explain astral/earth linkage; fully internal to Manichaean cosmic beings (Ambassador, Matter, rulers) and processes.
- ¶14: Tri-part descent and deterministic binding of ‘root’ that grants astral authority—mechanistic, correspondential, cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶15: Etiological explanation of two bindings (trees vs fleshes) via ‘nature’ and ‘abortions’; technical cosmological causation.
- ¶16: Continues the causal chain for astral authority. Although moral-sounding terms appear (‘sin’), they function as cosmological ‘roots’/substances in the mechanism.
- ¶17: Clarifies system boundaries (which conduits bind to wheel vs not) and introduces ‘plundering’ as a cosmic interaction; no institutional application.
- ¶18: The Q&A ‘beseech/our master’ is late frame. The remaining content is an internal cosmological enumeration/recap of roots and bindings.
- ¶19: A teaching-structuring question (relative greatness based on amount of Life/soul drawn) embedded in a later petitionary dialogue style.
- ¶20: Core is an impersonal explanation of ‘superiority’ grounded in cosmic hydraulics (amount of life drawn from whole earth). The speech tag is frame.
- ¶21: Ranked schematic (first root, then tree root) with internal cosmic referents (‘temples/buildings above’, ‘Cross’ as cosmic structure), not church application.
- ¶22: Compares spatial distribution of roots (earth-wide vs localized ‘world of the south’) to explain ‘lesser’; despite ‘even now’, it remains cosmographic rather than pastoral exhortation.
- ¶23: Summative systemic statement: mutual stirring/drawing of life across bound roots via powers above; internal causal mechanics.
- ¶24: Eschatological/mechanical outcome stated in cosmic terms; Matter is personified (has ‘members’), consistent with the older mythic-technical register.

## Chapter 49
### Concerning the Wheel and the Conduits

*The chapter is predominantly a compact piece of cosmological-mechanical teaching: why the celestial wheel does not shear or snag the conduits. Only the opening disciple-question is hagiographic frame; the remainder is core explanation using correspondential analogies (ship through water; bird through air).*

The reason that the conduits shall not be cut, for they are not cut, is because they are spiritual. Thus the conduits are like the waters through which ships sail. You shall find the prow that is in position in the front part of the ship divides the waters, casting them back and forth. So, when it parts the waters and that ship opens up the water with its cleaving prow, immediately and without delay the waters mingle with each other again behind the ship; and the path of the ship shall not be discernible in the midst of the waters.

Just as when a bird flies up in the air, for its path is not visible in the air, and in the way of mankind, in the sun within the conduits. They also shall not be tangled up in them, and neither shall you find their path through the air known.

This is also the case with the wheel. It turns through the conduits and the air. The wheel shall not cut the conduits, nor are the conduits themselves entangled in the wheel.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Direct dialogue frame and petition formula: "His disciples say to him" / "our master"; this functions as editorial container for the teaching question rather than the impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Core teaching is an impersonal mechanism explanation staying within cosmic/physical analogy (spiritual conduits ↔ water that closes behind a ship). The only later element is the speech attribution formula.
- ¶3: Continues the same cosmological-mechanical argument via a second analogy (bird through air). No institutional application, citation formula, or exhortative pivot beyond illustrative "you shall find" language.
- ¶4: Concise closure returning from analogy to the cosmological referent (wheel/conduits), in a purely descriptive explanatory voice (how it works), with no frame or pastoral overlay.

---

## Chapter 50
### Concerning these Names: God, Rich One, and Angel; who they are.

*The chapter is largely systematic, cosmological definition: a threefold taxonomy (god/rich one/angel) repeated across three moments (archetypes in the Land of Light; emanations engaged in the cosmic contest; households of major Light-beings). The opening Q&A is a later hagiographic frame. One paragraph (¶9) pivots from internal cosmological genealogy into mission-language (“apostolate… hope and confidence”), suggesting a later rhetorical/application expansion within an otherwise core sequence.*

There are these three archetypes [in the land of light, and there is no measure to] them!

So, the ones who are called 'god', are the gods whom the Father has summoned from himself. He has established them after the likeness of his greatness.

Conversely, these who are named 'rich [ ... ]', are the evocations of the first rich gods of the Father; because, when the Father had summoned (the first), they themselves summoned evocations. They called them 'rich'.

They too, the rich ones, have summoned their evocations. They call them 'angel'. These are the three archetypes who occur in the land of light. There is no measure to apply to them!

Once again, listen! Other persons who are named 'god' are the emanations who have come from the Father; the evocations of the Father whom he summoned forth. They came out to the contest and humiliated the Enmity.

Conversely, the 'rich ones' are the evocations of these first living words. They too, the rich ones, have come and performed and fulfilled the pleasure of the greatness, in the worlds that are above and below.

On the other hand, the ones who are named the 'angels' are the evocations of the rich ones, who had come from the three living words.

Again, they are called 'god', all the gods who belong to the household of the Great Spirit.

Conversely, the ones who are called 'angel', are all the angels who belong to the household of the Beloved of the Lights.

The ones who are called 'rich', are all the rich ones who belong to the household of the glorious Ambassador.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic opening (“Once again the enlightener speaks”) and set-up in audience-facing terms (“people pronounce it with their mouths”) mark editorial framing rather than impersonal system teaching.
- ¶2: Disciples’ request and honorific address (“our master”) are standard dialogue frame apparatus.
- ¶3: Core sentence is an impersonal cosmological thesis (three archetypes in the Land of Light, immeasurable); the prefatory speaker-tag is later frame.
- ¶4: Systematic definitional taxonomy within the cosmic system: Father → summoned beings → likeness/degree language; no citation or institutional pointing.
- ¶5: Pure internal emanational mechanics (summoning/evocation chain) defining a rank-name; remains cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶6: Completes the threefold degree-structure (god→rich→angel) in a closed cosmological register; no institutional application.
- ¶7: Second iteration of the threefold taxonomy applied to the temporal/cosmic drama (“contest” with the Enmity). Although rhetorically voiced (“listen”), it remains entirely within mythic-cosmological process.
- ¶8: Degree-language and functional cosmology (emanations fulfilling Greatness’ pleasure across worlds). No external/communal referent introduced.
- ¶9: Opens as core taxonomy (angel as evocation-level within the three living words), then pivots to later mission-idiom and soteriological encouragement rather than structural exposition.
- ¶10: Core content is a systematic third application (name-category mapped onto a cosmic ‘household’), but prefaced by a later speaker-tag.
- ¶11: Continues the internal triadic mapping across cosmic households; no citation, exhortation, or institutional referent despite the surface ‘application’ connective.
- ¶12: Completes the third triad (Great Spirit / Beloved of the Lights / Ambassador) in a closed cosmological system; definitional and structural voice.

---

## Chapter 51
### Concerning the First Man.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly a compact cosmological-mechanical exposition of the First Man’s descent and the five garments (living fire plus four helpers), explaining wounding, mixture, and the exceptional status of air. The only clear late layer is the brief hagiographic speech-introduction in ¶1. Despite the chapter-level ‘fatigue’ warning, the surviving second-half paragraphs here remain within cosmos→cosmos description (no institutional or NT overlay).*

When the [First] Man came forth against the ene[my ...] ⟨p.127⟩ garment [...] the darkness with the garment of [of] living fire. With that garment he withered the tumescence (?) of the enemy; he brought down i[t]s foolery and its vanity.

Now, this one alone was sufficient for them. [Y]et, si[n]ce the living fire was weakened, because of this he added [to it another fou]r garments: that of the wind, that of the water, that of the light, and that of the a[i]r. And this, (why) has he given the four? He gave them s[o] th[a]t they would be helpe[r]s to t[h]at god, wh[ich i]s the living fire; so that [...] not with [in] the living f[ire], and penetrate it; and [the po]ison and the [enmi]ty cut it off.

While the fire received a bl[ow and] a wound in its body; yet still, [...] all the powers of da[rk]ness because of it [...] It [...] necessity [...] brothers, to cause them to receive a blow and a woun[d to]o. The power of the en/emy mixed in with them. Rath[er, ex]cept [the air]. The darkness did not mix in with it, nor was the burning of the enemy able to penet/rate it.

Never-theless, even the one that remained behind without the wound, which is the air, it stripped off garment and power. It gave it to its [fo]ur brothers in its place; so that by that [ga]rment of the living air they would become strong, and [...] profit, and endure, namely its other four brothers. And it [be]came for them [...] life.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic/dialogue frame (“Once again… speaks”), functioning as editorial container rather than teaching.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological battle description: First Man vs. Enemy, garment-as-element mechanism; no citation, exhortation, or institutional reference.
- ¶3: Systematic five-fold/garment structure (one + four helpers) and causal mechanics (weakening, protection from penetration/poison). Both sides stay inside the cosmological system.
- ¶4: Continues the internal mechanics of wounding and mixture; the ‘except [the air]’ clause is a structural cosmological distinction. The word ‘brothers’ is plausibly intra-cosmic (elements as “brothers”), not an audience address.
- ¶5: Cosmos→cosmos redistribution motif (air empowering the other four garments) with no institutional anchoring; the higher ‘application’ score appears to be a vocabulary artifact rather than a genuine exhortation pivot.

---

## Chapter 52
### Concerning the [ ... ] of the Light

*This chapter is largely an exhortative homily on unity, using Light/Darkness as exempla and then directly addressing the community. Only small fragments preserve an older cosmological claim (unity/order in the Light; division in the Darkness). The extended comparison to "the kingdom of this world" and the second-person imperatives mark later application/pastoral expansion.*

[Now as to what affects the] Light, behold, at the beginning the Darkness was strong against it; [ ... ] no disunity [ ... ] exists in the Light.

[ ... ] how far [ ... the Lig]ht that came forth from [ ... ] in that place in a [ ... ] alone [ ... ] ⟨p.128⟩ the greatness of the Light with one another for ever, and nor did one of them transgress the command and order that his brother had entrusted to him forever.

However, the Darkness, the enemy, was doomed and overcome; because of the arising of disharmony it was divided within itself. Even the kingdom was divided within itself; [and] ev[en] the [powers, who had] left their abyss behind and come up fo[llow-ing] it, were doomed because of the disunity that was present in all of them. The Darkness was destroyed, and its other powers were humiliated,

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a formulaic frame marker identifying the speaker and audience (late compilation frame), but the remaining sentence makes an impersonal cosmological claim about the Light’s undivided nature (cosmos→cosmos), consistent with older teaching tone despite lacunae.
- ¶2: No frame/citation/exhortation; describes intra-Light order and concord as an eternal condition (cosmic polity). Though "command and order" could be moralized diction, the mapping remains internal to the realm of Light (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶3: Begins as cosmological mechanism (Darkness self-divides through disharmony), but pivots to an explicit analogy with "the kingdom of this world" (cosmos→contemporary-world comparison), signaling later applicative expansion.
- ¶4: Continues the kingdom/party-of-associates analogy in general terms ("associates in the m]ystery," glory, rivalry), without systematic cosmological mapping; reads as rhetorical application rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶5: Further develops the same moral-political analogy (condemnation/humiliation of the other kingdom). The paragraph functions as homiletic application rather than describing a fixed cosmic architecture.
- ¶6: Direct second-person exhortation ("you yourselves") about unity to conquer Sin; this is application/pastoral address to the community, not impersonal cosmological teaching.
- ¶7: Second-person warning and imperative tone ("Should you be divided... may it not happen!")—a pastoral consequence statement using Sin as oppressor.
- ¶8: Imperative exhortation ("Neither be divided...") with triumphal promises; fully applicative/community-directed rather than substrate description.

---

## Chapter 53
### Concerning the First Man.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly core cosmological teaching: First Man’s descent, the hunting/snaring of Matter and the rulers, and the devastation of the storehouses, illustrated through correspondential analogies (marsh/net; body/head/limbs). Only the opening dialogue attribution in ¶1 is later frame.*

At the time when the First Man was sent to the war in the first garment of [living fire], which he gave to the first nature that exists [...] in that single garment. [...] he hunted Matter, the thought of death, [...] and all its other powers.

⟨p.130⟩ When the First Man came up from the abyss, [...] the Living Spirit brought the rulers whom he had hunted [...] element, that [...] of [the land of] darkness, that he might construct from them this universe and fashion everything appropriate for the greatness. From that time on, the place of the storehouses of the elements [...] he destroyed and laid waste the [...] all that were in it.

Just like a marsh, of which the fish and birds are snared in a net, and only the waters are left behind and the reeds and the plants that grow in it, this is also the case with the storehouses of death. The [First] Man snared their counsels and [their k]in that were [in them], and the storehouses were left behind in their [dwelli]ng place.

So then, the case of the land of darkness is like this, as is that of the storehouses that were in it.

Just as the body of man has many limbs in it, [the land of darkness] has [storehouses from] its beginning [...] all [the limbs] that hang from the great head of the [body]. A single root is the root of them all, because they all hang from the great head that is on top of the body. Should a limb be cut from the tip of [the]se small [h]eads, the great head that is on top of them [al]l stays in its place the way that it is [...] you find this person alive. Al[l] these sm[all h]eads are firmly set in it [...]

Wh[en] they will cut from them [...] all of them, these little [heads] shall [...] the corpses, because they might see [...] be firmly set [...] in the body, which they joined wi[th it ...]

In exactly this way, it is also the case that the storehouses of darkness are the same, the things from which the [darkness gushed] forth.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core content is impersonal cosmological narration (First Man, living fire garment, hunting Matter/powers). The only clear late element is the formulaic speaker-introduction, typical of Kephalaia frame.
- ¶2: System-level cosmology: First Man, abyss, Living Spirit, rulers/elements from the land of darkness, construction of the universe out of them. No exhortation/citation/institutional pointing.
- ¶3: Correspondential analogy used to describe a cosmic process (storehouses left barren after powers are ‘snared’). The mapping remains descriptive, not applied to contemporary institutions.
- ¶4: Summative connective that closes the preceding analogy within the cosmological register.
- ¶5: Classic substrate-style body↔cosmos correspondence: storehouses/roots/heads described structurally, without moral exhortation or church application. The human-body analogy functions as a natural expression of a cosmic architecture.
- ¶6: Continuation of the ‘head/limbs’ structural analogy; despite lacunae, the voice remains explanatory rather than hortatory.
- ¶7: Explicitly re-applies the analogy back to the cosmological referent (storehouses/source of darkness), keeping the mapping internal to the system.

## Chapter 54
### [Concerning] the Quality of the Garments

*This chapter is overwhelmingly cosmological-architectural teaching (Living Spirit / Father of Life building and ordering the cosmos from the mixture), expressed through sustained correspondential metaphors (architect/carpenter) that remain inside the cosmic system. Only ¶1 contains an explicit dialogue-attribution frame formula; no NT citations or institutional/pastoral grafts appear despite the chapter-level fatigue warning.*

At the time when the F[ir]st [Man ...] he gave the leadership to his five [sons ... the] Living Spirit and the Mother of Life [...] as the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified [...] they were poured out and mixed with each other, the light with the darkness and the darkness with the light.

At that time the Living Spirit called one of his sons, one rich with power. He gave to him the leadership of the garments. Now, he himself [...] of [...] he hung them and set them up [...] of the Father of Life [...] he set up all things [...] he opened [...] ten firmaments [...] the wheel of the stars [...] them and the [...].

⟨p.132⟩ The Living Spirit is like this: Just as [...] and an architect who might build a dwelling for the king, and divide it up into separate houses and [separate ...] everything that is required for beauty [...] and he cleans them. And also the stones [...] them for beauty and {loveliness}, and he arranges them and makes them so that should the king come and inhabit that palace, he might glorify him.

Furthermore, the Father of Life resembles a wise and skilled carpenter [...] before it was worked [...] before it was established and made beautiful and [...] carpenter [...] works in it, but [...] in him in his [...] in his understanding, and he brings the saw and the adze, and cuts it into separate pieces [...] it and he pulls the [...] it with the {plane} and he smooths it. He shall make many [...] wooden implements, arranging them shape by shape; he cuts and produces wooden implements from it, wooden implements of different kinds, (such as) dishes, bowls. This wood, that [...] since previously it had not been worked before it was sealed by [the ski]ll and wisdom of this dextrous carpenter. He cut it up into different sorts of implements useful to his master.

So is it also with the Father of Life. In his wisdom he has [...] in limbs [...] palace [...] in it or in a [...] useful [...] the ships of light [...] all the other works [...] in the [...] so that when the great king comes, he who is the Third Ambassador, who [...] he might dwell in this universe [...]

⟨p.133⟩ ... live. The light, however, that is in it [...] wise, and he takes it up to the land; but the waste and the darkness will be gathered and thrown down to the abyss.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The opening attribution (“the enlightener speaks”) is a classic later compilation frame, while the remaining sentence is impersonal cosmological mechanism (mixture of light/darkness; First Man; Living Spirit; Mother of Life) consistent with the older systematic teaching.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological narration/ordering: the Living Spirit appoints a son over the garments and establishes firmaments and the star-wheel. No audience address, citation formula, or institutional identification; the mapping stays within cosmic architecture.
- ¶3: Sustained correspondential simile (Living Spirit ↔ architect) used to describe cosmic construction; the referents remain archetypal (“king,” “palace”), not contemporary institutions—typical of the teaching substrate’s explanatory style.
- ¶4: Although flagged by vocabulary as ‘pastoral’ (likely due to verbs like “He shall make”), the paragraph is entirely third-person descriptive, extending the cosmic-building metaphor (Father of Life ↔ carpenter) without exhortation or church/institutional targets—best read as core teaching.
- ¶5: Explicit internal cosmological identification (Third Ambassador as “great king”; ships of light; universe as constructed dwelling). This is application of the metaphor back to the cosmic hierarchy, not to a community practice.
- ¶6: Cosmic sorting/eschatological mechanics (light gathered upward; darkness cast to abyss) stated impersonally, with no frame, citation, or pastoral address—consistent with the older mechanistic layer despite fragmentary preservation.

## Chapter 55
### Concerning the Fashioning of Adam

*This chapter is largely cosmological teaching about the Third Ambassador’s displayed Image and the rulers’ imitation that results in Adam and Eve, plus a short doctrinal appendix on other non-sexually-generated figures (sea-giant, sea-monster, vegetation). The main later material is (a) the dialogue/Q&A frame (¶1–2) and (b) the extended worldly parable of the ‘free woman’ (¶5–6) and brief exhortative closure (¶8). No NT citation vectors appear; the key discriminator is cosmos→cosmos exposition versus cosmos→worldly illustration.*

⟨p.133⟩ Regarding the Ambassador, the occasion when he came displaying his image, he [d]id not come so th[a]t he might reveal his image to the ru[l]ers in the universe. Rather, he came and displayed to the universe be[cause of h]is soul and his son, who is [ ... ] every [ ... ], that he might enable him to live [ ... ] and make for him a release; and to free (the soul) from every bond and all the fetters, in which it is entangled and bound.

Still, you, look and see! The rulers and the authorities, in that they had no form in [ ... ] him, they lusted for his image. They reflected upon themselves, that [they] had no [one] like him in their creation. They sealed hi[s im]a[g]e in their heart, within their soul. Afterward[s, they] built it according to his likeness; they formed Adam and [Ev]e. They sealed the image of the exalted one in their [soul]. So, they [co]pied with an imitation; but they have not [copied i]n truth! Rather, just as [ ... ... ] it is made as these, for [i]t came [ ... ] but it is not l[ik]e him in truth. [Again, so too] is this figure of ma[nkind. They copi]ed it against the image of the exalted one [ ... ]

Similarly the [t]ree, for while it is like the [ ... it is] not [copi]ed in truth.

Now, this too is the way of the Third Ambassador, when he came and display[ed his] image with a show towards all the powers. [He did] not [co]me so that all the rulers and the author[it]ies would sculpt a form [after h]is likeness. R[ather], he came because of his son being crucified in the totality; [fo]r he would release him, free him, and rescue him from oppres-sion. Yet, when the rulers looked o[n] him, [th]ey lusted for his image. They fashioned after his [like]ness Adam and Eve, without the consent of the gre[at]ness.

[Understa]nd also this other (matter) that I will teach you. For, [a]s with Adam and Eve, whom the rulers fashioned and [se]aled after the image of the exalted one, so too this [i]s how a manifold divinity and many powers and angels [ ... received] the stamp of that image, which was displayed to them. For, in the majesty of the l[ig]ht and (the image's) love, they glowed with their desire and they were undone. Th[ey] were eager for their lust;

⟨p.136⟩ This is [als]o the case with those powers, when they looked on that majestic and beautiful [imag]e, they () [ ... ] a first image. They received the stamp of th[at image], which was displayed to them. They copied [ ... wh]ich th[ey co]pied out in a likeness, it being made [ ... ... ] the ones that they had put forth, which had received a [stamp of this im]age; but inward even to the rocks [ ... ... ] which occur from place to place. The {} of that image, which was displayed in the universe [ ... ] they received a stamp, it being revealed there, and an impress.

Now, this new birth that they begot in a new [ ... ] in the world, is Adam and Eve. [There is not a] form in their world since the beginning like it; because that [im]age [occ]urs not in the worlds b[el]ow.

Not all the figures that have been fashioned were fashioned by birth from displayed intercourse. Rather, there are other figures fashioned [ ... ]

One of them is the sea [gi]ant, the 'sweeping' from the sphere, which the [Father] of Life discharged into the sea. Its own fire and thought fashioned it [in] a figure.

The second figure is the one [that] fell to the earth. It made the sea its womb. Its own l[us]t became its sculptor. It alone fashioned it to a nature, which is the root of death. Yet, when it came up from the sea, it [ ... ] that it might destroy the works [ ... ] of Life. They immediately sent against it the Adamant of Light, the great 'instruction of vigour'. He humbled it in the districts of the north. He has trampled underfoot and set h[im]self upon it, until the end of the world.

⟨p.137⟩ [The] third figure is the nature that fell on wh[at] is dr[y. It] fashioned the tree and set itself there. [ ... the] new figure that was fashioned [ ... d]isplays.

So, in accord with the essence they [ ... the dark]ness; but yet th[ey] did not come with the King of Dark[ness to the wo]rld. Rather, their fashioning [came] about [ ... ] they are composite and have been displayed in the world.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic Q&A container: ‘disciples questioned… they say to him…’ The paragraph is presented as disciples’ argument, not impersonal system exposition.
- ¶2: Core cosmological mechanism (purpose of the Image-display: release/freeing of the soul) embedded inside a formal speech-attribution frame.
- ¶3: Impersonal cosmological explanation of how the rulers originate Adam/Eve via imitation of the displayed Image; stays within cosmic agents/processes (rulers/authorities/Image/sealing). Minor second-person rhetoric does not introduce institutional application.
- ¶4: Brief correspondential analogy supporting the ‘imitation not truth’ argument; no frame or institutional pointing.
- ¶5: Extended worldly parable (free woman, chamber, city, nobles/servants) used as illustrative application; mapping crosses from cosmic system into social-world staging, characteristic of later explanatory expansion.
- ¶6: Continuation of the same worldly simile, explicitly applying it (‘In accord with this simile…’). Though it names the Ambassador, the paragraph’s vehicle remains a worldly illustrative story rather than system description.
- ¶7: Systematic cosmological rationale (Image-display for rescue/release; rulers’ lust → fashioning Adam/Eve). ‘Crucified in the totality’ functions as Manichaean cosmic process language here, not a Gospel citation.
- ¶8: Direct address/exhortative closure (‘I have enlightened you… commit it to memory’), marking editorial/pedagogical application voice rather than cosmological mechanism.
- ¶9: Core claim: many powers/angels receive the stamp of the displayed Image; later explanatory flourish adds a human lust simile (cosmos→world), indicating secondary illustrative expansion.
- ¶10: Impersonal continuation of the ‘stamp/impress’ mechanism applied across powers and even rocks; remains internal to the cosmological/correspondential system (image → imprint → forms).
- ¶11: Cosmological negative-argument: the Image-form does not occur in the lower worlds; therefore the ‘new birth’ is anomalous. Despite the ‘Now’ connective, it stays in system-description.
- ¶12: General doctrinal proposition about modes of generation of forms (not solely sexual intercourse), setting up a numbered exempla list—typical teaching-core structure.
- ¶13: Mythic-cosmological exemplum with named divine agent (Father of Life) and internal formative faculties (‘fire and thought’)—system exposition.
- ¶14: Cosmic combat/process narrative (lust as sculptor; Adamant of Light sent; north districts; until end of world) remains within the Manichaean cosmic architecture, not institutional application.
- ¶15: Third exemplum completing the triad (sea giant / sea monster / vegetation-tree). Numbered exempla lists are a common core-teaching vehicle.
- ¶16: Concluding cosmological classification: these figures are ‘composite’ and not simply imported with the King of Darkness—system-level ontological sorting, characteristic of the oldest explanatory layer.

## Chapter 56
### [Concerning Saklas and his Powers]

*The chapter preserves a large block of systematic microcosm teaching (senses as “storehouses,” gates/orifices with guards, Light Mind taking the bolts), which reads like an older correspondential substrate. It is wrapped in Kephalaia dialogue attributions (“the enlightener speaks/says”) and ends with clear late application/exhortation. A key substrate→application boundary occurs where the mechanism of sense-control is illustrated with contemporary luxury/temptation imagery (kings’ garments, silken women) and then with explicitly communal/devotional items (psalms, prayers, hymns).*

⟨p.137⟩
All [these a]bortions, amongst them are Saklas and his consort and the ones whom they have served [ ... ], they are the ones that shaped Adam and Eve. How did they find this beautiful image laid over their shape, even though when it was displayed to their fathers they were not in existence? They never saw the image of the Ambassador! And how did they take the seal of the image of the Ambassador? They added it to [the] shape of Adam and Eve.

The Sin that spurted out from the rulers, which is the Matter, shot up [tow]ards the image of the Ambassador. It was cut off from [tha]t place and came down to the earth, for they did not accept [it wi]thin the firmaments. When it came do[wn to] the earth, it formed the tree. It was established within the wood and formed the fruits. And when [the] abortions fell [to] the ground, to the earth, at the [second] time [ ... / ... ]

⟨p.138⟩
[... in the] fruits, it assumed the[m] and spoke in the ruler, their leader. He says to his companions: Come! Give me your light, and I will construct for you an image aft[er] the likeness of the exalted one. What he said, they did: they gave it to him, and he constructed [ ... ].

The Sin that s[purted out ... / ... ] which is the Matter that saw the im[age of the Ambassa]dor and that formed the tre[e and was established i]n [i]t; afterwards it came up in the [fruits ... ]. It went in to the rulers [ ... ] they formed Adam and Eve after the [likeness of the] exalt[ed one]. Through the energy of the Sin that had s[een the image of the] Ambassador, it went into the (rulers) [through the fruits. It] assumed them and they [t]oo sculp[ted ... they] begat each of them according to his destiny [ ... ]; one he resembles, one he does not resemble.

The moulder placed in the form of Adam and Eve limbs, outside and within, for perception and activity. He was apportioned house by house. For everything that his perceptions and elements will receive externally there are internal storehouses and repositories and cavities; and what is received in to them is stored in them. Whenever they will be questioned about what is deposited in their internal storehouses, they bring out what they have received within and give it to the questioner who requested it of them.

In this way his faculty [ ... ] outer [limbs] to look at [ ... ] every type with [in ... ]

⟨p.139⟩
Also [the fa]culty of the eyes [has] houses and cavities and repositories and stores within, so that every image it might see, whether good or evil, whether loveable or detestable or lustful, it can receive into its storehouses and repositories. Also, when the faculty of the eyes is pleased to send out the image that it saw and took in, it can go in to its storehouses [at the] time and think and seek [ ... ] and it brings it out [and] gives it to the questioner who requested it and the one who wan[t]ed it—whether it be something from lu[st ... or a]n image of love [or ... / ... something] hateful. And thus shall that faculty (of the eyes) produce and do what it does in each category.

The faculty of the ears has its own storehouses also. Every sound it might receive, whether good or evil, shall be taken in and placed in its houses and inner repositories, and it is guarded in its [ ... ] for a thousand days. After a thousand days, if someone comes and asks that faculty about the sound that it heard [at] this time and took into its storehouses, immediately it [sh]all g[o] into its repositories and seek and review and search after this word, and send it out from where it was first put, the place in which it was kept.

[In] like order, the faculty of scent [s]hall function just as that of the eyes and that of the auditory organs. Every [o]dour it shall smell it shall take in [to it] and [d]eposit in its inner storehouses. Every time it will be [a]sked by a questioner, it shall go in [ ... ] and [ ... ] storehouse and remember [ ... ] only these things.

⟨p.140⟩
However, even the mouth and the tongue within it, and the taste organ, have a faculty dwelling in them. Again, that faculty too, of taste, has thus cavities and repositories set apart for it. It too receives these tastes and gathers them in. A[nd] at any moment when someone will ask of a taste, if [ ... ] it shall send it out and remember tha[t] taste. It shall snare and give even the mar[k of] th[a]t taste; give its memory to the qu[est]ioner who asks for it.

Again, the fac[ul]ty of touch by the hands is also s[o]: When it might touch, touch shall [receive i]ts memory. And it [takes] it in to its inner repository [until] som[eo]ne will ask this faculty for the memory. [Immediately], it shall go in again and bring out the memory of this touch that it made, and give it to whoever asks for it.

And the faculty of the heart that rules over them all is much the most like this. Every thing that these five faculties will receive and put in store for the faculty of the heart it shall receive and guard. Any time that they will ask for their deposit it shall send out and give every thing that they gave to it.

For the counsel of the heart is like the king. All the ad[v]isers of his city and the leaders of his a[r]my are placed with him. Any time if his genera[ls] will entreat him for whatever they wish, he shall give them the ad[v]ice and opinion by which they move.

Furthermore, it is like a cellarman: [Eve]ry thing that will be entrusted to it by them, and given to it, it shall receive and put in separate vessels and places. Any time [ ... / ... / ... ]

⟨p.141⟩
... in the place that it put it.

This also is the way of the faculty of the body. The vision that the eyes will see and give to it, and it receives it in, it shall bring out again from the storehouses wherein it had placed it; and give it to whoever asks for it.

Again, [the] sound that the ears will hear and give to it, and it receives it from them. At any time when someone will ask it for that sound, it shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked it for that sound. It shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked.

Again, [the] faculty of smell in this same pattern; and that of taste is of the same [ty]pe. The touch by the han[ds ... / ... ] they shall bring it out in to [ ... / ... ] And whenever they will as[k it] for it, it shall bring it out and gi[ve it].

Indeed, watchmen are a[t] these doors guarding them, and bolts are fastened on the doors at the hands of the guards that guard them!

Just like watchmen guarding a camp with towers and watch-posts. As the holding rooms come out from there and the bolts are upon the gates, the guards control the bolts of the gates while they sit at the entrance [of] the watchposts. So, someone who belongs to their city as a fellow [cit]izen of them, belonging to their country, for him they shall open [the ga]te and receive him in by their will. However, the foreigner who comes they shall prevent, and leave him standing at the gate. They shall not yield to let him enter. Again, another one whom they will receive in, with great trouble before [they] take him in, you shall find many holding rooms [ ... / ... ] that [ ... ] there are a mass [ ... / ... ] them. The number of d[oor] bolts [ ... ] are [ ... ] Whenever someone will wish [ ... ] the bolts at his hand, opens the gate and brings out whoever is hidden within.

⟨p.142⟩
This body too is like the mighty camp. And the gates of the camp with their guards are like the orifices and organs of the body. Now, the orifices of the body are of sight, hearing and smell; and they that send out words. There are many sentinels and a mass of guards placed over the limbs of the body, guarding their orifices. And the faculty in it is the directive of the body, the queen of the entire camp; so that whenever it wishes it can open, or when it wishes it can shut.

However, if and wh[en] the Light Mind comes, it shall tu[rn i]n to the orifices of the body. The rulers, who [a]re the door gua[rds], shall h[in]der and restrain it from en[ter]ing. They shall bind the circu[it] of the body so that the foreign[er] can not enter it.

Nevertheless, the Light Mind by its wisdom and awe and diligence shall hum[ble] the guards who are set at the body's orifices. When it finishes humbling them it shall take fr[o]m them bolts to all the orifices of the body. Now, the orifices that had been opened before to the parades of lust, to receive in the sights of pleasure, to transfer the [wor]ds of the practice of li[f]e's concerns, to receive in the t[astes] of the different foods and unclean meals together with the Sin therein; whoever stops receiving th[is] in, his heart and mind shall follow after.

So, now, bec[ause] the b[olt]s to the body of the righteous person are in the hands of the Light M[in]d within, he is open [to receive] in all that is pleasing to God. He is op[en to take] in by his eyes the visions of lov[e ... ] and righteousness.

[S]imply, all orifices in similar fashion were opening at one time to wickedness, but now have opened to the good. Sin was ruling in the body this time, doing anything it liked, having none in command.

Yet, [n]ow, authority has been taken from the Sin in the org[ans] of the body. The Living Spirit has become lord over all its limbs. It has bound them with a chain of peace, and sealed them with the seal of truth. It has opened the orifices of the body to the good, and because of this the good enters them through the ears and the eyes. It dwells in the heart. The H[o]ly Spirit reigns. It does all that it pleases. It annuls [the] will of [s]in, the begin-ning and the end. [In contrast], the will of the [Holly Spir[it] comes about, and the purpose of the Light Mind.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Substantive cosmological question preserved inside a later Q&A container; the “disciples questioned…” formula is a compilation frame marker.
- ¶2: Core-like impersonal cosmological mechanics (Sin/Matter as an agent; descent; formation of tree/fruits), but introduced by a later Kephalaia speech tag.
- ¶3: Cosmological narrative remains internal to the system (rulers, light-seizure, image-making) without exhortation or institutional targeting.
- ¶4: System-internal causal chain (Sin/Matter→tree/fruits→rulers→Adam/Eve image) with degree/destiny differentiation; speech-tag is later.
- ¶5: Concluding didactic seam (“Behold, I have taught you this lesson…”) signals editorial summarizing/transition rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶6: Older microcosm teaching voice (“storehouses…repositories…cavities”) embedded under a repeated Kephalaia speech formula.
- ¶7: Fragmentary continuation of the storehouse model; no frame/exhortation markers in the surviving words.
- ¶8: Systematic, repetitive faculty-by-faculty exposition (impersonal mechanism of intake/storage/recall) characteristic of the substrate.
- ¶9: Regularized procedural teaching (time span, storage, retrieval) with no institutional or citation overlay.
- ¶10: Continuation of the same systematic microcosm mechanism.
- ¶11: Microcosm teaching continues; “mark of that taste” stays within the faculty/storehouse register (correspondential sensory mapping).
- ¶12: Completes the five-sense inventory in the same impersonal, procedural style.
- ¶13: Hierarchical ordering (heart ruling the five) is characteristic of the substrate’s internal architecture of powers.
- ¶14: Correspondential simile (heart→king; senses→army/city officials) remains within the explanatory system, not exhortation.
- ¶15: Second simile (heart/storehouse→cellarman) continues the same internal mapping register.
- ¶16: Fragmentary continuation of the storage/retrieval simile; no editorial voice markers present.
- ¶17: System expands from particular senses to “faculty of the body” as an integrative storehouse—still procedural, impersonal.
- ¶18: Repetitive didactic cadence; no frame/pastoral pivot.
- ¶19: Closes the sensory pattern by recapitulation, typical of older schematic teaching.
- ¶20: A new correspondential image (doors/guards/bolts) begins; introduced by a late speech tag.
- ¶21: Extended simile stays on the system-side (guarding, admitting/denying, hidden contents), preparing the body-orifices mapping.
- ¶22: Classic correspondential microcosm mapping (camp/gates/guards ↔ body/orifices/sentinels) with an internal ‘directive faculty’; speech tag is later.
- ¶23: Cosmic agents (Light Mind; rulers as guards) operate within the body-as-camp system; impersonal process description.
- ¶24: Still largely system-mechanism (Light Mind seizing ‘bolts’), though the vocabulary begins to drift toward moralized ‘lust/unclean meals’—a likely pressure-point toward later application.
- ¶25: Opening retains the internal bolt/orifice mechanism; the remainder shifts into pointed, contemporary moral illustration, consistent with editorial fatigue in the chapter’s latter half.
- ¶26: Institutional/devotional application dominates (psalms, prayers, hymns, charity; sustained ‘now…rather…’ exhortative contrast). This is cosmos→community practice, not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶27: Summary of the internal governance model with personified Sin as ruling power; transitional in tone but still within the body-cosmos mechanism.
- ¶28: Strongly cosmological governance language (Living Spirit; seal/chain; inner rule). The ‘Holy Spirit’ terminology may reflect Christian-influenced naming, but here it functions as an inner ruling cosmic power rather than a NT citation.
- ¶29: Direct address and imperatives (“my brothers…make yourselves secure…be watchful…”) are clear late exhortation/application voice concentrated at the chapter end (editorial fatigue).

## Chapter 57
### Concerning the Generation of Adam.

*The oldest layer here is a compact astrological-cosmological explanation: five time-authorities (year/month/day/hour/moment) installed over the zodiac-sphere, whose changing dominion correlates with the diminishing lifespan/stature of human generations, plus an older-looking biological consequence of “richer light” in the earliest generations (multiple births). The chapter is strongly wrapped in dialogue frame, and the second half shows marked homiletic/application drift (end-times emphasis; moralizing about present humanity), consistent with editorial fatigue.*

Now, no[te] that Adam is mighty in his stature, and great is his size. Even his years are many! He spent many years living in the world. And he is not the only one we have found to be mighty; but all the others who were begotten in his generation, many are their years too. Still, these people who are born today, their stature has been diminished in power; even their years are lessened. Also, why is the birth today, of [the]y who are born, altered compared with that of these first [ ... ] place to place? See, even the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions. Why now have the age and the years of these last ones diminished, and he has also become smaller, compared to these ancient ones who belong to these first generations?

They increased, have turned, and diminished; because there are five types of authorities and leaders appointed in the sphere of zodiacal signs, and the heavens above it. They have names they are ca[lled] by: the first name is the year; the sec[on]d is the month; the third is the day; the fou[rth] is the hour; the fifth is the moment. Now, these five positions, and these five houses, exist in the sphere and the heavens. These places have five powers, and they are made masters over them. The master of the year exists; the master of the month exists; the master of the day exists; the master of the hour exists; the master of the moment exists. In turn one is above his companion; and in turn the one above is made the master over the one below him.

So, mankind is begotten, and [the ani]mals. They are born in these powers. And, thus, these powers have received authority since the beginning of creation till the end of the world. Now, in the beginning of the generations the powers who were masters over the years reigned. Therefore, because of this, the offspring born in their generations and their lifetime: their years were found to be greater. They attain a long life span in the generation of Adam, and [tha]t of Sethel his son, and that of the ones who came after him.

After they ceased to reign, namely the powers who are masters over the years; the powers who are masters over the months themselves reigned. Like the months, for they are less than the years; again this [is] also the case with the offspring w[ho were] born in them. Their life span is less [than the life span of the ones born] in the generation of the powers who master the years!

When the authority of the powers who master the months was completed; the powers of the days received authority after them. Therefore, like the days, for they are less than the months, this also is the case with the offspring who were born in them. Their life span is less than the life span of the ones born in the powers who are masters over the months. Again, even so the offspring who were born in the hours, and of the moments. Their {conduit} is less than that of the months and the days.

For the manner of these first generations is that they are richer in their light and purer in stature. Even their years are greater. They were spending a longer time [ ... ] in [the w]omb of their mother. Afterwards, they [ ... ] a number in a single womb. There are times five were brought forth from a single womb, and times six were brought forth from [a] single womb! There are some lesser than this; again, some greater than this. Indeed, because of this the offspring of Eve multiplied, and of her children. The earth was filled by reason of them alone;

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue-frame setup (“Once again… catechumen… my master”), with question formula and audience-address; functions as hagiographic apparatus rather than impersonal system exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal explanatory voice anchored in cosmic causes (stars/zodiac) rather than institutional exhortation; “today/now” is comparative but still internal to the cosmological-history schema (decline across generations).
- ¶3: Core-like substrate signaled by numbered fivefold structure and impersonal cosmological mechanics (zodiac-sphere/heavens; masters over time-units). Frame material intrudes via attribution/praise formula at the start.
- ¶4: Continuation of the astrological mechanism: births “in these powers” and a reign-sequence explaining lifespan. Despite “end of the world” phrasing, the paragraph remains causal/systematic rather than hortatory.
- ¶5: Iterative, mechanical descent (years → months) correlating a cosmic/time register with embodied lifespan—typical substrate-style process description.
- ¶6: Completes the stepped sequence (months → days → hours/moments) in an impersonal explanatory voice; still cosmos→cosmos causality (time-powers → lifespan).
- ¶7: Homiletic/application turn: rhetorical address (“Do you see”), imminent end-times framing, and present-day decline theme (“more than that of today”)—fits the chapter’s second-half drift flagged by the analysis.
- ¶8: Repetitive dialogue frame (“Once again… catechumen… I entreat you, my master!”) summarizing prior lesson; functions as editorial scaffolding.
- ¶9: Question-only transition prompting the next topic (Adam/Eve reproduction). Even without an explicit attribution, its function is dialogic prompting rather than system exposition.
- ¶10: Underlying teaching explains early human fertility as a function of “richer… light” and bodily purity (cosmic substance → biological consequence), but it is embedded in a dialogic and present-comparative preaching layer characteristic of later compilation.
- ¶11: Moralizing denunciation of present people (wickedness, ugliness, suffering, death) with strong “last generations” rhetoric; not systematic cosmological mapping.
- ¶12: Closing homage/thanks formula (“paid homage and gave glory… I thank you, my master”)—typical hagiographic wrap-up, not part of the substrate.
