## Chapter 58
### The Four Powers that grieve

*The chapter opens with a hagiographic speech frame and closes with institutional/eschatological application (notably pastoral drift in ¶4–5). The oldest layer is concentrated in ¶2–3: impersonal cosmological description of the Mother of Life and the First Man grieving for their children/sons amid the Darkness, ruling-power, and enemy assault. ¶4 shifts to Mani’s church-mission register (holy church, persecution, Light Mind) and ¶5 to Christian eschatological vocabulary (gehenna, repentance).*

⟨p.147⟩ The first who grieves is the first Mother of Life. She is sad because of her children who are set in affliction, for they were conjoined with the Darkness and the [poi]son. They have been bound with the entire ruling-power. For what was she grieving, [wa]tching over them taking these afflictions? Whenever she might [s]ee them and how suffering is brought upon them, she shall grieve and be unhappy on account of them.

⟨p.148⟩ [The s]econd one who is troubled of heart is the First Man, whose [great]ness and kingdom is established in the li[ght] ship [...]. And he too is troubled of heart because of his five sons who are set in the midst of danger. For any time he may see that they are given trouble, always he might hear the sound of their weeping as they cry out because of the blows and wounds of the enemy who assaults them daily; and as he sees them like this constantly, he shall g[ri]eve and mourn on account of his [s]o[n]s.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (“Once again the enlightener speaks to his disciples”) marks the redactional container; the first-person self-identification functions as part of the hagiographic setup rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal, system-internal cosmological description (Mother of Life; her children; Darkness; ruling-power) with no institutional address or citation; grief is explained as a function of cosmic bondage/affliction.
- ¶3: Classic core register: named cosmological figure (First Man) situated in a cosmic locale (“light ship”) and a systematic grouping (“five sons”), describing ongoing Light–Darkness conflict mechanics without exhortation or church-reference.
- ¶4: Shifts from cosmic system to Mani’s institutional present: “holy church,” mission claim (“I chose”), persecution, and the implantation of “Light Mind” in the church—cosmos→community identification typical of editorial/institutional application.
- ¶5: Uses Christian eschatological and penitential vocabulary (“gehenna,” “repentance,” ‘for ever’) and a salvific offer attributed to the speaker; this material most plausibly enters via Manichaean Christian synthesis rather than the pre-compilation cosmological substrate.

---

## Chapter 59
### The Chapter of the Elements that wept

*The chapter is dominated by a systematic fourfold teaching sequence about the garments/elements and their staged weeping within the cosmological drama (First Man, Living Spirit, Last Statue). A clear late drift appears at the end: the discourse pivots from cosmic description to justificatory/hagiographic talk about “apostles…prophets” suffering to save souls, followed by an admonitory maxim—consistent with the strong editorial-fatigue signal.*

The garments, the sons of the First Man, wept bitterly three times. Then they were silent. There is a fourth time of weeping, for they will weep at the end. And then their crying can stop, and they will never again weep from this time on.

Now, the first time of their weeping is when they had seen the Darkness, the enemy being about to burst out against them. They understood everything that would happen to them. They were aware of the one that would rise and come upon them, but by necessity they hastened to him and fought with him. They mastered him, they detained him. They bound him with their body and their limbs, they overcame his death and his fire and his Darkness.

[The] second time that they wept is [when the]ir father, the First Man, [ascend]ed from the [abyss] of Darkness and left them behind; that is the time when he stripped [them off] and left them below. Indeed, when th[ey] s[aw] how the First Man had risen out of the abyss and [l]eft them behind, and that they were abandoned in the enmit[y, th]ey wept and were sore of heart. Still, what could they do, wishing [ ... / ... ] their heart to every affliction. They [remained b]ehind and grew [s]ick under the pressure of the weighty burden of all the works, until the end time.

[The] third time that they wept is when the Living Spirit stripped off the three garments of activity, placing them beneath all things. He gave them a sign to lift their heads and rise to the heights from that place; and to pass and journey through all the earths until they should reach the land of tranquilli[t]y and peace, the place of rest prepared for them. So, when they had seen him, that he had separated himself from them, while they themselves remained behind after him and had not ascended; at that moment when they stayed behind these three garments wept. After their crying, they turned their [hear]t to all the heights that were concealed from them, and were of one mind. For [they would] soar up and come to the heights; and pass over all things until the time that was ordained for them all [ ... ] at the end of the worlds.

[The f]ourth time when they will weep is when the [S]tatue will be taken up on the last day, and they will weep [for] the souls of the liars and blasphemers; for they may give [ ... ] because their limbs have been severed [ ... ] of the Darkness. And also [th]ose souls, when the Statue will go up and th]ey are left alo[n]e, they will weep in that they will rema[in] behind in affliction for ever. For they wi[ll be cut off] and separated from the Last Statue. An[d it is] a necessity to take these souls who are ready for loss as retribution for the deeds that they have done. They go i[n to] this [darkne]ss and are bound with the Darkness; just as they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it. At that very moment, when the Last Statue rises up, the[y wi]ll weep. And then they will scream out loud because they will be severed from the company of this great Statue. And they remain behind for ever. This great weeping [ ... ] is terrible, it occurs in front of the souls and [ ... ] who are ready for loss in accordance with their deeds. And they [will] weep with that crying, they will never be silent; because they will not attain rest from this time on.

So, they too are the garments of these elements. This fourth time of weeping is heart-rending for them. It is much worse for them than these other three times of weeping before. This fourth time the weeping is not for themselves. Rather they weep and are heartsore for these soul[s] who will be cut off from peace, because rest will not come about for them, to allow them to rest from torment for ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame opening (“Once again…to his disciples”), characteristic of the compiling community’s hagiographic apparatus.
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural enumeration (three times + a fourth at the end) describing cosmic process, not addressed to an audience and not applied to institutions.
- ¶3: Cosmic combat description stays wholly inside the system (garments vs. Darkness) in expository voice; no institutional/ethical pivot.
- ¶4: Mythic-cosmological sequence (First Man’s ascent; abandonment in mixture) narrated as necessity within the cosmic economy; no contemporary/institutional identification.
- ¶5: Systemic cosmology of ascent through ‘all the earths’ toward a prepared place of rest; still descriptive/architectural rather than pastoral.
- ¶6: Eschatological mechanics remain within the cosmological system (Statue/Last Statue, separation, binding with Darkness). Despite moral descriptors (‘liars’ etc.), the paragraph is not an institutional exhortation but a cosmic outcome description.
- ¶7: Internal interpretive gloss that still explicates the cosmic schema (garments/elements; final separation/rest) without turning to church practice or audience commands.
- ¶8: Shifts from cosmic description to justificatory mission-hagiography (‘all the apostles and the fathers…true prophets’) and salvation rhetoric (‘second death’, ‘crucified’), reflecting Christian/NT-derived conceptual overlay and late editorial application.
- ¶9: Admonitory maxim addressed to ‘the wise person’ urging attentiveness to the lesson; moral-exhortative closure using ‘apostles’ language rather than continuing the cosmological mechanism.

---

## Chapter 60
### [Concerning the Four Fathers; what they are like.]

*This chapter is overwhelmingly core correspondential teaching: a systematic body→cosmos mapping (hidden/visible organs ↔ hidden/visible divine realities; eyes ↔ light-ships; tongue ↔ Jesus the Splendour). The only clear late layer is the dialogue attribution in ¶1 (frame) and the concluding summarizing flourish in ¶9 (frame-style closure). No NT citation formulae or institutional/pastoral grafts appear.*

Just as the corporeal body has limbs that are hidden, so it also has others that are visible. They can be seen upon it, they can be grasped and receive blows and wounds, whilst others can neither be grasped nor receive blows.

So it is also with this entire greatness, as we resemble it. Some of it is hidden, whilst some of it is visible; some of it is far away from the blows and suffering, whilst some of it receives blows and wounds.

Now, thus the directive of the body that lives within, hidden in the storehouses inside the body and unseen. So it is also how the Father, the God of truth, exists; as he too is hidden in his kingdom, not displayed before this outer desolation.

Thus, too, the body has two eyes that are visible and seen, and are even the body's source of illumination; so there are also these two light ships that shine and are seen in the entire universe and the whole of creation. The light has never been hidden, just like the two eyes of the body.

The living soul too is like this; it is established in apparent silence. In its apparent silence it is grasped and receives blows from these five fleshes, which destroy (the soul) and strike it. It can be likened to the mystery of the corporeal body, as it can be grasped and mastered, can receive blows and wounds.

⟨p.152⟩ Also, thus this tongue that speaks, proclaims, reprimands and reveals about everything happening in this body, inside and outside, also the considerations and bodily knowledges. The tongue, which is outside, makes it manifest. It also proclaims about the honour and shame of the body. And it speaks, unveiling the pain of the body that is sick from its sores.

This is also what Jesus the Splendour is like, who shall be sent from the greatness. He manifests and reveals about everything, both the external and the internal, both what is above and what is below. Be it about the outer aeons that are hidden and not displayed, or about the ships of light that are seen: he is the one who unveils about them. He is also the one who reveals about their glory, and the greatness that dwells in them.

And regarding this beating and wounding of the living soul, Jesus is the one who reveals it. He also preaches about (the soul) and its peace. He reveals about its cleansing and healing.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The teaching itself is impersonal correspondential exposition (body visibility/invisibility as an ontological template). The only late signal is the explicit speaker-attribution frame formula.
- ¶2: Pure cosmos/body analogy continuing the structural mapping (hidden/visible; impassible/passible) without audience exhortation, institutions, or citations.
- ¶3: Correspondential mapping stays within the cosmic system: inner directive faculty ↔ Father hidden in his kingdom; impersonal explanatory voice and cosmological naming (Father, kingdom).
- ¶4: Systematic correspondential equation (eyes ↔ two light ships) with cosmic geography (universe/creation) and no institutional application.
- ¶5: Core mythic mechanics: the living soul as a passible entity struck by the five fleshes; correspondential comparison remains cosmos↔body, not cosmos↔contemporary institutions.
- ¶6: Impersonal functional description of an organ/faculty preparing the next correspondence (tongue ↔ revealer). No frame/citation cues; remains structural.
- ¶7: Jesus the Splendour functions as a named cosmological entity in a systematic role (revealer like the tongue). Despite Christian vocabulary, there is no NT entry vector (no Gospel quotation), so it reads as Manichaean cosmological substrate.
- ¶8: Continues the same cosmic correspondence (revealer making known the soul’s suffering and restoration). The verb "preaches" does not introduce a Gospel citation or ecclesial rule here; it remains within the mythic-cosmological system.
- ¶9: Closing summary in demonstrative/authorial voice ("Behold, I have shown to you...") functions as editorial wrap-up rather than substrate exposition.

---

## Chapter 61
### Concerning the Garment of the Waters: how great is its Measure.

*This chapter is wrapped in a strong hagiographic-historical frame (Shapur, Tigris flood, disciples’ praise). The oldest layer appears mainly in the middle as systematic cosmological explanation of the First Man’s elemental garments and the ‘garment of waters’ being drunk by the rulers of death, with sevenfold/measure language. Several teaching units are embedded in dialogue attributions and occasional first-person/citational phrases (e.g., reference to the ‘Great Gospel’), which look like later compilation-layer delivery rather than the impersonal substrate.*

How great is the power of the garment of the waters? How far shall [it extend]? That it should enter with the roar of its flood and fill this river Tigris in its vastness like the mighty sea! It has carried waves from river bank to bank, and run from wall to wall. The source from which all these waters burst forth, how great will it be? For they come and come each year at their appointed season!

[At] the time when the First Man assumed the elements, and stood [fi]rm against the first enmity that had come about in the darkness, he defied these great and powerful rulers. They are immeasurable, greater than the extent or size of thousands and ten thousands of parasangs. At the start, when he spread the fight against them, he gave them fire and wind; and afterwards he gave the garment of the waters to the rulers of death. So they, the rulers, drank in and consumed those waters. Many [waters] poured forth from the garment of waters [...] the rivers flow. The mouth of the rulers [...] those [...], the warriors of destruction [...] laboured and brought them forth.

There were seven (rivers), like the great river Tigris [that you] see, flowing in to the mouth of each one of them, as they shall not [...] to a single stomach, to their fill; because in turn the descent to its stomach shall burn them with its fire and heat. Those rulers were like the [...] of [...]; they were not emptied; rain and [...] their existent days.

The measure of that river was expanded seven times, as they flow [...] of the body of the [...] waters [...] now.

[...] is how great a struggle, since it has [...] these creatures of destruction, the mighty giants. Behold, they are entwined, bound, and [...] in every place. They are hidden, not revealed before the eyes of every flesh that exists on the land of death [...]. These rulers [...] revealed. The entire universe of flesh will be destroyed and dissolved [...]; all pass away [...]. They will be destroyed with their angry voice, which is mightier than the thunder.

... the measure of the elements of the waters. ... the stature of the Man who bore them. ... how great is that god, active in power, the one whom these waters [...] than the stature of the First Man who [assumed] this garment of such size; and how much was made.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure hagiographic-historical apparatus (apostle before King Shapur); no cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Extended narrative scene-setting about the Tigris flood (time/month/city); functions as story frame, not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶3: Narrative staging with explicit institutional marker ‘leaders of his church’; compilation frame voice.
- ¶4: A substantive cosmological question (“garment of the waters”, measure/extent/source) embedded in a dialogue frame; the question itself is the teaching prompt.
- ¶5: Dialogue delivery and exhortation (‘listen to what I am about to tell you’) rather than impersonal cosmological exposition; functions as transition within the frame.
- ¶6: Core cosmological mechanism (First Man assumes elements; garments used as weapons; rulers of death drinking waters) with a later referential seam to an authored ‘Great Gospel’ and first-person address.
- ¶7: Systematic sevenfold cosmological description (rivers → mouths/stomachs of rulers; internal mechanics of burning/heat). Despite the simile, the mapping remains within the mythic system.
- ¶8: A compact, structural ‘measure expanded seven times’ teaching embedded in explicit dialogue attribution and followed by exhortative amplification (later delivery layer).
- ¶9: Cosmic process/eschatology (binding/hidden rulers; dissolution of the universe of flesh) without institutional targeting; retains mythic-mechanical voice.
- ¶10: Rhetorical comparison anchored to the specific earthly Tigris and first-person delivery (‘of which I have recounted to you’); functions as narrative application within the scene rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶11: Underlying cosmological valuation (measure of elements; stature of First Man) is old-style content, but it is delivered through direct exhortation typical of later pedagogical framing.
- ¶12: Closing reverence/thanks formula addressed to ‘our master’; classic hagiographic frame closure.

---

## Chapter 62
### Concerning the Three Rocks

*This chapter is largely core cosmological teaching (three stabilizing "rocks" set within the worlds), with a brief hagiographic opening (¶1) and a late institutional/application graft in ¶5 that relocates the third rock into "the holy church" (consistent with the strong editorial fatigue signal in the second half).*

Three great rocks, which exist, were appointed by the greatness. They were set in these outer worlds.

So, the first rock is the [Pillar] of Glory, the Perfect Man, which was summoned [by the] glorious Ambassador. He set it in this zone; [ ... he] brought forth its base (?) from the earth to heaven. It bore up under the [en]tire uni[verse], and became first of all the weight-carriers in [it]s powers. In its firmness it has been set upright, and has made fast every [t]hing above and below.

[The] second great and glorious rock is the new earth of light, which was set upon the earth of darkness. It has squashed the fastenings of the five storehouses of death. It has established firmness, with great firmness, over and beyond the earth of darkness. It has flattened, crushed and fixed the root of death.

[The] third rock is the great thought, which was summoned from Jesus, the glorious one.

[These] are the three great rocks, they that have come forth in great confidence, in three great and honoured places. They were appointed and distributed in three great places of these [self]same worlds. They were made fast according to the will of the greatness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame opening (“Once again…to his disciples”), characteristic of the later Kephalaia editorial apparatus.
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural exposition establishing a numbered cosmological triad placed within “these outer worlds,” with no audience address or institutional application.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmological architecture: named beings (Perfect Man, Ambassador), spatial placement (“this zone”), and a function-description of stability/weight-bearing within the universe—classic substrate voice.
- ¶4: Cosmos→cosmos mapping of a stabilizing structure (“new earth of light”) acting upon underworld architecture (“storehouses of death”), with mechanistic verbs and no institutional referents.
- ¶5: Opens in cosmological register (a named cosmic source, “Jesus, the glorious one,” functioning as a being in the system), then pivots to an ecclesial anchoring (“holy church”), indicating later editorial application consistent with chapter’s second-half pastoral drift.
- ¶6: Summative closure in the same impersonal, cosmological voice: reiterates triadic placement “in…worlds” and divine appointment without doxology, citation, or institutional extension.

---

## Chapter 63
### Concerning Love.

*The chapter preserves a short cosmological definition of Love (as the Father of Greatness) and Hate (as Death/Darkness mechanics), with a later ecclesial-Christian expansion: paragraph 3 pivots to the holy church, the apostle, and an explicit Gospel-style citation (Jn 15:13 / Jn 1 noted in apparatus), indicating later overlay. The opening of ¶1 and the attribution in ¶4 are hagiographic frame wrapping otherwise older teaching.*

⟨p.156⟩Love is the Father of Greatness, who dwells in his glorious land. The entirety of the divine has been revealed [there]in! These two are a single living body, the Father and his love; because he has given himself for every thing, as he exists in his aeons. Indeed, due to this, they called the Father, the Lord of the totality, 'love'. For he gave the victory to his aeons and his limbs.

What, conversely, is Hate? Hate is the first Death that welled up. It revealed the land of Darkness. This one called 'hate', because it has ruined all its perdition's offspring; it sinned even against a power foreign to it, and ruined it. Due to this they shall call it 'hated'; for it has caused destruction, both wounding in flesh, and what is not its own.

Today again it causes wounding and destruction in all flesh. It kills them all with diverse deaths; these that are displayed today in every thing, in every type. Even the Cross of Light, the Living Soul, likewise too it ruins and kills them; as it did at the start. Indeed, due to this have they called it 'hated', this one from the hated; the envy from the envy; the Devil from the Devil; the bad that came about from the bad.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again he speaks to his disciples”) introducing a Q&A; this is compiling-community apparatus rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: System-internal identification (Love ↔ Father of Greatness) and aeonic/limb cosmology in an expository voice; no citation formulas or institutional address.
- ¶3: Editorial application to the holy church and the apostle, plus explicit written/citation formula (“So is it written…”) and a Gospel-like saying (“no love greater… give himself to death”), marking NT-mediated Christian overlay and ecclesial extension.
- ¶4: After stripping the speaker-attribution frame, the remaining content is impersonal cosmological mechanics (Hate/Death as primal emergence revealing Darkness), consistent with the older system layer.
- ¶5: Continues the same cosmological agent-mechanics (Hate/Death attacking flesh and the Cross of Light/Living Soul). The repeated “today” reads as mixture-time description rather than institutional exhortation; no church/prescriptive pivot.

---

## Chapter 64
### [Concerning Adam]

*After a brief hagiographic opening, the chapter is dominated by systematic, enumerated cosmological exposition (three preeminences in Adam; seven intentions of the rulers). The mappings remain within the Manichaean cosmic system (image of the Ambassador, five sons, elements, zodiac/time-powers) with no NT citation formulas or institutional/pastoral applications, so the teaching body reads as core substrate material.*

Three great things that are distinct were revealed in Adam the first human. Indeed, due to this, he was found to be better and more outstanding than the conjoined powers that are in heaven and on earth.

The first: The image of the exalted one was placed upon him. The creators and moulders of his body sealed him after the shining form of the image that had been displayed to them above. Indeed, due to this, the image of Adam was found to be surpassingly beautiful beyond any conjoined power above and below.

The second thing: Adam was shaped in his structure through the light of the first-born of heaven and [earth ... ] the five sons. Indeed, due to this, his structure was found to differ from the structure of even the other creations; [sin]ce the form of all creations is different. As for Adam, the formation of his soul fits over the correct distribution of the elements. Therefore, he has intelligence surpassing that of the other creations and beasts.

The third are the teachings and counsels and the seal of all the powers above and below. The creators, who set him in order, gathered them and sealed them in him. Adam and his consort Eve became a dwelling and home for the signs of the zodiac and the stars; and the months, the days and the years. For the seal of the entire universe is stamped upon Adam. Indeed, due to this, heaven and earth moved because of him; trouble and disorder arose on his account between they that are good and they that are evil. So, the good induce him to life because of their image and shape placed upon him; while the [wi]cked were drawing him to death, so that they would have power through him and they would conceive the kingdom, and through him humiliate the entire universe.

Furthermore, understand that the rulers determined and formed Adam so that they would be masters through him and conceive the kingdom because of seven things.

⟨p.158⟩
The first: He became the enlightener in their creation; instead of the light of the image of the Ambassador who had displayed himself to them above. For they all desired the likeness of the image of the Ambassador. Their heart was drawn after it.

The second: So that they would scoop up the light through him and restrain and obstruct it from its ascent to the heights.

The third: So that because of the image of the Ambassador that is placed upon him the entirety of the divine that is above and below would be humiliated; on account of the likeness of the splendour, of the beauty of his superiority. For he is superior in his appearance to all creatures.

The fourth: He is a seal-ring and a new impression, so that all births who will be begotten might be brought forth after his seal.

The fifth: That might be displayed through him all the skil[ls] of the powers, they above and below, secret and manifest; and that they might determine through him the totality, and every thing be revealed in him.

The sixth: That there would come to be a great protection for them through the image of the exalted one that was placed upon him; for as the heavenly would [ ... ] even rulers; and no evil thing would be done to them on account of Adam.

The seventh: That he might become chief and king and master over all creatures; and they might conceive the kingdom through him.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue/frame opener (“Once again… disciples”), characteristic of the compiled Kephalaia hagiographic apparatus.
- ¶2: Impersonal, enumerative exposition introducing a structured teaching (“Three great things…”), with cosmic comparison language rather than address/exhortation or citation.
- ¶3: System-internal cosmological mechanics (creators/moulders; sealing after a revealed image) in numbered sequence; no institutional or contemporary-world targeting.
- ¶4: Correspondential/cosmological mapping (five sons; elements; soul-formation → intelligence) remains wholly within cosmic architecture; no editorial “nowadays/you” application pivot despite a mild explanatory cadence.
- ¶5: Strong core markers: systematic “seal of the universe” doctrine; cosmic time-powers (zodiac, stars, months/days/years) housed in Adam; cosmic conflict mechanics stated as process, not moral exhortation.
- ¶6: Structural transition to a numbered heptad; still impersonal cosmological intent (rulers’ plan) without ecclesial/pastoral targeting.
- ¶7: System-internal mythic logic (Ambassador-image as archetype; rulers’ desire) presented as part of a numbered cosmological rationale; no NT entry-vector markers.
- ¶8: Classic core light/dark mechanics (capture and obstruction of ascent) expressed succinctly within the cosmic system.
- ¶9: Continues the rulers’ sevenfold program with cosmological “image/splendour” logic; remains explanatory rather than hortatory.
- ¶10: Correspondential metaphor (“seal-ring… impression”) functioning as cosmological mechanism for generation; characteristic of core register.
- ¶11: Cosmic totality-language (“powers… above and below… secret and manifest”) in the same heptadic structure; no institutional overlay.
- ¶12: Still within the rulers’ strategy and the operative power of the placed “image”; lacuna preserved; no pastoral turn.
- ¶13: Closes the sevenfold rationale in the same cosmological explanatory voice; functions as completion of a structured teaching unit.

---

## Chapter 65
### Concerning the Sun.

*The oldest layer here is largely a numbered, systematic correspondential teaching: (a) seven benefits of the Sun vs. seven evils of Night, (b) five archetypes in the Sun, and (c) three further Sun-qualities corresponding to the Father of Greatness. Later hands add (i) a hagiographic dialogue setting (“the apostle… among his disciples”), (ii) polemic/application against “sects,” (iii) a scriptural/legal anti-Jewish passage about a “law” forbidding worship of the sun, and (iv) explicit Gospel/NT overlay at the close (Thomas saying; “children of light/day” language) plus concluding blessings/exhortation (editorial fatigue in the second half).*

The first good thing that it does for them is its light, which it shines upon them. It opens the eyes of all mankind. They see underfoot as they walk by it. It takes away the blindness of the night, which made them blind with its darkness.

The second: When (the sun) is come to the world, with its coming it shall bring calm and peace to the entire world. With its coming it shall also take away from them all the fear and trembling, which their heart is filled with the whole night; for it shall take away the darkness present by its light, and sweep it out. Also, this is how it extinguishes fear by its calm.

The third is its vigil; because when (the sun) comes to the world all sleeping mankind shall arise and awake. And the watchmen too shall cease from watching. The guards who sit at the gates of camps, they break vigil at the watchposts, because of its light that has shone upon them in the world.

⟨p.160⟩ The fourth: It shall nourish, and give strength, taste and scent, to the trees and fruits and vegetables, and all the herbs, and the flowers and grasses, which are upon the entire earth.

The fifth: When (the sun) shines in all the world, the evil reptile and the sharp-toothed beasts that are filled of wickedness, they shall flee to hiding in their dens.

The sixth: When it shines its light, the wound of any person is eased from pain. Its light shall melt away the evil arts of every doer of wickedness.

The seventh: (The sun) shall display and manifest to the world the sign of the splendour of the aeons of light, from which it has come out; it here in this world being their sign. It brings light to the whole of creation.

the night, for it has all wickednesses therein. Every occasion, would the night fall, it shall do seven evils in the world.

The first wickedness that the night shall do in the world is the darkness, with which it shall fill the entire world; because when the sun sinks from the world and draws to it its rays, at that instant the shadow of the night shall spread out over the whole world. The eyes of the people fill with blackness. They neither see nor wonder with their eyes, because of the darkness of the night which shall fill the whole world.

The second evil thing that the night shall do is its fear; because when its shadow looms forth, its fear and its trembling shall possess all of mankind. The entire world shall fill of the night.

The third: When the night falls, all creatures take up revolt. They rebel in their heart, to do wickedness and perdition.

The fourth: The heaviness of sleep shall lay upon them, and they fall asleep; they slumber and doze, and they are like these corpses in the night.

The fifth is its ugliness; again, when the night would fall, the form of the people shall be hidden in the darkness and the ugliness of the night. The beauty and the form of men and women shall not be displayed before one another, because of its darkness and its ugliness, which it shall spread out over all their forms.

The evil beasts shall come out, and all the reptiles that are filled with wickedness, from their dens. And they stalk in the night. Also, the demons and fiends find power to do wickedness in the night. Again, the blows and buffets and wounds and discharges shall torment people in the night; because in it the whole of evil shall find strength.

The seventh: The night shall display the sign of the darkness, its father. It has arisen out of its essence; because this night that came out of this first Darkness has been displayed in the world. Note this night, the shadow of this first Darkness, the one that is entwined and bound in all things above and below.

There are five archetypes occur in the sun, it making them [...] as it finds the world.

⟨p.162⟩ The first is its light; for it illumines by its light the world, and all the creatures who exist in it.

The second is its beauty; because when it shines, it shall flood beauty and loveliness upon all creatures and everything.

The third is its peace; for, again, when the sun would shine on the world, all people shall receive affection through it, and give peace to one another.

The fourth is the life of the living soul, which it shall release from all the bonds and fetters of heaven and earth.

The fifth: It gives a strength to the elements; and also it gives a scent and a taste to the entire Cross of the Light.

its light illumines more than all lights that occur in the world. Also, its beauty is more beautiful than all the beauties of mankind. Again, its peace is more surpassing than all the powers and guards who exist in the world. Its releasing action, by which it releases the living soul, is a full day ahead of all releasing actions! The strength that it gives to its limbs is a great strength, being mightier than all strengths!

Once again, there are another three archetypes made apparent by the sun, in respect of the mystery of the first greatness.

The first is the filling up of the disk of its ship; because its ship has filled up every season, and shall not wane at all, the way that the ship of the moon wanes. This continuous filling, by which it is filled, displays the mystery of the Father, the great greatness; from whom all the powers and the gods have come forth. He shall never wane, nor shall lack ever exist in him!

The second: (The sun's) light surpasses the light of all the stars, and all the lights that occur in the universe; corresponding to the mystery of the light of the Father; for his light surpasses and is greater than the light of his aeons.

⟨p.163⟩ The third: This sun is very high above all else. It surpasses the height of all the mountains and hills that exist upon the entire earth, corresponding to the mystery of the Father of Greatness; for he is high above all the household of his light-earth. Also, because the Father himself is high above all the heights and the mountains of his light-earth. He is 'filled up' in his image [all] the time and every hour from eternity to eternity!

The mystery of the night and that of the day, which daily come forth in the world. Now, the mystery of the light and the mystery of the darkness are displayed daily in the created order.

The mystery of the first great light is the sun, which is this second light-giver, the one that comes daily to the world. It displays all the marks of its graces, displaying and signifying on its own behalf that it is good, out of the essence of the good. It came from the good Father and appeared in this world.

In contrast, this night that is made of fear exists after the mystery of the darkness. Behold too the mystery of its essence! It displays it in the world in all these wickednesses. It displays this mystery daily in the world, but the sects know it not in their error. They do not distinguish the mystery of the light from that of the darkness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic scene-setting (“Once again, the apostle… among the congregation of his disciples”).
- ¶2: Depends on external ‘law’/scriptural polemic (OT citation flagged in apparatus); cosmos→contemporary/scriptural-juridical argument rather than impersonal system exposition.
- ¶3: Present-tense application and polemic (“even today”), rhetorical blame of “people” for blindness—application voice rather than structural cosmology.
- ¶4: Rhetorical exhortation (“Shall they not see them?”) introducing a list; functions as homiletic bridge.
- ¶5: Numbered correspondential schema (first of seven), impersonal mechanics of Light vs. night/darkness within the created order.
- ¶6: Second item in a structured heptad; describes a process-mechanism (Light dispels Darkness/fear).
- ¶7: Third item of the heptad; still expository and iterative, continuing the numbered system.
- ¶8: Correspondential register (strength/taste/scent) typical of older mapping style; impersonal description.
- ¶9: Fifth item; Light’s dominance over hostile life-forms presented as a regular cosmic effect.
- ¶10: Sixth item; Light as a dissolver of ‘evil arts’ (magical/demonic efficacy) is expressed as cosmological causation.
- ¶11: Seventh item closes the heptad with explicit sign/correspondence logic: Sun as ‘sign’ of the aeons of Light.
- ¶12: Application polemic against ‘sects of error’ and evaluative rhetoric; not impersonal system description.
- ¶13: Core heptad-setup preserved (night does seven evils), but the paragraph begins with direct address and authorial prompting typical of later application voice.
- ¶14: Numbered counter-heptad mirrors the earlier structure; mechanistic account of the spread of darkness.
- ¶15: Second item of the seven-evils schema; continues the impersonal iterative pattern.
- ¶16: Third item; Darkness-as-influx effect expressed as a general cosmic tendency.
- ¶17: Fourth item; correspondential effect of night (sleep/corpse-likeness) stated as lawlike.
- ¶18: Fifth item; sensory/esthetic register (‘beauty/form/ugliness’) fits the older correspondential style.
- ¶19: Core-like night/Darkness mechanics (beasts, demons, ‘evil finds strength’) are present, but the opening vice list is an application graft.
- ¶20: Closes the counter-heptad with explicit sign/correspondence logic; Darkness is personified as ‘father’ with ‘essence’.
- ¶21: Summative ‘I have taught you’ plus polemic against sects; functions as homiletic application rather than the schematic itself.
- ¶22: A new numbered structure (five archetypes) begins, but is introduced by a redactional dialogue marker that must be stripped from the core.
- ¶23: First of five archetypes; concise, impersonal mapping.
- ¶24: Second archetype; sensory/esthetic correspondential register.
- ¶25: Third archetype; continues list-structure; still framed as an effect-law of the Sun.
- ¶26: Fourth archetype; explicitly Manichaean cosmological mechanic (release of the living soul from bonds).
- ¶27: Fifth archetype; classic correspondential registers (elements; scent/taste) and a named cosmic structure (Cross of the Light).
- ¶28: Primarily an intensifying recapitulation of the five archetypes (still cosmological), but introduced with a second-person exhortative cue.
- ¶29: Introduces a new triad with a clear correspondential target (‘mystery of the first greatness’); impersonal and structural.
- ¶30: Explicit correspondence: Sun-ship fullness ↔ Father’s inexhaustibility; systematic comparison with moon-ship waning.
- ¶31: Second correspondence: Sun’s light superiority ↔ Father’s light surpassing aeons; formal ‘corresponding to’ marker in a cosmological frame.
- ¶32: Third correspondence: height of Sun ↔ transcendence of the Father of Greatness; strongly cosmological (‘light-earth’, eternity-to-eternity).
- ¶33: Contains a succinct core thesis (daily manifestation of the two mysteries), but framed with affectionate address and anti-sect polemic.
- ¶34: Cosmological identification of Sun as ‘second light-giver’ and as sign of the Good/Father; system-internal metaphysics of origin/essence.
- ¶35: Core contrastive cosmology (Night after the mystery of Darkness), though it includes a polemical tag about ‘sects’ that may reflect redactional pressure.
- ¶36: Explicit saviour-saying (Thomas) introduced as authoritative allusion; entered via Gospel tradition rather than the impersonal substrate.
- ¶37: Dialogue attribution plus ‘children of day/light’ idiom strongly keyed to NT language (cf. 1 Thess 5); best explained as Christian overlay.
- ¶38: Concluding beatitude/exhortation with direct address (‘my beloved ones’) and soteriological payoff (‘inherit life eternal’)—late homiletic closure consistent with editorial fatigue.

---

## Chapter 66
### Concerning the Ambassador.

*The chapter contains a substantial correspondential-cosmological exposition (sunrise/sunset as revelations of the First Man’s descent and the Last Statue’s ascent). The opening and closing are hagiographic frame. A strong late drift occurs near the end: an explicit exhortation to preach/repentance (pastoral/application layer) follows a self-contained cosmological teaching sequence, consistent with the editorial-fatigue signal.*

Two great mysteries are apparent in this shining sun, at its rising and setting. Now, at the moment when it rises and is visible in the universe, all creatures of flesh shall lift up their heads fr[om their sleep] towards the splendour of the sun, and open their doors. While at evening time, when the sun sinks from the universe, all people from every place and even all beasts shall go in to their dark hiding places. The population of the entire universe shuts their doors and dark night overcomes them. They go inside and stay within the dark night in their hiding places.

Very great is this mystery revealed by the sun! For when the sun rises and [be]come[s] visible at the beginning of the day all people open their doors, and all of humanity and every creature emerges onto the ear[th] to destroy this Light Cross that is established i[n] silence.

This pertains to the mystery of the First Man, because when he came out from the grea[t]ness he shone forth over all the children of Darkness. At that mome[nt] all the children of Darkness came forth from their storehouses, its powers and its armies, abandoning their caves and their black abyss. They destroyed the [outer] body of his five sons, who were swathed over hi[s b]o[dy, in t]hat first struggle. ⟨p.165⟩

Again, when the sun sinks from the universe and sets, and all people go in to their hiding places and houses and conceal themselves; this also pertains to the mystery of the end, as it presages the consummation of the universe. For, when all the light will be purified and redeemed i[n] the universe at the last, the collector of all things, the Last Statue, will gather in and sculpt it/self. It is the last hour of the day, the time when the Last Statue will go up to the aeo[n of] light. The enemy too, Death, will go in to bondage; to the prison of the souls of the de[ni]iers and blasphemers who loved the darkness. They will go in with it to bondage and the dark night overcomes th[em]; and thenceforth this name 'light' will not shine upon them.

Behold the two mysteries made visible daily in the sun, which is this splendrous light-giver.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame attribution (“Once again he speaks to his disciples…”) with imperative attention marker; typical redactional wrapper rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal correspondential setup using natural cycles (sunrise/sunset; opening/closing; day/night) as ‘mysteries’ without institutional address or citation; stays within cosmos/nature rather than pointing to contemporary practice.
- ¶3: Continues the mystery-language into a cosmological referent (“Light Cross”) and mythic action; still expository rather than exhortative.
- ¶4: Systematic mythic-cosmological explication (First Man; storehouses; powers/armies; five sons) in an impersonal explanatory voice; strong marker of the older teaching substrate.
- ¶5: Eschatological completion of the correspondence (sunset → cosmic end) with internal cosmological agents (Last Statue; Death; bondage/prison mechanics). Though it includes moral labels (“deniers…blasphemers”), it remains description of cosmic sorting rather than institutional instruction.
- ¶6: Concluding summary in the same expository register; closes the two-mystery teaching unit without shifting to church practice.
- ¶7: Direct audience address (“my brothers and loved ones”) + imperatives (train yourselves; preach) + repentance/time urgency: a clear pivot from cosmological exposition to community exhortation (late pastoral/application layer).
- ¶8: Narrativized reaction of disciples (“When his disciples heard…”) with emotive response and quoted plea; typical redactional scene-setting/closure rather than substrate exposition.

---

## Chapter 68
### Concerning Fire.

*A compact five-fold classification of the works of visible fire. Apart from a brief dialogue-attribution incipit, the chapter is continuous systematic teaching: numbered structure, impersonal explanatory voice, and correspondential registers (separation into components; taste/aroma/heat/light) without ecclesial or NT overlay.*

This fire, which burns and is apparent today in the world, it does five works.

The first work that it does is this: It incinerates and burns wood, and everything that shall be placed upon it.

The second: It separates four aspects of the wood, which are these: fire and light, ash and smoke.

The third: It gives a taste and an {aroma} to whatever will be cooked on it. It also gives heat and cheer to whoever will be close to it.

The fourth work that [it] does: All craftsmen shall work the utensils that they will fabricate in it.

The fifth: It shall display light, and the people see by [it].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Mixed because the opening attribution (“he speaks”) is a later hagiographic/frame device; the remaining sentence launches a numbered five-work schema in an impersonal, classificatory mode characteristic of the core.
- ¶2: Core: first item in a numbered five-fold natural/cosmological exposition; descriptive rather than hortatory, with no institutional or citation markers.
- ¶3: Core: systematic ‘separates…four aspects’ analysis (process-mechanics and internal decomposition) typical of the older explanatory layer.
- ¶4: Core: correspondential sensory/affective registers (taste/aroma/heat/cheer) treated as intrinsic effects of the element, not applied to contemporary institutions.
- ¶5: Core: continues the five-work schema; ‘craftsmen/utensils’ remains an impersonal account of fire’s function in the world, not a pastoral address.
- ¶6: Core: closes the five-fold list with a basic light/vision function; no frame/pastoral/NT overlay markers, completing a self-contained classificatory unit.

---

## Chapter 69
### Concerning the Twelve Signs of the Zodiac and the Five Stars

*The chapter is dominated by systematic cosmological-astral teaching: a pentadic derivation of zodiac/planets from the five worlds of Darkness, a four-sided distribution on the sphere, and correspondential effects on creatures. The main later layer is the dialogue/question frame (¶1) and several first-person recap/closure units (¶10–12). A few paragraphs contain compilational discourse markers (“I reveal…”) that can be stripped while preserving a coherent impersonal teaching substrate (¶2, ¶8, ¶13).*

Whether these twelve signs of the zodiac that are fixed on the sphere, or these five stars that circle upon them; those and these are rulers, all of them, according to their essence. They are all enemies and rivals to each other ⟨one oppresses another, they threaten each other⟩. They plunder one another by the action of the great craftsman who has formed all the worlds. He has gathered [them] and bound them on the sphere above, which circles constantly. Now, while it circles and tumbles them, so they shall do everything that they desire, above and below, according to their pleasure. Nevertheless, a guardian is over them, a debt-collector above them; compelling, extorting, and taking what is theirs away from them!

Now, this is how it can rightly be understood. They were [s]eized from the five worlds of darkness, and bound on the sphere. Two zodiacal signs were taken per world. Gemini and Sagittarius belong to the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aries and Leo belong to the world of fire. Taurus, Aquarius and Libra belong to the world of wind. Cancer and Virgo and [P]isces belong to the world of water. Capricorn and Scorpio belong to the world of darkness. These are the twelve rulers of depravity, the ones that wicked[ness] shall not [ ... ] For they cause all the evil and [ ... in the wo]rld, whether in tree or in flesh.

⟨p.168⟩ And understand also about these five stars, the leaders: from where were they formed? The star of Zeus (Jupiter) was generated from the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aphrodite (Venus) came about from the world of fire. And then Ares (Mars) belongs to the world of wind. Hermes (Mercury) belongs to the world of water. Cronos (Saturn) belongs to the world [of] darkness.

And also, the two ascendants are counted to fire and desire, the dry and the moist. They are the father and the mother of all these.

And also, the sun and the moon, which are counted in with them as a rule ⟨[counte]d to their kind, but why did they count them in with them?⟩ so that they would reign over them. You will discover they subjugate them at every moment.

These seven we have named, the five stars and the two ascendants: They are the doers of wickedness that do every wicked and bad act in every land, the whole of it, above and below, in every creature, in the dry and the moist, in tree and flesh.

[They] are distributed, appointed on four sides, three per angle in these four places. And they are fixed to this revolving sphere. Aries, Leo and Sagittarius, they three belong to a single side. In contrast, Taurus, Capricorn and Virgo, these other three belong to another side. And then, Gemini, Libra and Aquarius belong to another side. Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer belong to another corner. Now, they are placed like this, appointed to these four parts, and distributed on the sphere.

So, when the side of Aries, Leo and Sagittarius will be plundered by the guardian who is over it, who extorts from it and the leaders who move upon it; at that instant shall affliction strike all the four-footed creatures below. However, when [the side] of Taurus, Virgo and Capricorn will be plundered; [affliction] shall ⟨p.169⟩ at once befall the herbs, together with the vegetables and all the fruits of the trees. Yet again, when the side of Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer will be plundered; scarcity shall befall the waters upon the earth, and drought be from place to place. Conversely, should the side of Gemini, Libra and Aquarius be plundered; deformity and stuntedness shall befall the form of mankind from place to place.

They are strangers to them. Still, because of the constraint of the arrangement, in that the thing receives light, and as they belong and plunder them; because of this they were counted in with them in relation to the calculation of the number. For, the sun and the moon are from out of the greatness, not belonging to the stars and the signs of the zodiac.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue apparatus: setting (“apostle…among the congregation”), disciple question formula, repeated entreaty; functions as the chapter’s container rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Core astral mechanism (rulers bound to the revolving sphere; guardian/debt-collector constraining them) expressed as system-description; only the initial speaker-attribution is late framing.
- ¶3: Pentadic cosmology mapped onto the zodiac (systematic allocation from the five worlds) with impersonal explanatory voice; stays cosmos→cosmos/creature effects without institutional application.
- ¶4: Straightforward pentadic derivation of the five planets from the five dark worlds; schematic list form typical of the substrate.
- ¶5: Compact degree/correspondence statement (two ascendants ↔ fire/desire ↔ dry/moist ↔ father/mother) within the cosmic system.
- ¶6: Cosmological clarification about the sun and moon’s functional relation to the seven; bracketed aside may reflect a marginal/gloss-like question, but the paragraph remains system-internal rather than institutional.
- ¶7: Summative cosmological claim about the seven’s operative scope; despite moral vocabulary (“wickedness”), it functions as system-description of astral causality.
- ¶8: Core structural distribution (four-sided/three-per-angle schema) with impersonal listing; only the first-person ‘reveal’ header reads like a later teaching-delivery wrapper.
- ¶9: Correspondential causality: astral ‘plundering’ events mapped onto classes of terrestrial effects (animals/plants/waters/humans). This is cosmos→world mechanism, not exhortation or institutional polemic.
- ¶10: First-person recap/closure (“Behold now, I have explained…”) functioning as a didactic seam rather than new cosmological content; likely part of the compiled discourse layer.
- ¶11: Another recap unit (“I have taught you…”) repeating prior content; discourse-level summary rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶12: Recapitulation of the ascendants with first-person teaching marker; repeats ¶5 and reads as editorial/compilational summary.
- ¶13: Core cosmological distinction (sun/moon from ‘the greatness’ vs. astral rulers) with a functional explanation for their ‘counting’ in the number; only the introductory ‘I have revealed’ is late delivery framing.

---

## Chapter 70
### Concerning the Body: It was constructed after the Pattern of the Universe.

*The dominant layer is CORE: systematic microcosm–macrocosm correspondences (body parts ↔ cosmic zones, five watch-stations, four bodily ‘worlds’ with sevens, and zodiac-to-body schemas). A later PASTORAL graft (¶17–24) reuses the cosmological pattern to interpret ethical self-mastery and the ‘elect’ life; this crosses from cosmos→cosmos mapping into cosmos→community/behavior application. The chapter is wrapped and re-started by small FRAME dialogue-attribution formulas (¶1, ¶30, ¶37). No NT/explicit Gospel overlay appears.*

This whole universe, above and below, reflects the pattern of the human body; as the formation of this body of flesh accords to the pattern of the universe.

Its head is like the first-fruits of the five garments. And, from its neck down to the site of its heart, it resembles the pattern of the ten firmaments. And again, the heart accords to the wheel of the rotating sphere. And, from its heart down to its intestines (?), is like this atmosphere that extends from the sphere down to the earth. The [male] part of the body corresponds to this great earth. And also, from its intestines (?) down to its loins is like the three earths. And also, its shinbones to the pattern of the space in which the Porter stands. Its footsoles to the great earth upon which the Porter is stood; and the four fastenings that are under his feet. Its liver to the vessel of fire. Its flesh to the vessel of darkness. Its blood to the vessel of water.

Now, this is how the small body corresponds to the macro-cosmos in its firmaments, in its orderings, in its mountains, its walls and its vessels, as I have made clear to you!

Once more, I reveal to you: There exist five great camps in this great outer zone, of which the five sons of the Living Spirit are masters.

So, in the first watch-station above, of which the Keeper of Splendour is master, on the battlement of all the works, is found his authority over the three heavens. Then the ones below him [ ... ... ] the great King of Honour.

The second watch, of which the great King of Honour is master, his own authority (extends) over the seven firmaments beneath him.

The third watch, of which the Adamant [is master, his authority] reigns from the firmament down below to the [earth]. [And he] invigorates, according to his authority, the sphere and the worlds of the atmosphere; and the other four worlds that are appointed on this earth.

⟨p.171⟩
The fourth watch, over which the King of Glory has power, is the three wheels. And his authority lies over the three earths upon the head of the Porter.

The fifth watch is the watch over which the Porter is authoritative. Again, he is master, according to his authority, over this great earth he stands upon, and the four fastenings under his feet.

Affliction has come about in these five watches from time to time.

So, in the watch of the Keeper of Splendour, Sin desired to spurt up towards the image of the Ambassador. Yet, they barred it from that place, and it fell back down in shame.

Again, an earthquake and treachery occurred in the watch of the great King of Honour, which the watchers who had arisen at the time and had [ ... ] they who were sent came down, until they were humbled.

Also, in the watch of the Adamant, the abortions came to the earth and fashioned [the] figure of flesh.

Again, in the watch of the King of Glory, a commotion happened in these three earths above the Porter. The pathway was obstructed for the ascent of these three wheels; that of wind, that of water, and that of fire.

Also, in the watch of the Porter, the fastenings beneath were laid bare and strained their chains. A great earthquake befell that place.

Once more, understand this other (truth): There exist four worlds in this body of the flesh, and there exist countless times seven rulers in the body of the four worlds!

So, the first world is from its neck up; and also the seven rulers who are in this upper world are these: the two organs of sight, and the two of hearing, and the two of smell. The other one is the mouth, which is the organ of taste.

The second world is from its neck down to its entire upper torso. And also, the seven rulers who are in this second world are: its two arms that accord to the pattern of its two ears, which occur in its first world above; and its two breasts that are to the pattern of its organs of smell; and the two eyes of its heart; and the gullet of the stomach between the two breasts, which in the chest is the pattern of the mouth.

⟨p.173⟩
Once more, there exist seven rulers in the third world of the body, which are these: the fat, the lung, the spleen, the liver, the gall, and its two kidneys.

Also, the fourth world below. There are another seven there of this same kind: its two buttocks (?), and its two testicles, and its two loins; and the member out of which the seed comes, that begets all the forms.

Aries and Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius and Pisces; these are the twelve signs of the zodiac that occur in the worlds above, on the sphere. They are interlocked and bound on this turning wheel, the one in which they are [ ... ], fixed, and planted. And, regarding all these twelve signs of the zodiac, this is how they were appointed and made leaders [ ... ] on the sphere beneath the heaven that is the wheel of the stars. Now, they are appointed like this, they are thus, so that you can find the head of [ ... ] and you find the tail too [ ... ... ]

Again, they occur like this, these that we have recited, one after another in this body. They are counted by order and number from the head to the feet. Its head is Aries. Its neck and its shoulders are Taurus. Its two arms Gemini. Its upper torso is Cancer. Its stomach is Leo. Its belly is Virgo. The vertical spine and its intestines (?) are Libra. Its genitals Scorpio. Its loins are Sagittarius. Its knees are Capricorn. Its shinbones are Aquarius. The soles of its feet are Pisces.

Behold, these are also distributed one after another. They exist in this body, as if turned to the side and bent to the pattern of the twelve signs of the zodiac. They too are thus, arranged one against another, head to tail, as they occur on the wheel [ ... ] up.

Also, they are counted by order and number of the body and all its members. So, we have proclaimed that these are turned to the side, bent, and spread out; because from its head down to its hip shall count six to its left, and another six to its right.

⟨p.174⟩
The six that are down its right are these that I will recount: the right temple is Aries; its right shoulder is Taurus; its right arm is Gemini; its right rib-cage is Cancer; its stomach is Leo; the right side of its reproductive organs are Virgo.

Conversely, the other six about which we have spoken, which are to its left, in contrast they come from its hip to its head: its left [ ... ] is Libra; its left rib-cage is Scorpio; its left breast and left kidney is Sagittarius; its [left] elbow is Capricorn; its left shoulder is Aquarius; its left temple is Pisces. This is how the creator of the body himself has appointed them. He has [set] them in order and arranged them, one against its companion, head to tail.

Listen to this other lesson that I will proclaim to you. Understand that there exist many powers in this body. They are the house-dwellers who are made the leaders in it. There are eight hundred and forty times ten thousand rulers made chiefs in the human body! They are distributed and set firm, quartered according to house. The count and number of their houses is two hundred and ten times ten thousand.

When all these rulers come creeping and moving within the body, they will meet one another; and they shall beset and destroy one another, and [ ... ] in them there. When [ ... t/il]] they shall erupt from the body of the person who [will] die; and make putrid boils and sores and burning wounds in the body. Either they might have made him sick within; or else they might come forth upon his outer side. Those wounds shall first be squeezed and discharge their liquid, and empty out their pus, until they are squeezed [ ... ... ] and the suppuration forced out from the wound. Afterwards, their scars shall close over; and they are healed and obtain relief from the places that came out upon them.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic scene-setting: ‘the apostle … among the church … congregation … says to his disciples’. This is compilation-frame language, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Programmatic correspondential thesis stated impersonally: universe↔body patterning (microcosm/macrocosm) without institutional application.
- ¶3: High-density systematic mapping (body regions/organs ↔ cosmic structures: garments/firmaments/sphere/earths/Porter/vessels). Both sides remain inside the cosmological architecture.
- ¶4: Summative closure of a correspondential schema; minor 2nd-person address does not pivot to institutional discipline and stays within the same cosmic mapping register.
- ¶5: Numbered/structured cosmology (‘five camps’, ‘five sons of the Living Spirit’) in impersonal expository mode—typical core architecture.
- ¶6: Cosmic administration by named beings and ranked ‘watch-station’ structure; lacunae do not change the clearly systematic cosmological layer.
- ¶7: Degree/number structure (second watch; seven firmaments) within the cosmic system.
- ¶8: Cosmos→cosmos exposition with hierarchical ‘authority’ language and world-stratification; no community/application pivot.
- ¶9: Core numbered schema: watch-station ↔ wheels/earths/Porter (internal cosmography).
- ¶10: Continuation of the five-watch structure; purely cosmological correspondences.
- ¶11: Transitional cosmic-historical note introducing conflicts within the watch-stations; still internal to the cosmological narrative.
- ¶12: Mythic conflict within the cosmic system; ‘Sin’ acts as an agent (personified cosmological force), not moral exhortation.
- ¶13: Cosmic disturbance narrative tied to the watch-station hierarchy; no institutional target.
- ¶14: Cosmogonic event-language (generation of ‘figure of flesh’) located within the watch-station system—still core mythic mechanics.
- ¶15: Core: structured cosmos with three wheels (wind/water/fire) and obstructed ascent—mechanistic cosmology.
- ¶16: Completion of the five-watch affliction sequence; still internal to cosmic geography (Porter/fastenings/chains).
- ¶17: Editorial application pivot: ‘this is also the case with this body that the elect wear’ shifts the correspondence from cosmic structure to community identity (‘elect’), using the cosmology to interpret lived religious status.
- ¶18: Moralizing application (‘elect person … guide it towards the good’) aligning self-control with a cosmic ruler; correspondence now points to behavioral discipline.
- ¶19: ‘Whoever will become master…’ ethical interiorization of the cosmic hierarchy; still an application register rather than impersonal cosmography.
- ¶20: Explicit ascetic control of lust/genitals; cosmos used as exemplar (‘mystery of the Adamant… subdues Matter’) in service of discipline—application layer.
- ¶21: Purifying nourishment and mastering inner fire is practical-ethical instruction; cosmic comparison is in service of conduct.
- ¶22: Behavioral exhortation (‘bind it with the chain of peace’) framed through a cosmic analogy; the mapping points outward to practice.
- ¶23: Defines ‘teaching of wisdom … in that body’ by likeness to Virgin of Light—still a spiritualizing application to persons rather than a cosmographic map.
- ¶24: Virtue-list (‘love and joy, faith and truth’) and ‘the person should live’ are classic pastoral/ethical diction; light-ship imagery is applied to personal salvation.
- ¶25: Returns to enumerated anatomical-cosmological schema (four worlds; sevens) in descriptive mode; no ‘elect’/disciplinary targeting.
- ¶26: Systematic listing by number, mapping ‘world’ to sensory organs; correspondence remains structural.
- ¶27: Microcosmic correspondential reasoning (‘accord to the pattern’) with internal analogies; no institutional address.
- ¶28: Pure enumerative schema (seven rulers) tied to organs—typical core catalog style.
- ¶29: Anatomical ‘fourth world’ completes the fourfold structure; ‘begets all the forms’ reads as cosmological-anthropogonic mechanism rather than moral exhortation.
- ¶30: Dialogue-attribution formula (‘the enlightener speaks to his hearers’)—compilation frame marker; not part of the substrate.
- ¶31: Astrological-cosmological catalog of the twelve, located ‘above, on the sphere’ with wheel mechanics; the explanatory aside does not introduce church/ethics.
- ¶32: Classic correspondential body-map (zodiac→body) presented as a numbered ordered schema; fully internal mapping.
- ¶33: Technical description of a second arrangement (‘turned to the side’, ‘head to tail’) matching the zodiac wheel—system description, not exhortation.
- ¶34: Continues the formal counting schema (six left/six right), maintaining impersonal instructional cosmography.
- ¶35: Further elaboration of the second zodiac-body schema; list form is characteristic of the core substrate’s correspondential catalogs.
- ¶36: Completes the left-side sequence and closes with creator-ordering rationale; still an internal system description (appointment/order).
- ¶37: Short speech-transition formula (‘Once more he speaks’)—editorial framing device.
- ¶38: Despite introductory address, the content is a numeric/structural catalog of inner ‘powers’ and ‘houses’ (microcosmic polity), consistent with core enumerative teaching.
- ¶39: Mechanistic microcosm physiology/demonology (rulers moving, erupting as sores) described as process; no institutional exhortation or NT citation.

## Chapter 71
### Concerning the Gathering in of the [E]lements.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly a compact, systematic cosmological sequence: a chain of gathering/translation from element to element and then through the salvific cosmic stations (Answer → Summons → purified Mind → Pillar → First Man → Ambassador → aeons). The only clear later layer is the dialogue attribution in ¶1, which functions as a hagiographic framing seam over an otherwise substantive question.*

In what way shall the elements be gathered in, from one to another?

The light shall be gathered in to the fire, and the fire itself gathered in to the water, and the water gathered in to the wind, and the wind gathered to the air, and the air gathered to the answer, and the answer gathered to the summons. Also, the summons to the purified Mind, which is the intellectual. The intellectual to the Pillar of Glory, [the Pillar of Glory to the First Man, the Man to the Ambassador, the Ambassador to] the aeons of greatness.

They are gathered in like this; but, they will be gathered on one occasion only, and go up above to the place of rest for ever and ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The attribution to “the enlightener” is a classic Kephalaia frame marker; the remaining question is structurally substantive (sets up the ordered cosmological transfer of the elements) and likely preserves the teaching prompt beneath the editorial wrapper.
- ¶2: Impersonal, process/sequence exposition with a stepwise cosmological chain; both sides of each linkage remain within the Maniichaean cosmic system (elements → Answer/Summons → Mind → Pillar → First Man → Ambassador → aeons), with no institutional or exhortative application.
- ¶3: Continues the impersonal eschatological mechanism (one-time final gathering and ascent to rest) without dialogue frame, citation, or pastoral/institutional application; functions as a completion/closure of the cosmological procedure.

---

## Chapter 72
### Concerning the worn and torn apart Garments, or the tattered Clothes, or the Elements, the Cross, and the rest too.

*After a short dialogue frame (¶1–6) the chapter consists of systematic definitional cosmology (¶7–15): a series of internal mappings that equate named “garment” terms with specific light-powers and their stations/functions in the cosmic architecture. No clear institution-pointing seam occurs; the second half remains within cosmos→cosmos exposition despite the chapter-level fatigue warning.*

“It is proper for you to understand this. The garments that are named ‘great garment’ are the five intellectuals that make perfect the body of the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. They were purified by the coming of the Ambassador.

“In contrast, the ones named ‘rag’: the rags are the devoured enlightening power. It is compounded and entangled in the body of the universe in the rulers who are above, who exist in all the firmaments. Also, its shine is that which lights up and is displayed in the stars of the chain and the powers of the course. And likewise, these rags that are entangled in these three earths below, above the head of the Porter [...] on the earth that is under the Porter. These are named the ‘rags’, because [...] set [...]

“However, the ‘great worn and torn apart garments’, which we have proclaimed, is this entangled living power. It is reaped, cut, and devoured. It is overpowered by the five worlds of the flesh, which creep as they move from place to place.

“On the other hand, [these] we have termed the ‘images and the great tattered clothes’ [...] are the three great garments of the wind, the fire, and the water. These the Living Spirit cloaked upon his body. With them he set in order things in the universe. With them also he emptied the vessels. He swept them out and laid them bare in front of the Porter at the foundation of all things.

“Conversely, ‘[the ele]ments’, we have called them this name, are this power that remains in all things below. It rises up in the wombs of all the earths, gathering together, and pouring forth upon all things.

“And then, the ‘[Light] Cross’ [we have termed] this bound ‘light power’. It [...] all [...] upon the earth, in the dry and the moist.

“Also, the one named the ‘counsel of life’ is the summons and the obedience. It moves in all these powers that we have named. It awakens them, gives them ease, and drives them to their movement.

“The one named ‘the soul that is slain’, which is killed, oppressed and murdered; it is this power of fruit, vegetables and seeds. They shall be threshed, plucked and cut down; and they give nourishment to the worlds of the flesh. Further: the wood should it be dry, and the garment should it become ragged, will perish. Again, it is [a pa]rt of this entire soul that is slain and killed; since also even this whole part of it they kill [...]

“For all these names [...] are a single [...] since the beginning, it [...] as it enlightens; but they separated into all these parts in this first contest. They became set in all these altered forms, and these many names. Of course, if now all these varieties are laid bare, and stripped of all these appearances, and parted from all these names; they will gather together and make a single form, and a single name, unaltered and unchangeable for ever in the land of their first essence, from which they were sent forth against the enemy.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic sitting-scene with “apostle” + disciples; pure hagiographic/dialogue apparatus.
- ¶2: A scripted question-list anchored to “written in the scriptures”; functions as editorial setup rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶3: Appeal to “multitude of scriptures” and staged Q&A performance (‘I ask you…tell me’) marks compilation frame, not the substrate.
- ¶4: Dialogue turn marker.
- ¶5: Praise/entreaty to the teacher (‘source of all wisdoms’) is a classic Kephalaia frame voice.
- ¶6: Explicit dialogue attribution (‘Then speaks the apostle’) plus manuscript marker ⟨p.177⟩; must not enter core_text.
- ¶7: Impersonal definitional mapping (term → cosmological structure: ‘great garment’ = five intellectuals of the Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man); internal system vocabulary.
- ¶8: Cosmic localisation of the devoured light-power across firmaments/stars/three earths; correspondence stays within the cosmography (no institutional ‘pointing at’).
- ¶9: Mechanistic description of the light-power’s condition under the ‘five worlds of the flesh’; still system-internal conflict language.
- ¶10: Structured triadic mapping (name → three garments: wind/fire/water) plus mythic operation by the Living Spirit; classic substrate-style functional cosmology.
- ¶11: Definition of ‘elements’ as a remaining power moving through ‘wombs of the earths’; impersonal process description.
- ¶12: Term-definition of ‘Light Cross’ in relation to bound light distributed through dry/moist; correspondence remains cosmic/natural, not institutional.
- ¶13: Functional cosmological agency (‘summons and obedience’) acting within the named powers; systematic explanatory voice.
- ¶14: Definition via cosmic-natural register (vegetation as a station of the light-soul’s suffering) feeding the ‘worlds of the flesh’; no exhortation to a community.
- ¶15: System-level recap (many names/forms → one essence restored) framed as cosmological history and eschatological recomposition; still internal to the mythic architecture.

## Chapter 73
### Concerning the Envy of Matter

*This chapter is dominated by later editorial/application material (notably in the second half: Mani-self reference, church/catechumen categories, exhortation). The oldest recoverable layer is a short cosmological-mythic sequence defining Envy as native to the worlds of darkness and narrating Matter’s envy against key cosmic/human figures (First Man; the Ambassador’s image; Adam). The opening contains a dialogue-frame attribution that must be stripped.*

[The] entirety of envy is this first nature that occu[red] in the worlds of darkness.

In this envy that [ ... ] in it, the First Man [ ... ] its poiso[n ... ] its evil-doing. His garment[s] were [ ... ] it cut them and drew them; [ ... ]

⟨p.179⟩ It envied the first image of the Ambassador, which he displayed to it in the heights abov[e], i[n] his form that it saw. It established the seal and the image of flesh, and put the impression of t[h]at image on flesh, its very own birth.

[The th]ird time it envied Adam, the fir[st hum]an. It seized a power from him, and bore it forth from a [rib of] of Adam's [flesh].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening frame formula signals compilation layer, but the remaining sentence is impersonal cosmological definition (Envy as a primal nature within the darkness-worlds).
- ¶2: Mythic-cosmological narration remains internal to the system (Matter/its envy acting upon the First Man and his garments), with no institutional address, exhortation, or citation formula.
- ¶3: Cosmic actors and mechanisms (Ambassador’s image; sealing/imprinting flesh) are described as process within the mythic architecture; no shift toward church/polemic registers.
- ¶4: Although ‘Adam’ reflects the Manichaean Adam-myth with biblical resonances, the language here functions as internal cosmological anthropology (power seized from Adam’s flesh) rather than a Gospel/NT entry or ecclesial application.
- ¶5: Christian overlay vocabulary and motifs (‘blessed Christ’, ‘father of all the apostles’, ‘wood of the cross’) indicate entry through Mani’s Christian synthesis / later Christianized redaction rather than the older substrate.
- ¶6: Mani self-attribution (‘I myself was sent now, in this last generation’) plus institutional identifiers (‘my church’, ‘assembly’, ‘catechumens’) and direct address (‘it shall envy you’) mark a later applicatory/pastoral layer (also consistent with the chapter’s second-half fatigue drift).
- ¶7: Describes typical persecution dynamics against ‘the teacher’ and ‘the righteous elect person’ (community roles) and social slander among ‘mankind’; this is application to church life rather than cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶8: Explicit temporal anchoring and institutional scope (‘until my time… to my church’) plus faith-fall language indicates editorial application to the community’s experience.
- ¶9: Exhortative ethics for ‘the wise person’ (steadfastness, hope, faith, standing firm in God) is pastoral admonition, not systematic cosmological exposition.
