## Chapter 74
### Concerning the living Fire: It is present in Eight Places.

*This chapter is almost entirely systematic cosmological teaching: an eightfold ‘places wherein it is’ enumeration locating the living fire across Light-land, Father, First Man/Perfect Man, the two light ships, Living Spirit’s body, and the new earth. The only clear later material is the dialogue/frame attribution ‘Once again the apostle speaks’ in ¶1; otherwise the voice stays impersonal and intra-cosmic, with no institutional application and no NT overlay.*

The [holy] living f[ire] is present i[n] eight places.

The first place wherein it is, is this [light] land [where it is present and] exists. It is established beneath [it ... ut]terly beyond the totality. It is the foundation and ba]sis of that great earth, and the consideratio[n th]at is beneath them all.

The second place wherein it is, is the Father, the God of truth; because [the Fathe]r is established in his great, mighty consideration. So, [firs]t in the Father. The other powers that came from him were revealed through him, and (the fire) too came w[i]th them [and was revea]led by them.

The third [place wherein it is], is the First Man, h[e] wh[o ... ... ] armour. Again, it is this fire [ ... ... ] on the body of the Perfect Man.

[The fourth is this fir]e that was purified from all the [light] powers, [which is i]n the outer wall, in the ship of the day.

[The fifth i]s this fire that is in the light-giver of the night [ ... ] within the water.

[The] sixth is this f[i]re that is purified from the totality. The Living [Spirit] swathed it upon his body, over the summons and the obedience. He has established many things by it. He went down and stripped himself of it before the Por[t]er.

The seventh is this living fire that is of the new earth, and is the consideration. For it is the first foundation in the new earth, it they built anew [ ... ... ] the first course, that is a crown for the entire earth. T[hey] are below the totality, over the entire earth of da[rkne]ss [ ... ] in it, in the [ ... ] whole [ ... ]

[The] eigh[th] is the fire that shall be revealed in this [ ... ... ] person, and it signifies fear and war. Furthermore it [ ... ] it displays and manifests its sign in [its ter]ror. It shall be r[ev]ealed in these two light ships.

This i[s the way] that the holy living fire exists in these [eight places].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The colon-introduced thesis fits the chapter’s enumerative teaching; the prefixed speaker-attribution ‘Once again the apostle speaks’ is a classic compiled-frame marker and must be stripped from the core.
- ¶2: Pure eightfold structure (‘first place’) with entirely intra-cosmic locations (light land, beyond the totality) and degree-language (‘foundation…basis…consideration’). No audience address or external institutional referents.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmological placement (‘second place’) within the Father/emanation schema; explanatory mechanics (‘came…were revealed…came with them’) remain within the cosmological system.
- ¶4: Enumerative ‘third place’ locating the element on archetypal cosmic bodies (First Man, Perfect Man). Even with lacunae, the mapping is cosmos→cosmos (armour/body).
- ¶5: Cosmic geography and purification mechanics (‘outer wall’, ‘ship of the day’) continue the intra-cosmic list without editorial application.
- ¶6: Brief but structurally consistent (‘fifth’) and fully cosmological (‘light-giver of the night’, ‘within the water’).
- ¶7: Although more narrative, it remains a cosmological process account (Living Spirit, summons/obedience, Porter) describing what the fire does/where it is in the cosmic economy—no pivot to contemporary practice.
- ¶8: Intra-cosmic architectural correspondences (‘new earth’, ‘foundation’, ‘course’, ‘crown’) and spatial relations (‘below the totality’) mark the impersonal substrate style.
- ¶9: Still within the enumerative cosmology (‘eighth’) and tied back to cosmic vessels (‘two light ships’). ‘Person’ is ambiguous in the lacuna, but no institutional or exhortative anchoring appears.
- ¶10: A summarizing closure to the eightfold schema. It lacks explicit hagiographic/praise formulae; likely part of the teaching unit, though it could also be an editor’s tidy concluding line.

## Chapter 75
### [Concerning the Letter (?)]

*The chapter opens with a brief hagiographic frame, then preserves a compact core teaching (¶2–3) describing the primordial summons and answer between the Living Spirit and the First Man as divinised “letters” containing total foreknowledge of events. From ¶4 onward the pattern is extended to the holy church, good works, and direct exhortation—consistent with the strong editorial-fatigue signal and institutional/application drift.*

Consider the summons, which was sent from the Living Spirit in the beginning. He sent it to the First Man. It is a peace letter and greeting that he wrote and sent to his brother, in which all the tidings are written down, together with everything it will bring about and establish by that summons. That summons, the First Man [... is ordained to happen.

⟨p.183⟩
The answer that was sent from the First Man came to the heights, to the Living Spirit. That is also a letter, which was sent from the First Man to the Living Spirit, in which all the tidings are written down; and every war and the struggles that he had waged. It did [...] in the answer that the First Man [... ...] everything that had happened and everything [that would happen], all that had been done and whatever is made ready to be done by him. The Living Spirit knew all the wars and the dangers and the contests that the rulers had waged against the First Man.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic opening (“Once again he speaks to his disciples…”) functioning as a narrative container, not part of the cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal, systematic cosmological teaching: primordial actors (Living Spirit / First Man) and a structural claim about foreknowledge contained in the “summons,” without institutional address or citation.
- ¶3: Continuation of the primordial letter-pair schema (summons/answer) entirely within the cosmic drama (wars, rulers, heights), maintaining an expository voice; slight application-score presence does not correspond to an actual audience pivot.
- ¶4: Editorial extension of the same “letter” motif into the institutional sphere (“sent to the holy church… as the holy church shall understand”), i.e., cosmos → contemporary church application; high Christian register supports later ecclesial redirection.
- ¶5: Institutional/ethical application: “good works,” fasting, prayers, entreaty, and presentation before Christ—church practice rather than cosmological mechanics.
- ¶6: Direct second-person exhortation (“I say to you… Everything that you will ask…”) marks application voice and pastoral instruction rather than substrate exposition.

## Chapter 79
### Concerning the Fasting of the Saints

*This chapter is dominated by late institutional/pastoral material (fasting practice, saints, weekly observance, alms), with a small embedded cosmological-mechanistic substrate about (a) the soul/light entering via food and (b) the purity-state of the Children of Light. The strong editorial-fatigue signal fits the drift into explicit communal regulation in the final paragraphs.*

This soul that comes into him in the administration of his food, day by day; it shall be made holy, cleansed, purified, and washed from the adulteration of the Darkness that is mixed in with it.

the mystery of [the children] of Light in whom there is neither corruption nor [...] the food, nor wound it. Rather, they are holy, [there is nothing] in them that defiles, as they live in peace.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue/frame attribution (“Once more the enlightener speaks…”) typical of the Kephalaia’s hagiographic/editorial container.
- ¶2: Programmatic statement about fasting ‘the saints’ and its ‘profit’ for four works: an institutional-ethical rubric rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶3: Ascetic exhortation logic (“punish his body by fasting”) and moral-anthropological aim (“subdues… ruling-power”) presented as a disciplinary benefit; not a cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: Underlying core mechanism: a technical soteriological cosmology of ‘soul’ entering through food and being purified from Darkness-mixture (impersonal process language). The surrounding ‘four works’ scheme is pastoral organization.
- ¶5: Core-like descriptive register about the ‘Children of Light’ as a pure order whose being is unaffected by corruption/defilement—cosmic anthropology stated as what they ARE, not what an audience should DO. Embedded within a later fasting-benefits list.
- ¶6: Despite cosmological terms (“the Cross”, “living soul”), the surviving lines are framed as ascetic restraint of hands/actions (ethical practice). The lacunae prevent securely isolating a self-contained older cosmological proposition.
- ¶7: Institutional persistence language (“should they persist… daily… cause the body… members to fast”)—explicit practical discipline; no impersonal cosmological mapping.
- ¶8: Clear community regulation and accommodation (“have not strength… fast on the lord’s day… alms”), consistent with the chapter’s late pastoral drift and institutionalization.

---

## Chapter 82
### The Chapter of Righteous [Judgement]

*This chapter is dominated by later dialogical/pastoral material about anger, reprimand, repentance, and conduct among ‘brothers/sisters’, wrapped in Kephalaia-style Q&A frame. A single brief cosmological-correspondential fragment (¶9) grounds the ethical ‘two aspects’ (love/fear) in the heavenly light-givers’ purificatory function; it stays entirely within the cosmic system and reads like an older teaching insert. An NT paraphrase (Mt 6:21) appears in ¶10, marking Christian overlay there.*

For the light-givers that are in the heavens shall be revealed with these two aspects, in the aspect of love and the aspect of fear. They display these two aspects of the powers above that are in the heavens, because of their soul that passes through the totality. For in these two appearances, the aspect of love that is beauty and sublimity, together with the other aspect of confrontation that is in them, they shall purify in them the powers above.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with formulaic Kephalaia dialogue frame (“Once again… disciple… says to him: I beseech you…”). The question concerns practical dealings with ‘brother or sister’ and ‘judgement… righteousness’, i.e., community ethics rather than cosmological system exposition.
- ¶2: First-person practical resolve about using anger to subdue someone ‘with righteous judgement’; no cosmological mapping, framed as community discipline/application rather than impersonal system teaching.
- ¶3: Q&A petition formula (“I beseech you, my master, that you may instruct me… whether there is sin…”) typical of the editorial dialogue apparatus; content is moral casuistry, not cosmic structure.
- ¶4: Contains explicit dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle speaks…”) and proceeds with disciplinary counsel about ‘repentance’, ‘rebuke’, ‘censure’, ‘upbraid’—institutional/ethical application rather than cosmos→cosmos correspondential description.
- ¶5: Extended ethical taxonomy of ‘evil anger’ (pride, abuse, cursing, retaliation) and interpersonal conflict among ‘brothers’; lacks impersonal cosmological mechanics and functions as moral instruction.
- ¶6: Community disciplinary guidance (“strong reprimand that leads to repentance”); no cosmic register. Reads as institutional pastoral teaching.
- ¶7: Homiletic simile of the wise doctor using hot/cool remedies. This is illustrative rhetoric supporting ethical instruction, not a systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping characteristic of the oldest substrate.
- ¶8: Applies the doctor simile directly to speaking with people in ‘two aspects’ (love/fear) to overcome ‘obduracy’ and bring repentance; remains practical/disciplinary and audience-oriented.
- ¶9: Impersonal cosmological-correspondential statement: heavenly ‘light-givers’ manifest two aspects (love/fear) and purify ‘the powers above’ due to ‘their soul that passes through the totality’. Both sides of the mapping remain within the cosmic system (no contemporary institutions/audience address), marking it as an older teaching fragment embedded in a later ethical discourse.
- ¶10: Direct exhortation (“you too”) culminating in an NT/Gospel paraphrase (“wherever your heart… your treasure…” ~ Mt 6:21). Because this language enters via the Gospel tradition, the paragraph is classified as Christian overlay.
- ¶11: Closing reaction formula (“When that teacher heard… he said… my master…”) typical of Kephalaia hagiographic/dialogue framing rather than core teaching.

---

## Chapter 83
### Concerning the Man who is ugly in his Body, [but] beautiful [in his Soul].

*The chapter opens with a hagiographic congregation scene and a moralizing address that culminates in a Gospel-derived saying (overlay). The oldest recoverable substrate concentrates in the impersonal, process-style teaching on pearl formation (¶9–13) and the cosmological return/rest of freed souls in the aeons (¶17). The explicit identification of the image with “the holy church” and “apostles” is an editorial application graft (¶15–16), followed by exhortation (¶18).*

“Pearls shall arise not in every place in the sea, nor be formed in the sea as a whole. Rather, in various places that are in this sea, pearls are formed in them [ ... ... ] that sea, in which the [pearls] shall be formed [ ... ] this [ ... ... ] what the sea shall [ ... ... ] its fire {blazes} above and comes [down ... ... ] and it makes foam like the drop of water that flows [ ... ] down in rainwater [ ... ] is the water [ ... ... d]own first [ ... ... ] foam and comes down [ ... ... ] the sweet waters [ ... ... ] the waters.

“This drop of water shall [ ... ] down to the sweet waters and [ ... ] and they absorbed them and were combined with the [ ... ]. They did not [d]escend to the depths of the sea, but they [ ... ... ] it floated on the surface of the waters [ ... ... ] to it. The foam and the pearl-shell shall be formed [ ... ] this wholesome drop [ ... ... ] it, and it becomes a pe[arl ... ] that makes a drop of rainwater [ ... ] waters [ ... ] it not being whole. It breaks and separates out into many droplets, and it has time to become a drop of sweet water [ ... ] and comes up in the sea of [ ... ] rain and sweet water; and it is accomodated in the shell, which at first is foam. They shall be joined with each other at [this] time, and are shaped and become a great pea[r]l, a great and valued kind.

“When, however, a drop of rainwater falls, and that drop breaks into many droplets and various {particles of water}, they shall be formed into and be confined in [n]umerous pearls, in the shell and the pearl-shell. One might form two pearls, another may form three, others may form five; some mould more than these, some fewer.

“Now, when you might [find a] whole drop, and the shell receives it, it shall become a great and valued [pe]arl as its worth is perfected. [However], if these two droplets will have time (to adhere) to one another before any (water particle} escapes, and they mix with e[ach o]ther, and the shell [ ... ] before they break into [ ... ] within [ ... ] in a great kind [ ... ... ] the drop of rain, which [ ... ... ] another one, that [ ... ... ] the [w]aters form them in [ ... and] in a great, valued commodity.

“Behold, [I, I have] taught you how sea-pearls shall be formed. I have told you that as a pearl shall come into existence by means of rainwater that has [ti]me to become foam, the pearl-shell shall come into existence by means of the foam, and the foam itself comes into being by means of the transformation and the [ ... ] of the sea.

“Then immediately at the time when [ ... ... ] the pearl divers know it, they shall [ ... ] and they [ ... d]own to those places and they bring pearls up from the depths of the sea, and each pearl diver finds according to the fortune that is [ordai]ned for him. The pearl divers shall [gi]ve them to the traders, and the traders give them to the kings and the nobles.

“Fo[r a]ll the souls that ascend in the flesh of mankind and are freed shall be brought back to the great aeons of light. A place of rest comes about for them, at that place in the aeons of greatness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Scene-setting narrative with “the apostle” in a public gathering with teachers/elders/leaders; this is hagiographic container language, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Institutional identity markers (“one of the elect”, “commandments”) and virtue-description (“holy righteousness”, “truthfulness”) anchor this in community discipline/edification rather than substrate cosmology.
- ¶3: Narrative development (homage, mocking crowd) in a story mode; no systematic cosmological mapping—functions as hagiographic exemplum.
- ¶4: Hagiographic portrayal of the revered figure (“the glorious one”) embracing the elect; physical staging and reverence cues indicate editorial narrative frame.
- ¶5: Speech-introduction formula in a congregational setting (“He says to them:”), continuing the narrative frame rather than substrate teaching.
- ¶6: Contains explicit NT/Gospel entry vector: “For the saviour says: … their angel sees the face of the Father daily.” Even where Manichaean terms appear (e.g., “Light Mind”), the paragraph’s climactic authority is a Gospel saying, marking this unit as later Christian overlay/homiletic use.
- ¶7: Crowd reaction and settling of the scene; narrative continuation (“all heard these words… while his disciples stand”) characteristic of compilation frame.
- ¶8: Formal disciple request (“Tell us, our master…”) is a standard dialogue prompt that frames the ensuing teaching.
- ¶9: The opening attribution is a later dialogue-frame seam, but the remaining unit shifts into impersonal, process-oriented exposition (“Pearls shall arise…”, causal steps), consistent with older teaching-style discourse rather than institutional application.
- ¶10: Impersonal, stepwise “how it forms” exposition with no appeal to Gospel authority and no institutional identification; reads like a preserved teaching block.
- ¶11: Numbered/quantified variation within a natural-process teaching block; still impersonal and non-institutional.
- ¶12: Continues the same technical, causal register; no shift into exhortation or church-identification, supporting an older instructional stratum.
- ¶13: Closure of the technical teaching sequence (“I have taught you… I have told you…”), still without institutional mapping; functions as the end-cap of a self-contained exposition.
- ¶14: Remains within the parable’s internal economy (divers → traders → kings/nobles) without yet identifying these as church offices; still ‘image-world’ description rather than editorial pointing-at.
- ¶15: Editorial seam into institution: “This is also what the holy church is like.” The correspondence crosses from image-world to contemporary ecclesial identity; per seam rule the whole paragraph is late application.
- ¶16: Continues the editorial application mapping using institutional terms (“apostles”) and interpretive identifications; even with cosmological nouns (“aeons of greatness”), the mapping’s target is church structure, indicating later graft.
- ¶17: Impersonal cosmological-soteriological statement in system terms (souls, ascent, aeons, rest) without citation or institutional exhortation; reads as a compact substrate doctrinal kernel.
- ¶18: Direct second-person exhortation (“You too, my beloved ones, struggle…”) and promised reward language; application-voice concentration matches the chapter-end exhortative layer.

---

## Chapter 84
### Concerning Wisdom; it is far superior when on the Tongue than in the Heart of the Person.

*This chapter is dominated by a didactic analogy-teaching (child-in-womb/birth; fire-in-wood/light) with a dialogue wrapper. It lacks the hallmark “cosmos→cosmos” systematic architecture (named aeons, degree-lists, body/universe maps), so the extracted ‘core’ here is best understood as an older homiletic correspondential instruction embedded in a Kephalaia Q&A frame rather than a clear fragment of the deepest cosmological substrate. No NT citation overlay or institutional/pastoral graft is present; the primary late layer is the hagiographic dialogue frame (apostle/disciple, rejoicing/obeisance closure).*

Behold, when a person will be taught wise wisdom in his heart, and he seals it in his doctrine, so shall you find him rejoicing greatly about it. However, [sh]all his [ ... ... ] to him, more th[an] when he may pro-c[laim ... ] and utter it. ⟨p.205⟩ He shall be enlightened by it, and [t]hat [wisdom] shall shine forth the more in him. It is un-veil[ed be]fore him, and through it he assumes power and truth.

I understand [ ... ] that this word I have uttered is correct. I know that the wis-dom I have been taught is spread through my heart and perfected in my soul. (However), it is not found like the splendour in me, so that I regard it [ad]vanced, except when I shall proclaim it by my mouth and utter it to others. Indeed, when I proclaim it, I am giving it to the ears [of] others to hear. Would I do these same things, even if I had never heard it in [my] days of being? Would I desire greatly and my heart be drawn to the wis-dom I now proclaim? [I entreat y]ou, my master, that you might instruct me as to [w/hy] this wisdom becomes more advanced when I proclaim it, than when it is sealed in my heart.

[ ... ] it is superior in my mouth when I proclaim it than when it is set in m[y heart]. You yourself rejoice in it; and the other one who hears it from you, he shall rejoice in it, and be enlightened [by] it and receive thereby permanent strength.

For like this matter, just so a small b[o]y who is conceived in the belly of his mother. He [ ... he] turns in his mother's womb, filling her womb. The mother knows and understands that this child she conceives is alive within her. She rejoices over him until [the tim]e when she gives birth. And he comes from her alive with his [limbs whole] and perfect in beauty, without defect, ⟨p.206⟩ [in] the living open air that is more sp[ac]ious than the first air he was in. He fills his eyes with the light and speaks with his living voice in the way of they who are born.

Now, the time when this woman conceives the child in her belly, her joy at conceiving him in her womb is not so [ve]ry great as when she gives birth and sees him; and is full of his [be]auty and stature in the space of a single moment. The love and joy over him shall be a hundred [t]imes greater than it was, now that she has given birth and seen him.

For the first time when she conceived him in her belly, his beauty and the sight of his eyes was hidden from his m[o/th]er; but when she gave birth to him she saw his beauty. His s[t]ature and his loveliness came before the e[ye]s of his f[athe]r [a]nd his mother and all his relatives. They shall rejoic[e] over him more and more when they look upon him face to face and see his beauty and delightfulness.

Just as in this simile, the wisdom that is present [in] the heart of the person is like the living child who is co[nceived] in the belly of his mother. And when he is taught and seals it in his heart, it becomes like the child who shall be born, and they see his beauty.

So, in this way, the wisdom that the person proclaims, speak[ng] it from his heart, shall be advanced more and mor[e]. Its en-hancement and glory shall double from the time when the bea-uty and splendour of the saying will be displayed before the eyes [of t]hey who hear it, and it shall also advance for you [ ... ... ] your hearing, and you are astonished at what you proclai[m].

Once again, the wisdom is like this, while it is hidden in the heart of the person. [Bef]ore he has uttered it, it is just like [the blaze] of fire that is hidden in wood. An[d] that wood is [set aflame] by the blaze of the fire, but the garment of fir[e that exists in] the wood is not apparent. Indeed, you can see [ ... ] ⟨p.207⟩ wood and they put them in a single house. It is impossible to [put li]ght to that house as long as the light within [ ... ] until the time when they are added to the fire, and the light [comes f]orth from them. It is possible for that entire house [to be] lit by the light of a single piece of wood.

This is also the case with the wisdom that is in the heart of the person. [It] is like the fire that is hidden in the wood, as its light is not [d]isplayed. For its part, the wisdom is like this: its li[ght] is hidden and its glory is hidden in the heart; but when the person will proclaim it, its glory shall be displayed before the eyes and the ears of a multitude.

So, if the wisdom is like the paradigms you have taught me, why are there some people who shall hear the word of wisdom and rejoice in it and give glory to it; when others shall listen to it and neither rejoice [i]n it nor receive glory amongst them?

For in this respect the wisdom is like this child about whom I have told you, the one who [was] born from the woman. Now, when he will be born, his father and mother and family circle shall rejoice over him. However, you find others grieving by reason of him, [s]ince they are strangers to him. These are not reckoned among his family. [They do n]ot rejoice over him, because he is not of their race.

This is also [the case] with the wisdom. When it is proclaimed by the mouth of the teacher, these who are [a]kin to it shall receive it to them and rejoice in it; but [those] who are strangers to it neither rejoice in it [ ... ] nor receive it to them.

Just like the light of [the fire, which I] proclaim[ed] to you, that shall come from the wood [and be apparent o]utside before the eyes of every one. ⟨p.208⟩ So, [wh]oever looks shall see the light that has [come fro]m the wood; but whoever is blind does not see the [fir]e.

This is also the case with the wisdom, when it will be pro-claimed. The person, in whom is the [Mi]nd, of him is the wisdom. Whenever he may hear it, he shall receive it in to him; but the one who has no Mind in him is a stranger to it. He neither receives it in to him, nor shall he listen to [it].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening ‘Once again… disciple… apostle… I entreat you’ is Kephalaia frame; the remaining content states the teaching thesis (wisdom internal vs proclaimed) without institutional or NT markers.
- ¶2: The ‘apostle’ attribution is late frame; the remainder is a thematic, non-cosmological instruction/question continuing the wisdom-on-tongue vs wisdom-in-heart contrast.
- ¶3: Clear hagiographic speaker-tag ‘apostle’ plus commendation formula; the extracted lines are the compact teaching claim about proclamation producing enlightenment/strength.
- ¶4: Impersonal analogical exposition with no dialogue tags, institutional terms, or citations; functions as the first correspondential paradigm (hidden life → manifest birth).
- ¶5: Continuation of the child paradigm in an expository voice; despite a slight ‘affect’ register, it stays within the analogy rather than exhorting an audience or naming institutions.
- ¶6: Completes the ‘hidden → seen’ logic of the paradigm; no frame formulas or later application markers intrude.
- ¶7: Explicit correspondence application (wisdom-in-heart ↔ child-in-womb/birth) stated as a mapping; still not institutional/NT and remains within the analogical system.
- ¶8: Expository ‘how it works’ voice (increase/doubling, display before eyes/ears) with no hagiographic tags; slight second-person residue does not pivot into institutional exhortation.
- ¶9: Second paradigm (fire-in-wood) articulated as a process-mechanism (hidden light → manifest light) without dialogue frame; ‘Once again’ here functions as internal rhetorical iteration, not Kephalaia speaker-tagging.
- ¶10: Tight mapping statement (wisdom ↔ hidden fire/light) in impersonal explanatory style; no institutional vocabulary or citations.
- ¶11: Speaker-tag ‘disciple… apostle’ is frame; the remaining sentence is a substantive problem-question extending the teaching (differential reception) without pastoral regulation.
- ¶12: Pure dialogue management (‘the apostle speaks… I will persuade you… teach you’) with no independent teaching content.
- ¶13: Analogy extended to a second dimension (kin vs strangers) in an explanatory voice; no institutional naming (church/elect) or citation formulas.
- ¶14: Direct mapping from the ‘kin/strangers’ paradigm to wisdom-reception; ‘teacher’ remains generic and not an institutional office-list, so the mapping does not cross into pastoral regulation.
- ¶15: Paradigm reiterated (visibility vs blindness) as a natural correspondence; still not anchored to contemporary polemic or institutional categories.
- ¶16: Introduces Manichaean technical term Mind as an inner capacity determining receptivity; remains doctrinal/explanatory rather than exhortative or institutional, suggesting a teaching layer rather than later pastoral graft.
- ¶17: Formulaic closure reaction (rejoiced, persuaded, obeisance, sat down) characteristic of Kephalaia hagiographic framing.

## Chapter 85
### Concerning the Cross of Light: [...] trample upon it.

*This chapter is dominated by later practical/pastoral casuistry (travel, obedience to superiors, alms-collection, elect/catechumen relations) with clear editorial fatigue in the second half. The oldest recoverable layer appears mainly as a self-contained teaching unit using the doctor/healing analogy to explain why “trampling” that causes short-term distress does not incur guilt when done for healing/liberation; one NT/Gospel saying is inserted into that teaching (overlay).*

because the living soul is like this. As a person, ⟨p.210⟩ if a disease will take hold of his limbs, and his heart [is sl]ow, his soul distressed, and he has pain in his disease. He seeks after a doctor to cure him, to make him strong of heart. And he cures, attends to, and gives him health [from] the disease that has risen up against him. So he thinks about this.

[And] a wise person comes, knowing the formulas proper to a skilled doctor. If he shall achieve a healing of the one whom he [... ...] it, he recites over him the formulas that he knows, and he even tramples with his foot upon all his limbs! That person, who is ill, knows that this person is trampling upon him so that he might achieve a healing; rather, not doing this to him out of some enmity he had against him beforehand. Instead, all that he does, he does to him that he might make a healing of his body. And he expels [the] disease and the pain, trampling upon him to occasion this benefit. That person, who is ill, he can bear no anger nor hatred against this doctor, who tramples upon him. He can not hate him out of enmity, because he knows that he does this to him to his benefit.

However, should someone come and trample upon him in enmity; he may hate, prevent and not permit him! For he knows that he tramples upon him in enmity; and as he strikes out of a wanton whim, he piles another illness upon his disease.

Again, this too is the case with the living soul; whether in the earth, or in the Cross of Light. Should you walk along upon it on the path, should you trample the Cross, it may take no reckoning against you in anger nor wrath. For it knows that [you] walk upon it for relief and healing, to proclaim on its behalf. [You] walk because of it, to reveal its mysteries. Also, the distress of the bones, which you have oppressed, befits not sin, but is set towards the victory. ⟨p.211⟩ Again, because of this nothing will be lost for you due to the distress of the bones and your toil; rather, it all accumulates to the relief (of the soul).

Rather, the word that wounds is this: should a person utter a saying for the sake of the murder of a person, or the murder of animals, or for [the sake of] the murder of trees and the Cross of Light, the lying word and the wrathful and the bitter, or a word of envy and [...] only, or a word of accusation, which someone will spread against his brother; this is the [wor]d that wounds [...]. Should the person utter it, it befits [si]n; because [he has br]ought forth from it the demons [...] concerning this [...] the righteous person, or [...] all of them, which I have proclaimed, in order that [...] his word to the fetter; because anyone who would [...] his word to the fetter. And again, he [...] his coming forth, they will bind him in the place wherein he has bound his [...].

[He is li]ke this, a wise doctor who might turn back [... ...] his disease. As a wound is in his body, he cuts [...] this person who is ill, in his wisdom, [...] he [...] this wound, and he cuts it. After these things [...] and he places the soluble drugs upon it, so that [they will be able to diss]olve. He might incise it with the iron, and he casts it out ⟨p.212⟩ [f]rom him, with the soluble drugs. And the wound is purified [fr]om the fever. After these events, he shall place the soothing drugs [...] and it heals up. That person, who is ill, he shall give [...] but he shall not [...] he [shall not] generate anger in his heart against him; because he knows that he is making the aspect of his benefit and his healing. After the cut that he may make, and the cauterizing that he may do there, he shall even give rewards to the doctor! He gives his thanks and is a friend to him his whole life through.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue-question apparatus (“Once again… disciple questioned the apostle”) plus explicit congregational/church setting; functions as hagiographic framing of a practical concern, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Institutional logistics (superior, teacher of the church, foreign brethren, alms brought to church) and first-person anxiety; cosmos→church-operations application rather than cosmos→cosmos teaching.
- ¶3: Contains a brief frame attribution (“when the apostle heard… he says”) and then casuistic reassurance about travel ‘for the work of God’ versus ‘lustful matter’; second-person persuasion voice and ethics dominate.
- ¶4: Direct address and institutional identity (“Every elect and righteous person…”) with moral evaluation (crown/palm-branch vs acquisition); application voice, not structural cosmology.
- ¶5: Pure moral contrast (lust/body/acquisition = sin/error) without cosmological mechanism; late ethical casuistry style.
- ¶6: Continues practical reassurance tied to teacher-sent travel; still framed as addressing fear of sin in daily conduct.
- ¶7: Underlying analogy reads as general, impersonal illustrative teaching about the living soul and healing; however the paragraph opens with direct reassurance (“do not be afraid”), indicating later application grafted onto an older didactic unit.
- ¶8: Self-contained teaching unit: impersonal illustrative logic (healing-by-pain) with no church offices, alms, or NT citation; stays within a general correspondential analogy used to explain cosmic ‘distress’ mechanics.
- ¶9: Completes the analogy by distinguishing beneficent trampling from hostile trampling; remains within the teaching illustration without institutional pointers.
- ¶10: Core explanatory logic about the living soul in earth/Cross of Light and ‘distress’ functioning toward victory is interrupted by an explicit ‘saviour said’ citation formula, indicating later Christian overlay inserted into an older teaching flow.
- ¶11: Explicit institutional scenario (teacher/foreign brethren/alms) and second-person reassurance; practical guidance about speech in church life.
- ¶12: Though morally framed, it asserts a mechanistic consequence (speech ‘brings forth’ demons; binding in a ‘fetter’ and reciprocal binding-place), which reads as internal cosmic causality rather than church rule; no NT citation or institutional offices.
- ¶13: Returns to alms-focused application (“need of the alms… its gathering in”); institutional concern overrides any cosmological exposition.
- ¶14: Extended, internally coherent teaching parable (cutting/cautery/drugs) explaining beneficial pain; lacks church markers and functions as a general correspondential model for ‘wounding’ that heals.
- ¶15: Explicitly identifies the analogy with church offices and economy (“namely the elect person… gathers it in, bringing it to the church”); cosmos→institution application dominates (editorial extension).
- ¶16: Institutional program for the ‘holy church’ and ascetic disciplines (prayer, self-control, chains, lashings); this is operational/pastoral material, not the older impersonal substrate.
- ¶17: Direct command address (“I command you, my brothers and my limbs”), catechumen instruction, and eating rules; clear late pastoral/application voice.
- ¶18: Dialogue attribution and summative reassurance (“Then speaks the apostle…”); functions as formal closure of the exchange.
- ¶19: Formulaic response of rejoicing/thanksgiving to ‘my master’; hagiographic closing typical of the Kephalaia frame.

## Chapter 86
### The Chapter of the Man who asks: Why [am] I sometimes at peace, and troubled at other times?

*The chapter is a dialogue: a heavy frame/question section (¶1–5) including experiential self-report and a clearly institutional/pious vignette (¶4), followed by a compact cosmological-mechanistic answer about the Light Mind, astrological disturbance, and the mixed character of nourishment (¶6–11). No NT-citation overlay appears; the main late material is the hagiographic/dialogue apparatus and recap-style address to the disciple.*

The soul that assumes the body, when the Light Mind will come to it, shall be purified by the power of wisdom and obedience, and it is cleansed and made a new man. There is no trouble in (the soul), nor confusion nor disturbance.

However, when a disturbance will arise for him and he will be troubled, this disturbance shall go into him in [ ... ], first through his birth-signs and his difficult stars that [ ... ] they turn over him and stir him and trouble him with lust and anger and depression and grief, as he wills. Also, as he wills, the powers of heaven shall trouble him through their roots, to which he is attached.

Again, there are times you shall find the powers of heaven are peaceful and tranquil for him, and it comes to [ ... ] a difficult part comes into him by the nourishment that he has eaten, or else indeed in some [ ... ] or in the water that they have drunk. Again, trouble and confusion and anger will increase in him, and lust multiplies upon him together with depression and grief; because of the nourishment of the bread he has eaten and the water he has drunk, which are full of bothersome parts, a vengeful counsel. They shall enter his body, [mixed in] with these foods, and they even become joined in with the wicked parts of the body and the sin that is in him; transferring the anger and the lust and the depression and the grief, these wicked thoughts of the body.

There are times, however, if you shall find that the nourishment that comes into you is pure [ ... ... ], so it is greater in light and life while the waste is less in it, and the evil-doing is diminished from it. Even the atmosphere above is still, being purified through the [ ... ... ] also the image is calmed from [ ... ... ] increases in you through ⟨p.216⟩ those good parts of the food that come into you. And they find you at ease and tranquil, as you are well governed in your abodes because of the living part, which is greater on account of the light of these justified souls that are in it. The ones that have perfected their deeds. Their dues have ceased, while their soul is carefree [ ... ] they have been allied with these living souls that [are p]resent in you. Due to this you shall find them in you, calm in tranquillity, and they come from you without trouble; and you are found flourishing of body. Even your deeds are determined, being well constructed after their manner, and your doctrines are in order, as your words [ ... ] your soul is carefree within you, ascending like a bird.

when [your bo]dy is troubled, you shall be troubled by the conduits of they below, the difficult parts that come into you by food and the act of eating. Again, when the conduits above are calmed from you, even your food that has come into you is clear and pure. Your heart shall be found ordered in its place, your counsels at peace from disturbance.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue opening (“Once again… I beseech you, my master”), a classic Kephalaia frame marker.
- ¶2: First-person experiential report within the question scene; not systematic cosmological exposition but part of the dialogue setup.
- ¶3: Extended first-person complaint/psychological turbulence in Q&A mode (application/affect language), functioning as the disciple’s question material rather than an impersonal cosmological mechanism.
- ¶4: Institutional piety vocabulary (fasting, praying, reciting psalms) and brother-relations; this is church-practice coloration rather than cosmos→cosmos mechanism.
- ¶5: Petition/transition back to the master (“So now I beseech you…”), serving as scene management.
- ¶6: Core cosmological mechanism (Light Mind purifying the soul) embedded in explicit dialogue-frame/authorial staging (“apostle… to the disciple”).
- ¶7: Impersonal causal explanation inside the cosmic system (birth-signs, difficult stars, powers of heaven, roots/attachments) rather than institutional application.
- ¶8: Cosmic-physical mechanism: disturbances mediated through mixed nourishment and bodily ‘parts’; still cosmos/anthropos mechanics rather than exhortation or church practice.
- ¶9: Recap/bridging in second-person with explicit ‘I have explained to you’ meta-discourse; functions as editorial/compilation connective, and is too lacunose to securely isolate substrate wording.
- ¶10: Extended internal mechanism (purity of nourishment ↔ light/life balance ↔ justified souls present within the mixture), with correspondential imagery (soul ‘ascending like a bird’) but no turn to institutional/polemical targets.
- ¶11: Contains a clear core causal scheme (conduits below/above; food-mediated influx) embedded in a concluding ‘I explained’ discourse typical of the compiled Q&A presentation.
- ¶12: Formulaic closing praise of the apostle and blessing language; hagiographic/community frame.

---

## Chapter 87
### Concerning the Alms, that [ ... ] life in the Church.

*This chapter is dominated by institutional/almsgiving material about the holy church and catechumens (pastoral/editorial extension). The oldest cosmological teaching appears mainly in ¶7–10, where the ascent of the living soul through the sun and moon and the resting of the light-givers in the Land of Light is described entirely within the cosmic system (no church application).*

Likewise this [liv]ing sou[l] that today is set in mixture; for it wishes to [as]cend and go to the house of its people, but it kn[ow[s a]nd understands that it has no open door in all the powers of heaven and earth. For they are its oppression that [ ... ] it everywhere. Indeed, it has no open door except the sun and the moon themselves, the light-givers of the heavens [ ... / ... ] they become the place of rest for it. And they become a door that opens for it [in] the coming forth, and (the soul) comes out through them to the country of the household of i[ts] people.

Again, further in, also the light-givers of the heavens have no place of rest amongst all the powers of heaven; except for the lan[d] of light, that has indeed been theirs for ever.

So, thus the living soul is purified of all afflictions; and they provide a rest for it, and open a door that opens to the house [of] its people.

Also, it is the case that the land of lig[ht] will become the final receiver for the light-givers of [the] heavens, and they will rest themselves therein and rule in it [for] ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic opening scene-setting (“Once again… the enlightener… He says to his disciples”).
- ¶2: Institutional polemic and alms discipline (sects, catechumens, holy church, commandments of alms). Cosmos→institution mapping/application dominates.
- ¶3: Defines the church ‘in two forms’ (brothers/sisters) and treats alms as redeemed through the church—operational ecclesial register, not cosmic system description.
- ¶4: Editorial extension in institutional terms (church as doorway, depends on catechumens). This is church logistics/application rather than cosmological mechanism.
- ¶5: Although cast as a simile (earth/tree/gardener), it is explicitly keyed to the ‘assembly of catechumens’; the correspondence is used to point to institutional roles, so it is not the pre-compilation substrate.
- ¶6: Direct church/catechumen dependency teaching (“place wherein there are no catechumens…”). Pattern-mimicking extension of the simile into institutional application.
- ¶7: Impersonal cosmological mechanics (mixture; ascent; ‘open door’; sun and moon as light-givers/vehicles). Despite the word ‘today’, the mapping remains cosmos→cosmos with no ecclesial application.
- ¶8: System-internal hierarchy: light-givers’ resting-place defined only by cosmic domains (powers of heaven; Land of Light).
- ¶9: Continues the same cosmological process description (purification/rest/door) without institutional referents.
- ¶10: Cosmic eschatological settling: Land of Light as ‘final receiver’ for the light-givers—structural, non-exhortative, non-citing voice.
- ¶11: Explicit institutional analogy returns (church ↔ alms of catechumens), concluding with shared ‘place of rest’—a cosmos→church application paragraph rather than core cosmology.

## Chapter 88
### Concerning the Catechumen who found fault with the Elect: why he is angry.

*This chapter is dominated by dialogue frame and pastoral/institutional exhortation (catechumen vs. elect/saints). Only small embedded fragments read like older doctrinal exposition (dual-essence anthropology; the divine element from the heights; the foreignness of the body/flesh of Sin). There is no sustained systematic correspondential mapping sequence here; the ‘core’ appears only as brief doctrinal clauses pressed into a disciplinary argument.*

They are gods as they stand firm in the image of the gods. The divinity that is planted in them came to them from the heights and [dw]elt in them.

they are established in a body that is not their own, hating the flesh of Sin that makes the other dwell in a foreign land. Due to this they shall be angry, and say and utter hard words to each others' faces.

For you have known the mystery of the two essences, you have understood that what is good and what is evil dwell in every person.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic scene-setting (“Once again… my master, the apostle”).
- ¶2: Dialogue/question setup voiced by a catechumen; part of the compiler’s narrative apparatus rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶3: Dialogue attribution (“our enlightener speaks…”), a classic Kephalaia frame marker.
- ¶4: Exhortation plus illustrative parable aimed at correcting behavior (“not constructive… do not…”). Natural imagery is used rhetorically (cosmos→audience), not as a closed cosmological mapping sequence.
- ¶5: Embedded doctrinal anthropology (divinity from the heights indwelling humans) inside an institutional identification of the Elect; the ‘elect’ framing reads later/applied, while the indwelling-divinity clause reads older and more impersonal.
- ¶6: Causal mechanism stated in cosmological-anthropological terms (foreign body; flesh of Sin as an agentive force) but embedded in second-person pastoral direction.
- ¶7: The ‘two essences’ doctrine is a compact cosmological principle presented as explanation; the surrounding rhetoric is disciplinary/pastoral, aimed at regulating speech and attitude toward the saints/elect.
- ¶8: Direct moral accusation and community-role justification (sins, lusts, teacher relationship). Even with mythic vocabulary (‘Cross of Light’), the paragraph is aimed at shaming/correcting the catechumen in the present setting.
- ¶9: Pure application-voice exhortation (“so that you may… Do not…”), entirely institutional/behavioral.
- ¶10: Narrative transition and reaction formula (“Then, when the apostle had uttered…”), part of the dialogic frame.
- ¶11: Catechumen’s contrition speech in the narrative frame (“Forgive me my sins…”). The explanatory phrase about ‘the old man’ is not presented as impersonal teaching but as a framed response within the story.

---

## Chapter 90
### Concerning the Fifteen Paths; and whether the Catechumen would have avoided causing his Wealth to go on the Three Paths to Gehenna

*The oldest layer here is relatively compact: a systematic cosmological scheme of fifteen post-mortem “paths” in the zone, with a threefold “waste” discharge to gehennas, plus a mechanistic doctrine of “deeds/wealth” as detachable substances that can be freed from the places where they are bound and travel before/with/after the soul. Much of the chapter is a later institutional/pastoral graft focused on the catechumen/elect distinction, the apostle choosing “forms” of his church, seals of faith, and discipline (fasting/prayer/alms), consistent with the flagged redaction and the strong second-half pastoral drift; paragraph 8 is a classic editorial seam extending a cosmological scheme into church predestination language.*

For we have heard thus from you: ‘Fifteen paths are drawn out in the zone. Four paths are pure and belong [to the] Light, leading up to life. Eight other paths are [mixed], leading above from that place. The Light shall go [u]p and become free through them; be purified and go in [to the] ships. However, the waste is separated and thrown [dow]n to transmigr[a]tion and the other three paths of [waste ...] discharged to the gehennas’.

You have explained [that the three] paths that belong to waste, the ones that are drawn to the gehennas, [a]re drawn from the fleshes. O[n]e of them is the appetite for [lawl]essness, that of people and of all flesh. The second is the [ ... ] all bodies, which is slaughter, [with] which all flesh is consumed. The third is the damaging action, which [ ... ] the Cross of Light and every body; together with the rest of the error and the blasphemy that wounds the gods.

Th[ese th]ree paths have you taught us, that they lead to the gehennas. And you have also [told] us that every person shall follow after hi[s] de[e]ds, whether to life or indeed to death.

Immediately all his first deeds, that he did before he received the knowledge, shall be freed from every place wherein they are [bou]nd and snared. They shall loosen their bond and asce[n]d from heaven and earth, from the trees and the fleshes. They are loosened from every place wherein they are and go to the heights

Now this matter is like this, the way [som]eone might dwell at a city, while his enti[re] following [is present] in the cities and the dwellings and the villages [of the country]. As long as, overall, the king is living in t[hat] place, you shall find his entire host living in those [dwelli]ngs that surround it. Also, whenever he may [give ... / ...] the sign for departure to the country, when he wishes [ ... ] to it, an indication is given by him to his entire follow[ing] so that he might set out on the road and depart [ ... ] each [pl]ace wherein it is. And he goes to the place where it i[s].

So, there are some among his expedition who shall travel before him and be prior to him. And there are others am[ong t]hem who shall wait for him and travel with him. They depart [at] his departure. Also, there are others among them who shall come after him, travelling behind him. They continue [very] many days and months travelling until they arrive.

His entire following shall not depart a[t] a single time; but someone shall go first and another after him, while others shall depart with him.

There are some among his limbs and his deeds shall be puri[f]ied while he is set in the body. They are cleansed in [the] firmaments of the heavens and go before him. [Th]ere are some also among his limbs shall be freed wi[t]h him, at the time when he comes out from [h]is body. There are others shall be freed [af]ter him from the bonds of the earth and that of the creatures. [They] go and reach him in the land of the living.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame: “Once again… elect… before the apostle… I beseech you, my master”. Hagiographic/instructional staging rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Systematic numbered cosmological architecture (15 paths; 4/8/3 division) mapped entirely within the cosmic system (zone, Light, ships, transmigration, gehennas), with an impersonal explanatory voice.
- ¶3: Continues the schematic “path” taxonomy (three waste-paths) as a cosmic-mechanical discharge “drawn from the fleshes,” still inside the Manichaean cosmological system (Cross of Light; gods) rather than institutional exhortation.
- ¶4: General rule stated as impersonal cosmological law (deeds determine the route), still framed as teaching content rather than church discipline.
- ¶5: Petition/question addressed to “my master” focused on the catechumen and “faith of God”; this is dialogue scaffolding and institutional problem-setting, not substrate exposition.
- ¶6: Continues the question in a pastoral/institutional register (multitude of catechumens in the world; “faith of God”), functioning as narrative prompt rather than core cosmological mapping.
- ¶7: Dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle speaks…”) and reassurance formula; explicit ‘apostle’ marker is later compiling frame.
- ¶8: Editorial seam flagged: extends discussion into institutional predestination (“apostle… choose the forms of his entire church… elect or catechumens”)—cosmos→contemporary church identification rather than cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶9: Institutional polemic and soteriology for catechumens (sects/heresies; seal of faith/knowledge; angels guiding deeds). This is church-facing application material.
- ¶10: Analogy deployed to support the catechumen ‘seal’ doctrine; embedded within institutional argument rather than independent cosmic mechanism teaching.
- ¶11: Catechumen-focused predestination (“form… chosen… in the heights”) tied to “mark of the faith” and ‘seal of truth’; institutional application dominates.
- ¶12: Explicit discipline and communal practice (fasting, prayer, alms to the saints) as institutional mechanisms; strong pastoral layer.
- ¶13: Within a catechumenate/ritual frame, a distinct older mechanistic substrate appears: deeds as quasi-substantial entities bound in cosmic locales (heaven/earth/trees/fleshes) that can be loosed and ascend—cosmos→cosmos process language.
- ¶14: Returns to catechumen-centered capability and exhortative implication (“has the ability… by his own hand”), in a pastoral/institutional voice rather than impersonal system description.
- ¶15: Impersonal correspondential analogy (king/host distributed through places; “sign for departure”) used to explain the mechanics of ‘deeds’ traveling; no explicit church institutions or NT overlay.
- ¶16: Continues the structured, impersonal threefold sequence (before/with/after), consistent with older schematic teaching.
- ¶17: Concise schematic recap of the before/with/after structure; no institutional anchoring.
- ¶18: Underlying core mechanics are clear (deeds purified while in-body / freed at death / freed after, traveling through firmaments to the land of the living), but the catechumen-identification is a later pastoral application cue.
- ¶19: Concluding address in the teacher’s voice (“I have instructed you…”) about catechumen/limbs and angels; functions as editorial wrap-up rather than substrate exposition.

---

## Chapter 91
### Also concerning the Catechumen; shall he be saved in a single Body?

*This chapter is overwhelmingly later institutional/pastoral instruction about catechumens (fasting, alms, church loyalty, forgiveness of sins), wrapped in dialogue and praise formulas. Only two short passages preserve older, impersonal cosmological-soteriological mechanics (post-mortem ascent/purification and the formation of the “light image”), embedded inside catechumen/church application and (once) an NT-style treasure saying.*

When, however, they come forth from their body, they travel on their way and pass by in the place above, and go in to the life. They shall be purified in the heavens, and they are plucked just as a fruit that ripens is plucked from the tree.

⟨p.234⟩ As for the rest, he shall be questioned about a single part; and receive blows for (those sins) and retribution. Afterwards he is purified, whether indeed above or below. He shall be purified according to the worth of his deeds, and cleansed and washed and adorned. Afterwards, he is sculpted a light image; and [he glid]es up and reaches the land of rest,

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue frame (“Once again… speaks… to this apostle”), catechumen topic; hagiographic apparatus rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Beseeching/question formula and explicit intent to instruct “my other brothers” and catechumens; community editorial frame.
- ¶3: Conduct-description of the ‘perfect catechumen’ (marriage/household/wealth detachment, heart set on the holy church). Even though it contains a speech attribution, the substance is institutional ethics, not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: Explicit ‘saviour’ + apostle citation of 1 Cor 7 language; entered via NT/Pauline tradition.
- ¶5: Seam-flagged editorial extension summarizing ‘perfect catechumens’ and equating them with elect; institutional vocabulary dominates.
- ¶6: Rules/virtues (fasting, prayer, alms, church attendance) plus ‘children/wife’ brought into church; institutional application throughout.
- ¶7: Exhortative identification (‘good pearl’, catechumens) and internal book reference; not an impersonal cosmological register.
- ¶8: The extracted lines are impersonal cosmological mechanics (post-mortem transit, heavenly purification) with a natural correspondence (ripe fruit plucked). The removed continuation applies the image to alms/elect/catechumens—crossing into institutional present.
- ¶9: Institutional bookkeeping (‘contribution to the church’) and deference to another manual (‘Treasure of Life’); not substrate.
- ¶10: Prescriptive repentance/forgiveness via ‘God and the holy church’ and accounting of sins; institutional soteriology.
- ¶11: Closing-praise scene with disciples blessing the teacher; hagiographic frame.
- ¶12: Question frame (‘we beseech you’) introducing a catechetical problem-list (idols, sects, magic, murder, etc.); community Q&A apparatus.
- ¶13: Though it contains cosmological terms (‘separated the light from the darkness’), the salvation logic is mediated by ‘right hand of peace’ from the Light Mind ‘who dwells in the holy church’—an ecclesial rite/context rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶14: Absolution doctrine tied to entry into faith away from ‘sects and idols’; moral/institutional discipline.
- ¶15: Warning against relapse; catechumen/elect state as church categories; not cosmological mapping.
- ¶16: Explicit catechumen instruction: absolution through fasting/prayer/alms.
- ¶17: Detailed rulekeeping (fifty fasts, sexual abstinence on lord’s days, dietary prohibitions); institutional discipline.
- ¶18: Hours of prayer, daily alms, ‘garment… upon the saints’, ‘daily communion’; church practice.
- ¶19: Merit accounting (‘five parts’, ‘protection of the holy church’, ‘faith and love of the elect’) even where cosmological phrases appear; application dominates.
- ¶20: The extracted portion is process-description (purification, transformation into a ‘light image’, ascent to the ‘land of rest’) characteristic of older cosmological teaching, while the removed ending shifts to a Gospel-style ethical aphorism.
- ¶21: Conditional exhortation about steadfastness in catechumenate and recompense; institutional.
- ¶22: Moral warning against lying/apostasy and total sin reckoning; disciplinary tone.
- ¶23: Promise of ‘destiny’ and salvation conditioned on steadfast faith; hortatory soteriology rather than systematic cosmology.
- ¶24: Narrative closure/praise (‘these disciples… gave glory’); although it names ‘the first light’ and ‘the Mind’, its voice is doxological frame, not substrate exposition.

## Chapter 93
### A Catechumen asked the Apostle: When I would give an Offering to the Saints, shall I inflict a Wound on the Alms?

*This kephalaion is dominated by an institutional problem (catechumen alms for the elect) presented in Q&A form (frame + pastoral). The most plausibly older substrate-like material is the long impersonal legal/medical analogy (retaliation before a judge versus a doctor cutting a wound to heal). The explicit application to catechumens/elect/church and exhortation language is later pastoral usage of the analogy.*

For this matter is like to two people, if a personal enemy and an opponent of theirs will hit one first and make a sore and a wound on his body. Afterwards he smites him again and [...] and he hits over his blows and [...] blows that have made sores on him and [...] ⟨p.237⟩ strikes him these two times [...] accuses his enemy who has struck him. And this judge questions him to his face about the [...] of the sores that he has left as marks on his body. And they [...] on him as if he had split his head. And he is led to judgement and sentence, and is punished before the judge of the world. He makes retribution for a sore in the sore place, and adds to him the wound that he had left on this person who had not yet sinned against him. He shall not omit the retaliation of the opponent, as he is judged [...] according to enmity.

After the end, this enemy is condemned for the retaliation [...] sin against him. Afterwards, the other one is sick [...] the one whom this enemy has struck, and a wound comes out on him [...] and he becomes a sick person by cause of his illness, and he [...] He calls the doctor and shows him his wound. However, that doctor has no [...] nor [...] and he takes the knife to him and cuts this wound. Nevertheless, when he cuts it the blood shall pour out of his wound. That person shall be pained, but he accepts those and these things. This doctor has not retaliated nor was harm imposed on him; because he did this thing to him for healing his wound. All that he did to him was done for the good; and not directly for evil.

After the completion, he cuts this wound and lets blood from it and heals it. He shall even make [...] gifts and compliments. That illness and the healing become a pride for this doctor, and he is given honour.

Also, [this] is the other person [who] struck the comrade of the [...] sick with his [...] he struck in [...] he being cruel according to [...] living soul [...] upon it [...] that [...] accusation of [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue/Q&A frame markers (“Once again… catechumen… questioned the apostle”) with institutional roles (catechumen/elect) and first-person anxiety about alms-work; this reads as the compiling community’s question-setting rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Continuation of the catechumen’s question in direct address; still part of the Q&A apparatus rather than a stand-alone cosmological/correspondential teaching sequence.
- ¶3: Answer is direct exhortation (“Do not be frightened…”) about sin and alms, aimed at catechumen practice; even though it mentions ‘living soul’, the whole paragraph functions as pastoral reassurance/application in institutional context.
- ¶4: Impersonal, illustrative analogical teaching (legal retaliation/judgement) without catechumen/elect/church identifiers or second-person exhortation. Not a Gospel citation; reads like a pre-institutional didactic unit used as a base for later application. (Dating remains somewhat uncertain because it is not a systematic cosmic-degree map.)
- ¶5: Continuation of the same self-contained analogy (doctor/knife/healing) explaining ‘harm for healing’ as a structural principle, still not anchored to institutional practice; likely an older teaching kernel later applied to alms-preparation.
- ¶6: Completes the medical analogy in an impersonal narrative-teaching voice; no institutional vocabulary or audience address.
- ¶7: Explicit application pivot (“When you make an alms-offering you are like…”) plus institutional mechanisms (“healed by the elect, by the psalms and prayers and blessings”); this is cosmos→contemporary practice/institution, so the whole paragraph is later pastoral use of the analogy.
- ¶8: Highly fragmentary continuation that still reads as part of the analogical unit contrasting ‘cruel striking’ with ‘healing cutting’. Mention of “living soul” could be a bridge toward application, but there is still no church/elect/catechumen register here; retained as core with uncertainty due to lacunae.
- ¶9: Seam-flagged institutional extension: catechumens/church language and soteriological bookkeeping (stripping sins, absolution, intercession) that applies the preceding teaching pattern to community practice; per rules this is not MIXED despite possible imitation of teaching syntax.
- ¶10: Institutional summary with catechumen group action and Q&A closure-like movement; dominated by pastoral/church roles and lacks impersonal cosmological exposition.

## Chapter 94
### Concerning the Purification of these Four Elements [that have been place]d in the Flesh.

*The chapter is predominantly old, systematic correspondential teaching about the four light-elements’ mixture, purification in the body, and the resulting emissions (triadic chains). It is wrapped in a brief hagiographic opening (¶1) and ends with an institutional/application paragraph about the elect vs. sinners (¶9), which crosses from cosmic mechanism to church categories.*

The four elements [are what is] purified. From the beginning they are holy ones, living ones, and enlighteners. However, when they undertood [ ... ] in that first struggle, the waste was [mixed] in with them, and the Darkness of the enemy was mixed with them. And when they were come into the body, stripping off these polluted garments, they set them in the flesh. Their pollutions, which they have stripped off, shall be displayed in a [ ... ] and become a danger to the body.

The discharge of this pollution, that [the fir]e shall disgorge and place in the body, is [blo]od. The discharge of the blood is anger. The discharge of anger is the humours.

⟨p.240⟩
Also, the emissions that the waters discharge from them are lust. The discharge of lust is bitterness. The emission of bitterness is f[e]ver.

The emission that light shall disgorge is flesh. The emission of flesh is gloom. [The] emission of gloom is obstinacy.

The emissions that the tree shall disgorge, they are the voices of the winds of shame. The emission of winds of shame [is ... ]

The emission of the [tear] is the [ ... ] of these twelve emissions.

[ ... ] these four elements shall [ ... ] what are gathered in are [f]ound by the management of this soul food [that] enters the body. When they enter the body, they are cleansed and purified and established [i]n their living image, which is the new man. They shall live [ ... ] and receive the Light Mind and be purified in their image; and they come forth, being cleansed and holy. They attain their first rest.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic scene-setting (“Once again… the apostle… among the congregation”), a compilation frame marker; no cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal mechanism of mixture/purification (cosmic event → bodily process) without citation or institutional address; “first struggle” + elements as ‘holy/living/enlighteners’ is characteristic systematic cosmology.
- ¶3: Classic substrate-style correspondential chain (element → bodily substance → affect → physiological state) in a tight, iterative structure.
- ¶4: Systematic triadic emissions chain; no audience address or institutional application.
- ¶5: Same iterative correspondential mechanism (element → body/affect/mental state). The vocabulary remains within the system’s internal psychophysiology rather than polemic or discipline.
- ¶6: Fragmentary/corrupt, but still presented as an emissions-mapping in the same formal series; “tree” likely reflects an internal technical term (perhaps for wind) rather than institutional reference.
- ¶7: Though lacunose, it continues the enumerative/structural exposition (“these twelve emissions”), consistent with a pre-compilation schematic list.
- ¶8: Process-description of purification through ‘soul food’ and restoration to ‘living image’; still cosmological/anthropological mechanism (how purification works), not a Gospel/NT citation and not an ecclesial rule.
- ¶9: Crosses into institutional categorization (“the elect”, “the sinners”) and applies the mechanism to community sorting (elect vs. sinners), with a moralizing consequence statement (sins → transmigration). This is cosmos→church/world application rather than the older cosmos→cosmos mapping.

## Chapter 95
### The Apostle asks his Disciples: What is Cloud?

*After a brief hagiographic dialogue frame (¶1, ¶14, ¶18), the chapter is dominated by systematic cosmological teaching: five clouds rising from the five light elements, each with a sign (lightning, thunder, storm-wind, stillness, etc.), purification of lives, and the release/seizure of rulers by angels under the Virgin of Light, culminating in confinement in the seven outer ditches/paths in the sea outside the world. Late material here is mostly dialogue attribution and recap/expository address to disciples; the underlying mechanism remains consistently cosmos→cosmos.*

Happen you know that it shall be released from five places and ascend over this great earth and [ ... ] and it is revealed and seen in the atmosphere between.

So, the cloud shall ascend from fire to the heavens towards the likeness of the Virgin of Light that she shall display to it. Its sign is the flashes that occur with lightning storms [ ... i]ts exchange [ ... ]. And they shall be purified by her towards [ ... ] the light that she shall reveal [ ... ]. The rulers shall be released by the lightning storms and they are freed and sent.

However, [these] angels shall immediately be summoned to them, so that they will seize them, because they know that they shall never do good. Every place they will touch they shall make dead and a loss. Whenever the angels will make towards them and come beside [ ... ], they shall flee in their constraint before them. They go into whatever they will meet upon and assume it, like a robber being abandoned and fleeing before one stronger than him, as he runs after him to arrest him.

Now, those rulers that shall be freed from these clouds and assume whatever they will meet upon [ ... ] into the [ ... / ... ] that they did not [ ... ] will seize this thing, whether indeed a tree to which they have found the way, or animals, or people. Simply, every place that they will assume they shall burn and destroy by the fire of their body. Namely, the rulers, [the] children of fire, they that shall be stripped of the fire.

In contrast, [the] cloud that shall be raised up from water, and comes above towards the Virgin of Light; its sign is the thunder storms and the booms that shall come in the atmosphere in various forms and are heard. [ ... ] Again, there are times if the rulers that belong to them, which exist in the cloud, shall on occasion be constrained [in a] water [clou]d. And they flee from it and abandon it.

Now, [they] too are so, in that every place that they will [reach], they shall make frost and hail and snow. [They rui]n the seed-corn and the fruits and the plants; and they [ ... ] every place [that] they will reach.

Again, [ang]els shall be summoned to them who seize them. Still, while the angels [ru]sh after them in order to seize them, they shall make this destruction.

Also, [the cl]oud that will be raised up from wind ascends to the atmosphere between, towards the likeness of the Virgin that she shall display to it. Its sign is [the s]torm wind that shall blow with bitterness.

So, in those winds and tempests shall the lives be purified. Again, the rulers will be stripped of the cloud of wind and they descend from it, safe in their image. All the rivers and the seas that they will reach and arrive at, they shall make waves in them and raise [ ... ] and they [ ... ] the ships. They wreak destruction [ ... ] and they seize and bring them [ ... ].

Also the cloud, the one of light, immediately they will be stripped of it [ ... / ... ] the Virgin. She shall display its sign too [ ... ]. And its sign is this, in that you shall find it sailing in stillness and calm. The lives in it shall be cleansed in stillness and calm. Again, the rulers that will be stripped of it [ ... ] that will come forth [ ... ] they shall put [ ... ] and they take [ ... / ... ] there [ ... ] the angels seize them.

Again, the cloud that shall be raised up from the mingled air comes above towards [the image] of the Virgin. Its sign is this, in that [ ... ] are purified in it in stillness and [rest ... these rulers] that will be stripped of that cloud and come from it; every place that they will find [ ... ] they shall make it [ ... ] one of [ ... ] until [ ... ] they are seized and taken to their midst.

Their strippings, that they shall leave behind them in various places they will have reached, whether the produce of the trees or the offspring of the fleshes, they shall be taken midst [ ... ] wither away. They shall inflict great dangers, but again those rulers also the angels shall send to their midst. They bring the light from them and cast the demons down to these seven outer ditches of the great sea; which is the vessel of the waters that are outside the world.

Those ditches and the [ ... ] until they reach the earths below [ ... ] feet of the Porter.

for sometimes these angels shall be sent to them, to seize the rulers. Whenever the rulers [ ... / ... ] for the angels rush after them. They beseech them [ ... ] they that are given the command. However, [t]hose [rul]ers shall be freed from them alone and assume [ ... ] and [the fle]sh. They are consumed by this offspring [ ... / ... ] tree and that of flesh. The angels shall set a [ ... ] over them, that they might guard them until the hour when [those r]ulers will be stripped of tree and [flesh; a]nd they go up above. They shall be gathered [ ... ] above, and they are poured out [ ... ] in their first for[ms ... im]ages in which they first were. They take the light from them, which had remained behind in them. And they too are cast down to these seven outer [path]s that are in the great sea; the place to where these former ones [were c/as]t. They shall reach their midst [ ... ] and the light that is in them is taken.

And they are cast to the outer prison by the angels, when the command is given to them through the power of the Virgin of Light. She has authority over the entire zone, and cleanses the life that is in it.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic scene-setting and dialogue frame: “Once again, the apostle… sitting… he says to his disciples… I will reveal and teach you…”. This is the editorial container, not the substrate teaching voice.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological statement establishing a fivefold structure (five sources/places) and the ‘atmosphere between’—a system-internal mapping without institutional application.
- ¶3: Systematic element→cloud description plus sign-phenomenon (lightning), purification mechanics, and rulers’ release—all within the cosmological system under the Virgin of Light.
- ¶4: Mechanistic cosmological policing scene (angels vs. rulers) with illustrative simile; no institutional address or NT citation. The register is process-description rather than exhortation.
- ¶5: Continuation of the same cosmological mechanism: rulers’ descent/possession and destructive effect, with an internal identity label (“children of fire”). Despite mention of trees/animals/people, it remains explanatory cosmology, not moral-institutional application.
- ¶6: Second element-cloud (water) with sign (thunder) and the same internal cycle (rulers constrained, flee/abandon). Structured iterating exposition—typical of the substrate.
- ¶7: Effect-description of water-cloud rulers (frost/hail/snow). Although it touches agriculture, it remains within the cosmological ‘what they do’ mechanism rather than exhortation or institutional identification.
- ¶8: Refrain-like policing mechanism (angels summoned; rulers seized). The iterative pattern is characteristic of systematic teaching.
- ¶9: Third element-cloud (wind) with sign (bitter storm wind) and the ‘atmosphere between’ location—system-internal correspondential description.
- ¶10: Purification + stripping + descent in ‘image’ + destructive marine effects: a closed cosmological process chain, not pastoral address.
- ¶11: Fourth type (cloud of light) continues the same cosmological template (sign, cleansing, stripping, seizure). The brief second-person (“you shall find”) reads as pedagogical deixis within teaching, not a late institutional application.
- ¶12: Fifth element-cloud (mingled air) preserves the iterative, system-internal mechanism: purification, rulers’ stripping, local damage, eventual seizure.
- ¶13: Concludes the mechanism with extraction of light and confinement geography (“seven outer ditches… great sea… waters… outside the world”). Strongly cosmological and architectural—substrate-style closure.
- ¶14: Direct disciple question with honorific (“our master”)—dialogue apparatus of the compiled Kephalaia frame.
- ¶15: Core cosmological geography is present, but introduced by a dialogue-attribution marker (“[The apostle] says to them”), which is a later compilation frame that must be stripped from core_text.
- ¶16: Underlying passage is an impersonal mechanism of capture/stripping/light-extraction and confinement (substrate), but it is introduced by an audience-address imperative typical of compiled discourse rather than the oldest layer’s impersonal expository voice.
- ¶17: The recap is editorial/discourse-level (first-person teacher summary to an audience). The retained sentences revert to cosmological administration (authority over the zone; cleansing life), consistent with the core system.
- ¶18: Formulaic closing doxology/thanks by disciples (“they glorified him… we give thanks… our father…”)—standard hagiographic frame ending.
