## Chapter 96
### The Three Earths that Exist, they bear Fruit.

*The chapter begins with a brief dialogue-frame formula and then presents a compact, systematic “three earths/fields” teaching. Paragraphs 1–3 remain within cosmos→cosmos correspondences (earth/field ↔ farmers ↔ ascent mechanics). Paragraph 4 is an editorial seam that extends the pattern to the institutional “holy church” (elect/catechumens) and is therefore pastoral. Paragraph 5 is explicit exhortation/application to the audience and is pastoral.*

Three earths exist in the universe, and are being toiled over. At first they [...] the farmer in them. Afterwards they bear [good] fruit to they who toil over them.

One of them is this great earth that people are living upon. They are ploughing in it [...], it with iron hoes, they cultivate, [making] the furrows, turning it over [...] Afterwards they harvest fruit from [it ...

⟨p.245⟩
The second field is the living soul, the holy one. It is mixed in the entire zone, above and below. The farmers who toil over it are these two great light-givers, which journey through the heavens. They are toiling and cultivating it, so that they will draw it towards their aspect. And it leaps up and comes to the heights. It goes upwards, out from every place wherein it is, and it adds to them.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame-style incipit (“Once again… to his disciples”) introduces an older, impersonal, enumerative teaching (“Three earths exist…”) that sets up a systematic correspondential schema.
- ¶2: Remains descriptive and structural (field + cultivation + harvest) without audience exhortation or institutional identification; functions as the first term in the three-fold correspondential set.
- ¶3: Clear cosmos→cosmos mapping: living soul as a “field,” sun and moon (the “two great light-givers”) as its farmers, with ascent mechanics described impersonally. This is characteristic systematic substrate exposition.
- ¶4: Editorial seam/extension: applies the “third earth/field” slot to the institutional holy church and its ranks (“elect,” “catechumens”), shifting from cosmological mechanism to community-identification; even though “Light Mind” is cosmological, the paragraph’s mapping crosses into church structure.
- ¶5: Direct application voice and imperatives (“You yourselves…,” “Preach and enlighten…,” “Rather, toil!”) aimed at behavior and mission; this is late pastoral exhortation built on the preceding teaching.

---

## Chapter 97
### Concerning the Three Creations of the Flesh: the ones that were brought forth [...]; and the ones that were begotten by the Abortions; and the ones that were formed of themselves.

*After a brief hagiographic dialogue-introduction, the chapter is a continuous, systematic teaching sequence: a threefold classification of earthly life-forms (and their propagation) grounded in Manichaean cosmology (the Ambassador, rulers/powers, and the descent of “thought” as reproductive impulse). No institutional/pastoral or NT overlay appears.*

“All these animals that you see, and also the rest of the [crea]tures [...] of the earth, confirm them in three likeness[es ...]: some from [...] abortions. Others, however, form their own selves in their shape [...].

“Also, their nourishment is thre[e ...] light in them, if they shall wish [...]. [...] they eat see[ds and] fr[uit ...]; they eat seeds and fruit. The [...] it entirely, and they [...] the three archetyp[es] from their nature and the [...] the time when they are on the ea[rth ...]; they are seized [...] in their [...]. The Ambassador [...] all powers [...].

“[N] it [...], first [...]. ⟨p.247⟩ Plant them generally to this name: shoots and grass. It formed the bad, bitter trees, the reeds and the thorns and the [...] and these others that are like them.

“For that thought belongs to the flesh-eating rulers. Still, these [that were] planted (belong to) grass and gourds, since they are in their essence. Nevertheless, [this t]ho[ugh]t that came down from the rulers, as they are in fruit and grass first and shall not be planted in flesh and blood, it formed the trees that shall give nourishing fru[it], as their gourds are sweet.

“Again, this thou[ght] that came down from the powers that eat m[ea]t and also eat grass and gourds, it has formed the trees that shall not produce any nourishment. Their fruits belong neither to good nor evil.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again…”; “our enlightener… to his disciples”), characteristic of the compiled Kephalaia apparatus rather than the impersonal teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Systematic threefold taxonomy of generation (“three likenesses”) describing modes of arising within the cosmic/physical system; no citation, exhortation, or institutional pointing.
- ¶3: Correspondential/technical exposition (nourishment, light-mixture, “three archetypes”) with explicit cosmological agent (The Ambassador) operating within the Manichaean system; still descriptive rather than hortatory.
- ¶4: Continues the impersonal classificatory voice: naming categories of vegetation and their formation outcomes (“bad, bitter trees… reeds… thorns”), staying within natural/cosmic mechanics rather than contemporary application.
- ¶5: Mechanistic explanation of propagation via descending “thought” associated with rulers; maps cosmological actors → generative principle → plant-types and qualities (sweet/nourishing), without pivot to moral/institutional address.
- ¶6: Closes the schema by iterating the same causal mechanism (“thought… came down”) to account for another botanical class; the balanced valuation (“neither to good nor evil”) remains within descriptive cosmological-natural classification.

---

## Chapter 98
### What is Virginal; or, otherwise, what is Continent?

*The chapter is built as a stepped (roughly five-level) definitional sequence: ‘virginal’ vs ‘continent’ mapped to successive cosmic/processual layers of Light (aeons → mixture → purification by the Ambassador → transmigration remainder). The opening Q&A is late hagiographic frame. The middle run (¶3–8) is the strongest core substrate: impersonal cosmological/process language about mixture, purification, ascent, and transmigration. The later paragraphs pivot into institutional/ethical application (elect imagery; sexual renunciation), which reads as pastoral/Christianized extension of the pattern.*

What is called ‘virginal’ is assigned to the mystery of the aeons of greatness. And they called it ‘virginal’ by the category of all the light that did not [taste] of death, nor come to the enemy’s war.

However, what is called ‘continent’ occurs to the mystery of the light that came and was mixed with the darkness. It was defiled in bodies [ ... ] and went up to the light. [They] called [it ‘co]ntinent’.

Furthermore, the holy vir[gin]als [ ... ... ] should they come out against the en[emy ... ... not] taste death, nor [ ... ].

⟨p.249⟩ On the other hand, what is called ‘continent’ [is this li]ght that mixed with the darkness, was purif[ie]d, came [up f]rom all the creatures, and was confirmed in the great[ness]. They called it ‘continent’ since it tasted [of the l]ust; but afterwards was cleansed from it and [become an] holy one.

Furthermore, what is called [‘vi]rginal’ is this light that was purified by the ima[ge] of the [A]mbassador. It wen[t up and] attained [the] he[ights. It was] confirmed in the image of the gods.

Again, what is [called] ‘continent’ is the remainder of this light th[at] remained behind, from what was cleansed. It goes up and comes down in transmigration. Al[so], there is a part [of] it that shall come and attain the form of [hum]an [fl]esh. Again, that one too shall be chosen in the flesh, [accept] the hope, and become an holy one. And they name it ‘continent’.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic setting and dialogue setup (“the apostle… among the congregation of his church… he says to his disciples”) marks compilation frame, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Formulaic disciple request (“Proclaim to us, my master…”) is a classic Q&A wrapper around the teaching.
- ¶3: Core is the aeons-of-greatness definition and the light/death/war criterion (cosmic-process description). The removed lead-in is a late instructional frame marker.
- ¶4: Impersonal cosmological/process mapping (Light → mixture with Darkness → defilement in bodies → ascent) stays within the system; no institutional address or citation.
- ¶5: Fragmentary but continues the same cosmological criterion (“not taste death… against the enemy”) without audience exhortation or church-institution identifiers.
- ¶6: System-internal mechanics of mixture/purification/ascent; “lust” functions as a state of contamination within the cosmic process, not a direct moral injunction.
- ¶7: Classic core cosmology: purification by the (Third) Ambassador’s image, ascent, confirmation among gods—no institutional referents.
- ¶8: Processual teaching on the ‘remainder’ and transmigration (up/down movement; eventual human flesh) is substrate-like. The phrasing “[accept] the hope” slightly gestures toward later soteriological idiom, but it remains embedded in the cosmic mechanism rather than exhortation or NT citation.
- ¶9: Pattern is extended into institutional/para-Christian anthropology: “images of the elect” (church category) and “new man/old man” (strong Pauline idiom) apply the schema to community/personhood rather than staying cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶10: Explicit ethical/sexual definitions “in the flesh” (virginity vs renunciation after having a woman) are institutional moral application, not cosmological mechanism.
- ¶11: Concluding pedagogical summary (“Behold, I have taught you…”) functions as editorial closure/lesson-capping rather than core exposition.

---

## Chapter 100
### Concerning the Dragon with Fourteen Heads; what it is and [...]

*The chapter’s core is a systematic microcosmic mapping: the Fourteen Heads are identified with sense/organs (upper and lower), and the Five Dens with specific internal organs/blood—an internal correspondence (body ↔ spiritual ‘Dragon’ mechanism) with no external authorities. The chapter is wrapped in a Q&A frame (disciple ↔ apostle) and closes with a devotional/praise response. A late application/pastoral drift appears where the combat with the Dragon is tied to Manichaean status language (“elect”) and reward rhetoric.*

There is never a Dragon in the flesh with fourteen heads, as the Magi say! Rather, this is a spiritual lesson.

Again, this is how this earth [ ... ] to it, as it is accounted a corporeal Dragon [ ... ] I will instruct you [ ... ] the Dragon is this doctrine [ ... ] of the flesh [ ... ] also the fourteen heads that are distributed over the Dragon are these.

The seven sense organs on the head of the body are the two of sight by which one sees; the two of sound by which one hears; the two of scent by which one smells; also the tongue [ ... ] by which one selects, receiving there the different tastes that occur in every form.

These are the seven heads of the Dragon, the seven sense organs that are on the upper part of the person; [ ... ] below. The seven of the torso are these: two [ ... ]; the two [ ... ] ruler [ ... ] the seven heads that are above; so with the ones below they total and make fourteen heads of the Dragon.

⟨p.253⟩
Conversely, the five dens about which they have spoken, where the Dragon is congregated and protrudes, are these: the first is the tongue; the second is the lungs; the third is the heart; the fourth is the spleen; the fifth is the blood that dwells in them. Again, if it shall look out from them [and reveal its likeness and its observation above and below, it wages war by] its fourteen heads.

Whoever will recognise them with the Dragon, which is the thought of the body, and struggles with it and is victorious and kills it in them [ ... he is called]

So, behold, I have taught you of the Dragon that exists with its fourteen heads and five dens, wherein it is concealed and hidden. It does through them [ ... / its des]ire all the time.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Strong hagiographic/dialogue framing (“Once again… disciple… apostle… I beseech you, my master”). Although it introduces the topic, the paragraph is primarily the compiled Q&A container.
- ¶2: Begins with a compilation dialogue tag (late), but preserves a concise ontological correction (corporeal vs. spiritual) consistent with the core’s explanatory voice.
- ¶3: Core-style transition into exposition: defines what the Dragon ‘is’ and announces an enumerative mapping (“the fourteen heads… are these”), with correspondence staying within the anthropological/cosmic system.
- ¶4: Systematic, impersonal enumeration (sevenfold schema) mapping faculties/senses—classic substrate correspondential listing.
- ¶5: Degree-structure arithmetic (7+7=14) extending the mapping from head to torso; the teaching remains internal to the microcosm schema.
- ¶6: Highly structured fivefold mapping (dens ↔ organs/blood) and mechanistic description (how it ‘looks out’/‘wages war’), characteristic of the older explanatory substrate.
- ¶7: Starts with a general mechanism-identification (“Dragon… thought of the body”) compatible with the core, then shifts into application voice and Manichaean community terminology (“elect”)—consistent with the chapter’s flagged second-half drift.
- ¶8: Summative restatement of the defined structure (14 heads, 5 dens) plus a functional claim about continuous operation (“desire all the time”); remains internal to the Dragon/body mechanism without institutional targeting.
- ¶9: Closing disciple response with blessing and eternal-life/victory rhetoric; typical Q&A wrap-up and doxological framing rather than substrate exposition.

---

## Chapter 101
### [Concer]ning why, if the Person shall look down into Water, [ ... ]

*This chapter is dominated by an older correspondential/scientific teaching about reflection, inversion, birth, and shadow as signs of cosmic processes (Summons/Obedience; astral ‘fathers’; body–earth fastening). A thin dialogue frame is present, and several paragraphs show a later pedagogical address (“I reveal…”, “listen…”) grafted onto otherwise cosmological exposition—best treated as MIXED rather than institutional pastoral material. The grouping is loose, consistent with redaction/textual development noted in the apparatus.*

instruct me about this lesson: When the person is above water and looking down [into the wat]ers, if he shall see his face reflected why shall you find his head turned upside down and also his feet upwards? Yet, when he stares down into the waters, his face shall not be visible, nor can he see it there?

[And] this: The face of people and the shape of trees shall appear turned upside down in the water as this is signified in [the] mystery of the Summons, when it was sent down to the worlds of darkness towards the First Man. Sin[ce ...] in this way it cast itself down [...] like a person [who] plunges headlong into water; so it is also with the Summons. It cast itself headlong to the worlds below, with strength and diligence.

And this too: Shall the person lift his head up from the water, his face shall not be visible to him, being bent below. This characterises the mystery of the Obedience that ascended from below, from with the First Man his father to the Living Spirit.

The face of people and of animals and of trees is visible in the water, hanging upside down. This occurs to the mystery of the stars and the zodiac, which hang upside down and are visible in the great sea. For the face of people and beasts and all trees hang on the root of the stars and the zodiac, being begotten from them. Just as their fathers, who are spread out above, hang upside down; so it is also that the mystery of their fathers is being revealed to them whenever they stare down into the water. This is, that you shall find their shapes inverted.

⟨p.255⟩
Not only this, but [the] child who is born; as the ho[ur] approaches for him to be born, he hangs upside down and is born head first. Indeed, due to this, his mystery shall be revealed in these waters people stare down into.

Behold, you will find the shadow of the person who walks along is joined to the earth all the time. If the person is troubled [...] his shadow up upon the earth [...] above. It is impossible for him to do [... ...] on the earth. See, this mystery is [a] great sign, signifying that the entire body came from the earth and had ascended from the abyss. Again, due to this, its shadow is joined on the earth, turned downwards, and hanging from above.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Substantive inquiry into a natural ‘mystery’ (reflection/inversion) likely preserves the teaching agenda, but the bracketed ‘disciple… apostle’ attribution is a later dialogue frame.
- ¶2: Pure dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle says…”) plus a generic programmatic maxim; high frame register and no systematic cosmological mapping.
- ¶3: Cosmos→cosmos correspondence: physical inversion in water mapped to the descent-mechanics of the Summons toward the First Man; impersonal explanatory voice and named cosmological beings mark the older substrate.
- ¶4: Primary content is a tight structural mapping (visibility shift ↔ ascent of Obedience to the Living Spirit). The closing first-person disclosure (“I have revealed… to you”) shifts into teacher-address, suggesting later editorial/pedagogical overlay within the paragraph.
- ¶5: Core is a cosmological-natural correspondence tying earthly forms to astral ‘fathers’ (stars/zodiac) and their inversion; the excised opening is an editorial teaching-voice marker rather than the impersonal substrate style.
- ¶6: Natural sign (fetal inversion/head-first birth) treated as a ‘mystery’ disclosed by the same inversion-law; remains within the correspondential science without institutional application.
- ¶7: Underlying teaching is an impersonal cosmological anthropology (body–earth origin/binding explained by shadow mechanics). The paragraph begins with direct address (“Behold… you will find”), indicating a later didactic wrapper over older content.

---

## Chapter 103
### Concerning the Five Wonders that the Light Mind shall display in the Elect

*This chapter is dominated by institutional/ethical material (the elect preaching, faith, love, peace for brethren, and legal excommunication in the church). Only one short paragraph (¶7) reads like an impersonal cosmological-soteriological mechanism (Light Mind acting upon the new man) without direct institutional anchoring. The ending (¶9) uses strongly NT-sounding idiom (“vessel”, “pearl without price”), suggesting Christian overlay or late devotional diction.*

The Light Mind puts these five signs in the new man, [whom he has] purified, cleansed, supported and chosen [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic attribution (“Once again the apostle speaks”) is a compilation frame marker; “apostle” is explicitly Layer-2/3 Kephalaia apparatus.
- ¶2: A fivefold list of virtues enacted by the elect through preaching; the mapping is not cosmos→cosmos but behavior within the community (“preach and proclaim”).
- ¶3: Continues virtue-listing in community/hearing-believing register; this is catechetical/institutional rhetoric rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶4: Even through lacunae, the pivot is explicitly communal (“peace of heart for the assembly of his brethren”), an application-to-community register.
- ¶5: Ethical exhortation in immediate social setting (“brothers and sisters who are seated [before] him”); not an internal cosmic correspondence.
- ¶6: Explicit church discipline vocabulary (“legal excommunication”, “found in the church”) is institutional material; this is the clearest pastoral layer.
- ¶7: Impersonal, mechanism-like statement about a named cosmological entity (the Light Mind) acting to produce/establish qualities in the ‘new man’, without direct reference to church offices/discipline. Some uncertainty remains because it presupposes the preceding virtue-list, but the sentence itself stays within a soteriological-cosmic agency register.
- ¶8: Beatitude-style exhortation (“Blessed is every one … is diligent”) addresses the hearer’s piety; application voice rather than structural cosmology.
- ¶9: Devotional climax with idioms that strongly echo NT language (“vessel”; “pearl without price” ≈ ‘pearl of great price’). The lead-in (“The [other] one:”) also signals a later dialogue/redactional presentation rather than substrate exposition.

---

## Chapter 104
### Concerning Food: It shall be allocated to Five Products of the human Body.

*After a brief hagiographic opening formula, the chapter is a compact five-fold, impersonal teaching on how sustenance is distributed into five bodily ‘productions’. There are no Gospel citations, no institutional application, and no exhortation; the discourse stays within a systematic degree-structure (food → five products), consistent with the oldest teaching style despite the non-cosmological subject matter.*

This sustenance of various kinds that people gather in and eat, and enters the body; it shall be distributed to five productions.

The first product is this that shall issue from the person in rapture and rises up in the Mind, and it comes out from all his limbs, and is immeasurable.

The second is this that shall issue from the person in voice and word.

The third is this that shall spring from them in strength and activity.

The fourth is what is engendered by the pleasure of lu[s]t in men and women.

The fifth is this that shall be formed and sculpted in the flesh, and be engendered and come from them. It is this corporeal creature; only its parents can recognise this creature that they beget. They are attentive to its desire and its thought and its [lo]ve every day, all the time. However, these other four forms they neither notice nor are concerned about; because they are not displayed.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame opener (‘Once again… to his disciples’), characteristic of the later Kephalaia editorial apparatus.
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural exposition introducing a numbered five-fold scheme (distribution into five), without address, polemic, or institutional anchoring.
- ¶3: Numbered item in a systematic series; descriptive ‘what it is/does’ voice with internal body-terms (‘Mind’, ‘limbs’), not applied to church or moral instruction.
- ¶4: Continues the numbered five-fold list in the same impersonal expository register.
- ¶5: Same five-fold structural style; no editorial seam or application-voice markers.
- ¶6: Still itemized description within the five-fold production scheme; no exhortation or institutional mapping despite sexual vocabulary.
- ¶7: Completes the fifth item with explanatory contrast (visible fifth vs. unnoticed four). The rhetoric remains descriptive rather than hortatory, and does not pivot to contemporary institutions—suggesting core teaching rather than pastoral application.

---

## Chapter 106
### There is no Joy that shall remain in the World till the End.

*Nearly the entire chapter is a compact, systematic cosmological exposition: the sun and moon cannot fully rejoice during the time of Mixture because (1) they behold the Enemy ruling, and (2) they behold the Living Soul oppressed until its final cleansing and the Enemy’s binding. Only the opening dialogue attribution is later frame material; there is no NT citation and no institutional/pastoral grafting.*

There is not [o]ne person [existing in] the world, standing firm [i]n mixture, for whom joy remains to the end.

Not only people, even the sun and the moon, the light-givers of the heavens, [ ... ] nor are they oppressed, they know not [ ... s]et firm in a summons and an [obe]dience. Nevertheless, they too, they do not rejoice completely.

So, one: Their eyes stare at their Enemy, they [perceiv]e him and also look at his ugly shapes. They [s]ee him, that he is alive and established and is king above and below, in secret and manifest. He does every thing that he wants according to his wil[1].

[The s]econd: They perceive the living soul, they see [i]t and that it is ensnared and set in a great [fetter] above and below, in the tree and in the flesh, [ ... ] with every oppression. It is being pressed, drawn near to [and] sliced and eaten as it comes up and down; from above below and from below above. It [is] despoiled and moved from body to body.

Now, because of their living soul that is loved by them and honoured of them, when they see it in oppression th[ey can] not [re]joice completely.

So, one about the soul; and one about their Enemy whom they see ruling [in] what is not his own. He is proud and satisfied with a wealth that is not his own. Indeed, due to this, they do not rejoice in total; but bear up and swallow their heart until they cleanse the living soul and take it from the Enemy's hands. Then they obliterate the Death that is their Enemy and they gather it in [and fetter it in the lump] for ever and ever.

When they will chain their Enemy in the bond at the [las]t, then they will rejoice and no longer grieve from this t[ime]. Also, they become the very same as they were from the [start] for ever!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The sentence itself is a general cosmological-anthropological claim about Mixture (fits the teaching voice), but the introductory dialogue attribution is a classic Kephalaia frame marker.
- ¶2: Cosmic agents (sun and moon) are treated as personified powers within the system; no citation, no institutional targeting. The rhetoric remains internal to the cosmological situation of Mixture.
- ¶3: Numbered, reasoned structure (“one:”) and description of the Enemy’s modalities (secret/manifest; above/below) reads like an older explanatory teaching layer, not a later application.
- ¶4: Second item in a structured pair; depicts the Living Soul’s oppression and cycling “from body to body” as a cosmological process (cosmos→cosmos), with no turn toward church discipline or polemic.
- ¶5: Continues the same causal explanation internal to the cosmic drama (luminaries’ affect tied to the Living Soul’s condition), without editorial address to an audience.
- ¶6: Summative restatement of the two reasons, moving to an eschatological resolution (cleansing, removal, binding). Still fully within the system: Enemy/Death are cosmic forces, not contemporary institutions.
- ¶7: Classic closure of a cosmological cycle (end returns to the primordial state); no hagiographic closing formula and no pastoral/exhortative pivot, suggesting direct teaching material.

---

## Chapter 107
### Concerning the Form of the Word, that [ ... 30 ... ]

*A compact, systematic five-fold correspondence teaching: the production of a word is analyzed through five mouth/throat members, then mirrored by a five-step artisan process (coin-making). Only the opening attribution is later frame; the teaching itself stays in impersonal, mechanical description without institutional application or NT overlay.*

the production of the word t[hat comes] from the mouth and is heard by the [ears]. [ ... ⟨p.261⟩ ... ] and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips g[at]her it! The word shall come forth through the power of th[ese five] members and be heard outside.

Simila[rly] the coin: One shall pour it out and an [othe]r beat it and another trim it as it is turned, and a[nother] put the stamp on it and another wipe it in the sieve (?). [Behold], these five craftsmen shall shape and beautify [their] coin, and it comes amongst mankind. It becomes a posse[ssion] to be received and given.

This is also the case with the [wor]d, as it is formed and embellished by five [member]s. It comes forth and is heard by the ears [of] others.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a formulaic frame marker (“Once again… He says”), but the substance is a five-member mechanistic analysis (impersonal, degree-like enumeration), characteristic of the older correspondential teaching style.
- ¶2: Pure correspondential analogy structured as a five-fold process (five craftsmen/operations), with no citation formulae, exhortation, or institutional pointing; the mapping remains descriptive and systematic.
- ¶3: A brief impersonal recapitulation that closes the five-fold mapping; no frame, pastoral address, or overlay markers.

---

## Chapter 108
### Concerning the Seed Grain that shall be formed by the Elements, and also be destroyed by them

*Dominantly cosmological/process teaching using a seed-grain analogy: the five elements form/nourish, and the same cycle also entails deprivation/blight as Matter casts its shadow. Only paragraph 3 clearly pivots into institutional application (teachers/elect) while borrowing the analogy’s syntax.*

“This seed grain and this barley grain that you see shall be formed and [...] and beautified by the five elements. Now, the warmth and the cold nourish the seed grain and the entire tree. So, just as these things nourish it [...] it is destroyed [...] through [...] hunger.

“In this way also righteousness gathers the five to it.

“In this way too, a lack and a shortfall shall occur from place to place through them; and they who have chosen it have gathered it in and beautified and attained it, while an affliction shall arise for it through those.

“People also shall be caused to stumble on their account, through the energy of Matter that dwells in them. It has poured its shadow over them, just as it shall also pour its shadow over the elements and destroy the fruits of all the trees.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame attribution: "Once again the apostle... speaks to his disciples" with ecclesial title "builder of the church"—a compilation-layer wrapper.
- ¶2: Impersonal correspondential mechanism within the cosmic/natural system (grain/tree ↔ five elements; warmth/cold; nourishment ↔ destruction) with no institutional targeting or exhortation.
- ¶3: Opens with a system-internal salvific statement (righteousness + five), then pivots to Manichaean institutional agents (teachers/elect) and their activity, indicating editorial application grafted onto an older analogical line.
- ¶4: Continues the internal cycle logic (gain here ↔ lack elsewhere; affliction arising) without explicit church terminology. Attribution of agency (“they who have chosen it”) is ambiguous, but the paragraph still reads as systemic consequence within the elements/mixture framework.
- ¶5: Systemic cosmology of Mixture: Matter as an agent with operations (dwells, pours shadow, destroys fruits) affecting people and elements alike; not exhortation but description of how the mechanism works.

---

## Chapter 109
### Concerning the Fifty Lord's Days; to what Mysteries do they correspond? Or the Second (Fifty); to whose Sign (are they)?

*The chapter opens with a late Q&A frame about catechumen/elect fasting. The oldest layer appears in the central computation-mapping: five garments × five powers → twenty-five limbs/sons, tied to the hypostases Summons/Answer/Obedience and the ascent mechanism. The second half shows strong editorial fatigue: institutional categories (catechumens/elect/church) and then explicit NT-derived narrative justifications (Christ’s desert fasting, Simon the leper, tomb), which are overlay/pastoral rather than substrate.*

Happen you know that [in eac]h one of these five garments that the [First] Man summoned there are five powers. They shall be counted to the [ ... each] other, these five, and five times [ ... ] their number. However, at the time [ ... ] the Summons went down. The Answer wished to ascend with it. And the Summons with the Obedience [constitute] twenty-five characteristics in their twenty-five limbs, [which] are the sons of the Man. The Summons and [the Obed]ience were raised to the heights through them.

⟨p.263⟩ Just as their fa[ther] the living Man raised himself up through them, by these these other ones have set up all things a[bov]e and below. Indeed, these are what are called pentecost, the fifty great days that are the [archetypes of the] holy [d]ays. They fasted from their [foo]d, [w]hich is their own.

So, from that time until [the time] of [the adv]ent of Jesus the Splendour, the glorious one who came [ ... / ... t]heir food which is the [ ... ] of sou[l], which is the Summons and the Obedience [ ... ] that spiritual food. They rele[ased ... ] all these fifty days, which [ ... ] this living food suffices for all of them. It filled them all with power and richness and life. Light [ ... ] this living food that [ ... ] became rich.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic Q&A container (“Once again… disciple… I beseech you, my master… proclaim… persuade us”) plus institutional fasting categories (catechumens/elect). This functions as editorial apparatus rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Core teaching marked by systematic numeric correspondences (five garments × five powers = twenty-five), and by intra-cosmic hypostases acting as agents (Summons/Answer/Obedience) with ascent mechanics; only the initial speaking formula is later frame.
- ¶3: Impersonal cosmological causality and archetype language: ascent of the Living Man through the limbs/sons, and ‘fifty’ treated as a structural/archetypal measure within the system (not yet tied to church practice or NT narrative).
- ¶4: Continues the same intra-cosmic mechanism: ‘food of soul’ identified with hypostases (Summons/Obedience) and described as living/spiritual nourishment. “Jesus the Splendour” functions as a cosmological figure, not as a Gospel citation.
- ¶5: Institutionalization of the scheme: ‘catechumens’ and ‘elect’ fasting practice, counting of “holy days,” and liturgical scheduling. This applies the earlier cosmological arithmetic to church groups—cosmos → institution.
- ¶6: Explicitly anchors the practice in ecclesial revelation and polemic against ‘sects,’ then grounds it in apostolic precedent (“apostles themselves fasted…”). This is Christian-historical legitimization language entering via the NT/Christian milieu rather than the old cosmological substrate.
- ¶7: Direct NT narrative material (temptation scene, Simon the leper, time in the tomb). This entered via Gospel tradition, regardless of any numerological/correspondential use.
- ¶8: First-person bestowal onto ‘the entire church’ with catechumen/elect differentiation—an editorial application of the mysteries to institutional practice (cosmos → church). The paragraph’s function is ecclesial assignment rather than cosmological exposition.

---

## Chapter 110
### Concerning the Nourishment of the Person, for there are Powers in it [ ... / ... ]

*This chapter is overwhelmingly technical and cosmological, describing the body as a purificatory mechanism (counts of seals/rulers; cleansing via voice/word/silence; expulsion and transmigration of dark powers). The only clear later material is a short hagiographic/dialogue opener in ¶1.*

exists [ ... ] daily to the body of the person [ ... ] there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it [ ... ] there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers [ ... ] they are cleansed and purified and enter [ ... ] ⟨p.265⟩ by the voice, by the word, and by the [ ... ... ] quiet with the silence at the time when his heart [ ... ... ] of God the Father.

Also, the twelve [ ... ... ] rulers that belong to the Darkness shall be swept out outside in wandering and in transmigration [ ... ... ] some of them [ ... through] this transmigration, and others among them shall be brought back.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core substrate markers: impersonal, mechanistic description of purification in the body; large enumerated totals (250,000 + 250,000) and process-language (cleansed/purified/enter) stay within the cosmic-physiological system. Mixed only because it is introduced by a formulaic frame opener ("Once again speaks ...").
- ¶2: Old teaching voice: procedural eschatological mechanics (swept out; wandering; transmigration; brought back) applied to a numbered cosmic set (twelve rulers) without exhortation, citation, or institutional application. "Darkness" functions as a cosmic domain/force.

## Chapter 111
### Concerning the Four Archetypes that occur in the Eye, and the Fifth that is hidden in them; to whom do they belong?

*Highly fragmentary, but dominated by cosmological-correspondential teaching: elemental/cosmic attributions (Fire, Darkness/abyss, Light/living air) and a microcosm teaching on the eye/pupil tied to cosmic structure (cardinal points; First Man). Paragraphs 1 and 3 carry a light hagiographic dialogue frame (“Once again he speaks:”) and explanatory address that likely entered with the compilation frame, but the underlying exposition remains internal to the cosmic system (cosmos↔man correspondences) rather than institutional application.*

There are four aspects among mankind that perceive, as they perceive [...] without these four that are hidden in them [...] they belong [...]. So, [...].

[...] that is in the [...] belongs to the fire. The Darkness belongs to the abyss; but the Light that perceives what is hidden in all these things, being clear, is the living air.

How shall the pupil of the eye perceive? While it perceives by day, yet come the night it shall not perceive. What mystery does this matter signify? [...] The pupil of the eye shall not [...] the mystery of the [...] is the door [...] below [...] the other four cardinal points. For in the [...] above. He has [...] great [...] they came down [...] living [...]. The First Man came forth from it and knew all things.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening formula is a classic hagiographic/frame marker; the remaining fragment is a microcosm teaching (four perceiving aspects + a hidden element) stated as ontology rather than exhortation, consistent with the core layer despite lacunae.
- ¶2: Impersonal, correspondential mapping of perceptual principles to cosmic elements (Fire / Darkness-abyss / Light-living air) with no citation, no exhortation, and no reach into contemporary institutions—typical substrate style.
- ¶3: Frame formula plus first-person didactic address suggests compilation framing; underneath is a structured cosmological explanation (eye/pupil as mystery ‘door’, relation to four cardinal points, descent, emergence of First Man) that stays within the cosmic architecture and reads like older systematic teaching.

## Chapter 112
### The Human is less than all the Things of the Universe, and he is rebellious beyond them all.

*This chapter is dominated by hagiographic reminiscence (Mani-on-the-road scene) and polemical/ethical application against humanity’s blasphemy, followed by later catechetical lists (“five” and “three”). The oldest recoverable substrate appears only as embedded cosmological kernels (Light/Darkness mechanics; aeons; descent to the province of death; eschatological binding; Cross of Light as a cosmic presence).*

Jesus did not come and s[a]ve the world because of mankind alone, but [ ... he] came and revealed on earth [ ... ] was strong outside in [ ... ] And [whe]n he had finished doing his [t]ask outside [i]n the great u[ni]verse, he came [ ... he wen]t further with Adam and Eve and revealed [to them].

they belong to the race of ligh[t].

the aeons [of the greatness], how they occur; and [the manner of] the darkness, how it t[o]o exists.

the [l]ight great-[ness], how it has been active against the pow[er of] darkness and conquered it.

he came to the province of death [ ... ... ] that is flesh, until he should find profit and bring them from the gate of the underworld below, for [they had] been swa[ll]owed into it.

the rebels shall be bound in a great fetter

the Cross of Light, which g[rie]ves in the totality, being present in what is visible and what is not vis[ib]le.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic travel-setting about “the apostle” with narrative staging; no systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶2: Continuation of the anecdotal scene and illustrative comparison (mountain vs. gathered flesh). Embedded speech, but still a worldly illustration within a biography-like frame, not the impersonal cosmological system-exposition of the substrate.
- ¶3: Moralizing comparison (mountains/seas silent vs. humanity blaspheming) and anti-theodicy polemic (“no cause of evil after God”); application content rather than cosmological degree-structure.
- ¶4: Polemical denunciation of humanity attributing rulers/demons to God; accusatory voice and ethical/theological disputation, not systematic correspondential cosmology.
- ¶5: Dialogue/Q&A apparatus (“one of his disciples… Tell us, our master”): classic Kephalaia frame layer.
- ¶6: Explicit NT passion complex (cross, enemies, torment/shame) used as argument; this enters via Christian narrative tradition rather than the pre-compilation cosmological substrate.
- ¶7: Kernel is cosmological-task language (Jesus operating “outside… in the great u[ni]verse,” revelation to Adam and Eve) consistent with Manichaean cosmic hierarchy; surrounding dialogue frame and apostolic-mission/catechetical pivot are later.
- ¶8: “race of ligh[t]” is a cosmological-anthropological identity claim; its packaging as a numbered apostolic teaching-item suggests later catechetical editing.
- ¶9: Direct cosmological content (aeons of greatness; Darkness as an ontological domain) fits the substrate’s internal cosmic system; numbered catechesis is later scaffolding.
- ¶10: Light-vs-Darkness mechanics (“active… conquered”) is core cosmological teaching; the didactic list format likely reflects later catechetical arrangement.
- ¶11: Mythic-cosmological descent/rescue schema (province of death; underworld gate; swallowing) reads like substrate mechanism; list packaging is secondary.
- ¶12: Eschatological binding motif can belong to the older cosmic judiciary; explicit linkage to what is “proclaimed by the apostle” is a later authority-anchor.
- ¶13: Frame marker plus community category (“All the faithful who believe…”) introducing a catechetical triad; this is church-facing instruction, not impersonal system description.
- ¶14: Catechetical identity for “the faithful” in virtue/faith terms; no cosmic degree mapping or mechanism.
- ¶15: Institutional loyalty/authority (“believed the apostle who has been manifested”) and blessing language; later community catechesis.
- ¶16: Opening is pastoral ethics (restraint/charity) aimed at community behavior, but the tail is a cosmological statement about the Cross of Light as a pervasive reality spanning visible/invisible—an older kernel embedded in later exhortation.

## Chapter 113
### The Chapter on whether any [Lig]ht comes from the Three Vessels.

*This kephalaion is almost entirely systematic cosmological mechanism (gates, firmaments, rays, purification). The only clearly later material is the dialogue wrapper (“Once again…”, “The apostle says…”), which frames otherwise core teaching and a substantive cosmological question.*

[Preac]h about this bit of life and light that comes from [these three] vessels that surround the universe [ ... ] life comes up [ ... ]

[ ... ] b[u]t should the [ ... ] ascend [ ... ] light of the rays that [ ... ] which are received, which shine and go in through these great open gates, through these doors, firmament by firmament.

For four [gates o]pen in each world, distributed at the four points of the compass, opening downwards to the vessels. So, due to this, the [light of the] rays shall go in through these openings and it shines on [that] place, and pulls through them the light and the little pi[llar (?) that ex]ists there.

From that place [it] shall be poured [out to the] firmaments. It shall also be clarified in these firmaments [ ... ] a part there is cleansed and it is [all] taken up with the living ones. However, its other part [ ... ] shall all [be cast] down.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The opening formula is Kephalaia frame. The remaining question is a substantive cosmological prompt about extraction of light from the three vessels, matching the teaching substrate’s concerns and register.
- ¶2: Mechanistic cosmology (rays, gates/doors, ascent through successive firmaments) in an impersonal explanatory voice, but introduced by a later dialogue attribution.
- ¶3: Systematic spatial/architectural mapping (four gates at compass points; worlds→vessels; rays extracting light) stays entirely within the cosmic system, with no institutional or citation markers.
- ¶4: Pure process-description of purification and sorting (clarified/cleansed vs. cast down; taken up with the living ones) expressed as cosmological mechanics, without frame/pastoral/NT overlay.

## Chapter 114
### Concerning the Three Images that are in the righteous Person.

*After a brief hagiographic opening, the chapter is largely a systematic, process-oriented exposition of a threefold inner structure (spiritual/psychic/corporeal) and the ascent/purification of the living soul through these degrees, culminating in the action of the Light Virgin/Virgin of Light. Only the final paragraph shows probable Christian-moral coloring (virtues; ‘first/last’ formula), while the main mechanism remains cosmological-anthropological rather than institutional/pastoral.*

Three images occur in the elect person.

The first is the spiritual image, which is the new man, which the Light Mind shall form in him; and it goes into him and dwells in him.

The second image is the remnant and remainder of the new man, which is the psychic image that is bound in the flesh, as the old man [ ... since the] times of this psychic image.

The other one is the corporeal image [that is] added to them all.

These three images [ ... ] entirely. When it will [ ... ... ] through the nourishment of food. If it shall sink [ ... ... ] down to the corporeal image. [And] the corporeal, [that] is in it, shall divest itself of it and place it in the [ ... ] image. [ ... ] again it rises up and falls to the [ ... ] image; it shall divest itself of the psychical of the enemies, which [ ... ... ] anger. The lust [ ... ... ... ] with it.

Then shall a Light Virgin come and reveal the spiritual image that is [there], which [is] the new man. That Virgin acts as a guide. [She g]oes on before and it is extended to the heights above, and received into this spiritual image. And she [sculpts] it and adorns it with the new man [wi]thin. It is sealed with all the limbs of this Light Virgin who is present and dwells in the new man.

So, this is [h]ow this living limb shall be [puri]fied and live, the one that comes into the body of [the ri]ghteous one from without through the administration of food of various ki[nds], like this. The living soul shall be clea[n]sed entirely every [d]ay and traverse these three [im]a[ges].

So, it shall divest itself of the body, which is not its own, in the corporeal. It shall also divest itself of the souls that are not its own, these that are mixed with it in the psychical [ ... ... ] anger and desire and [ ... ]-ness and foolishness and envy and strife; and these other wicked teachings that are not its own.

However, in [the] spiritual image itself it shall live. It is the Virgin of Light who robes the new man and who shall be cal[l]ed ‘the hour of life’.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic dialogue-introduction (“Once again… speaks to his disciples”), a classic Kephalaia frame marker.
- ¶2: Programmatic thesis statement introducing a numbered, systematic anthropological structure; no citation, exhortation, or institutional rule.
- ¶3: Degree-language (“first…”) and impersonal mechanism (Light Mind forms/dwells) typical of the systematic teaching layer.
- ¶4: Continues the numbered degree-structure (“second…”) and defines entities by function/state (bound in flesh), not by contemporary application.
- ¶5: Completes the triad in a compact, schematic manner; still purely structural.
- ¶6: Process-description of ascent/stripping through degrees (divesting/placing/rising) is characteristic of core mechanism-teaching; ‘food’ functions here as part of the cosmological economy of mixture/purification rather than a pastoral command.
- ¶7: Mythic agent (Light Virgin) operating within a defined soteriological mechanism (reveal/guide/seal) in impersonal expository voice; cosmos→cosmos operation.
- ¶8: Summative ‘how it works’ explanation of purification-as-process; despite “administration of food,” the paragraph remains descriptive and mechanistic, not disciplinary.
- ¶9: Continues the stripping/mixture logic (foreign accretions removed by degree), still internal to the anthropology of mixture rather than an address to contemporary institutions.
- ¶10: Core mechanism (life in the spiritual image; Virgin of Light robes the new man; ‘hour of life’ epithet) is systematic and mythic. The inserted virtues (faith/love/long-suffering) and ‘first/last’ formula sound like later Christian-moral diction rather than the impersonal degree-mechanics of the substrate.

## Chapter 115
### The Catechumen asks the Apostle: will Rest come about for Someone who has come out of the Body, if the Saints pray over and make an Alms-offering for him?

*The chapter opens and closes in dialogue-frame and quickly pivots into two substantial archetypal “entreaty” narratives (Mother of Life → Living Spirit; then the Five → Third Ambassador) that are strongly cosmological and systematic (CORE). After these, the text drifts into church practice around alms/remembrance for the dead with heavy institutional vocabulary and a seam-flagged ecclesial extension (PASTORAL), consistent with the strong editorial-fatigue signal. A few late sections retain small embedded cosmological phrases (MIXED).*

For this entreaty has been since the beginning [...] the gods first made entreaty of their Father. They were granted their entreaty and their request.

⟨p.272⟩ The Great Spirit, the Mother of Life, she claimed and prayed and besought and glorified and praised the first established one, who is the Father. She besought him an entreaty. She claimed of him a request. She received a great gift; she and the many powers with her.

She besought an entreaty on behalf of the First Man, because of how he had come forth and thrown himself down. He became distant from the Mother of Life. He separated from her and gave himself; in great affliction. He joined war on earth with the destruction and the grief and the weakness; in the worlds of Darkness; in the abysses made of fear; in the midst of the demons' ditch; amongst the powers of the Devil!

Indeed, he is like a king standing in the midst of his enemies. And when the Mother of Life petitioned and prayed and glorified and praised the Father, the first established one, she did petition him that he might send a power; a protector and redeemer and helper for the son of God who is set in affliction.

⟨p.273⟩ Now, just as she had besought and interceded, so likewise was her prayer fulfilled and received into the presence of the Father of Greatness. He turned and gave to her her entreaty: the great power, the Living Spirit, the giver of ease. The Mother of Life and the Living Spirit came with great power to the borders of the territory. They brought the First Man up from that war and struggle, wherein he is set.

He gave himself as a grace for the Mother of Life. He became estranged and far from her, the one whom his enemies (place) in great affliction, the one who is in the land of Darkness since he became weak.

The Living Spirit grasped and took him in. He freed him. He set him right and gave him ease in the land of rest [...] the Living Spirit, the first giver of ease [...] the will of these three. The welcomer [...] find with the welcomer that [...

[...] a great power [...] of the loved one [...] the First Man who had been far away. He had become distant. She had besought on his behalf. He was set fast in affliction. (The Living Spirit) went to the place of destruction and weakness, the one that freed and redeemed him and gave him ease from the affliction. He set him right with rest and joy. He has himself become rested, with him for ever.

[...] and they built the universe and completed it with its firmaments, with its wheels, with the ships of light that are in the heights, with its fastenings, with its earths and its walls, with its vessels. They completed the universe and set it right by the fathers of light.

⟨p.274⟩ Then, the Great Spirit stood with the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder with the Living Spirit and the First Man. These five stood to prayer; they glorified and praised the Father, the first established one. They besought of him an entreaty. They claimed of him a request, that he might give them the power [...] the leader, a guide to all the things of activity that they had constructed. That he might come and purify the living soul that is set in affliction.

They prayed and besought their Father, and he received from them their prayer [...] he gave them their entreaty and their request [...] he summoned from him [...] which is the Third Ambassador. He, he came from the power of his greatness. He saw the entire soul, how it is entangled in a chain and set fast. It sees the things of activity that his brothers have constructed, that have come from the heights. Again, he saw the living soul, that it is entangled and set fast in a great torture. It is oppressed in the stink of the abyss that is joined with it.

And therefore, he, the Ambassador [...] in his charity. So, he revealed his way, before this underworld that is dug deep down, to free the living soul. He redeemed it from all the fleshes of death wherein it is pained. He gave it ease. He set it right in the place of rest and joy.

And again, he too, the Ambassador, he fulfilled the will of these three; the will of his brothers who had besought the Father for him. They claimed of him a request, together with the [...] of the Father, the one that [...] he gave the entreaty that they had besought of him.

The third is the transferral of the living soul that he had freed. He released it and gave it ease from the affliction. He became king and first to the entire body.

Again, let us come to the third entreaty when they besought him the third time [...] displayed his image [...] he hid his image from the [...] he stood firm [...] they revealed [...] the light [...] the First Man [...] mock [...] to him in [...] which is exalted [...] garment [...] they were freed [...] to him [...] their voice [...] the body [...] prayer [...] it [...] beseech [...] the body [...] beseech [...] she besought [...] in [...] be free [...] Ambassador [...] and also [...]

⟨p.276⟩ [...] with the living ones and [...] for ever [...] the first [...] from the [...] he gave him the hope [...]

Also, Jesus the Splendour perfected the will [...] the will [...] they found the light [...] the will of the [...] that he had [...] he gave them the power that is Jesus the Splendour. He granted the will of the elements. [...] for ever. This first [...] he was humbled, he was made free [...] the [...]-ness is displayed to his enemies. He received the hope [...] reveal [...] Jesus the Splendour, the light power [...] and the entreaty of the [...] of light [...]

[...] of the living soul that is entangled and bound in the entire universe. For it shall be freed and cleansed and purified and redeemed

It goes into the land of light [...] comes about for it; and it goes to the land of life and rest [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic scene-setting (“Once again… our enlightener… congregation”) is hagiographic frame.
- ¶2: Direct address + beseeching formula (“I beseech you, my master”) is dialogue frame.
- ¶3: Problem framed in terms of saints’ intercession and retribution-for-deeds; this is community practice/theodicy rather than impersonal cosmological mechanism.
- ¶4: Second beseeching formula and appeal to what is ‘honoured amongst people’—editorial framing.
- ¶5: Institutional categories (elect), faith-language, and direct pastoral assurance; also begins with dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle…”).
- ¶6: Opens with cosmic-historical claim (gods’ primordial entreaty) typical of archetypal substrate, then pivots outward to church/Christ (late application/overlay).
- ¶7: Programmatic transition (“I will instruct you… in what form…”) functions as discourse scaffolding, not cosmological mapping.
- ¶8: Archetypal cosmic beings (Mother of Life, Father) in impersonal prototype narrative; wholly within the cosmic system.
- ¶9: Classic Manichaean cosmological drama (First Man in Darkness/abyss) narrated as prototype; no institutional reach.
- ¶10: Still cosmic prototype (rescue-helper request). “son of God” here functions as a cosmic category within the First Man episode, not a Gospel citation.
- ¶11: System-internal soteriology: Father of Greatness → Living Spirit → extraction of First Man; impersonal cosmic mechanics despite a transitional ‘now/likewise’.
- ¶12: Continues the prototype rescue situation; Darkness treated as cosmic domain.
- ¶13: Cosmic rescue sequence + triadic ‘will of these three’ motif typical of older structural teaching; no church application.
- ¶14: Continues the same archetypal entreaty/provision logic entirely within cosmic geography (place of destruction vs rest/joy).
- ¶15: Meta-instruction (“See now, I have taught you…”) functions as rhetorical recap in teacher-to-audience voice rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶16: Directly applies the archetype to saints praying over the dead; institutional category (‘faithful saints’) and pastoral reassurance.
- ¶17: Cosmic construction catalogue (firmaments/wheels/ships of light) is system-internal and characteristic of the oldest cosmological layer.
- ¶18: Structured ‘five’ assembly of cosmic beings and an archetypal request for a salvific agent; fully internal to the cosmic hierarchy.
- ¶19: Archetypal granting of the Third Ambassador and description of the soul’s entanglement—cosmic mechanics rather than moral exhortation.
- ¶20: Salvific descent/way-revelation in underworld terms; still cosmos→cosmos (underworld, fleshes of death, rest/joy).
- ¶21: Triadic ‘will of these three’ coordination + request/fulfilment motif; system-internal.
- ¶22: Degree/office statement about the freed soul and kingship—cosmological hierarchy language.
- ¶23: Meta-recap (“Behold, I have instructed you…”)—teacherly stitching rather than substrate content.
- ¶24: Explicit institutional address (elect, catechumen) and application of the prototype to community prayer practice.
- ¶25: Despite heavy lacunae, the surviving nouns/actors (First Man, light, image/garment, Ambassador) remain within the cosmic drama rather than church practice.
- ¶26: Fragmentary continuation with no institutional markers; likely part of the same cosmological sequence.
- ¶27: “Jesus the Splendour” appears as a named cosmological power operating with ‘elements’ and ‘will’—not a Gospel citation; register is cosmic despite lacunae.
- ¶28: Second-person anchoring (“you are set firm… Happen you know…”) indicates application voice; too fragmentary to isolate a clean core block.
- ¶29: Mentions “petition of the holy church,” anchoring the mechanism in institutional practice.
- ¶30: Alms/remembrance for the dead and ‘four victories’—explicit community rite framing.
- ¶31: Contains a compact, system-internal liberation formula embedded inside a late ritual list for alms-for-the-dead.
- ¶32: Institutional mapping to ‘holy church’ and ‘children of the church’; alms as ecclesial benefit.
- ¶33: Too fragmentary, but appears as part of the late ‘victories’ enumeration tied to intercession.
- ¶34: Extended kinship list (father/mother/son/daughter) + alms/remembrance ‘of the church’—practical community instruction.
- ¶35: Catechumen + alms and sins language in communal register; not cosmological exposition.
- ¶36: Fragmentary but dominated by kinship/catechumen/sin vocabulary; institutional context.
- ¶37: Fragmentary, but includes catechumen/intercession logic and a Christian token (“Cross”); overall an applied exhortative register.
- ¶38: Analogy about a bound sinner and a relative petitioning—moral/legal example, not cosmic architecture.
- ¶39: Fragment within the same legal-analogy section.
- ¶40: Speaker attribution + restatement of the catechumen’s question in didactic prose; editorial frame within the analogy.
- ¶41: Charity-as-payment logic inside the analogy; community ethics/application.
- ¶42: Seam-flagged institutional extension: catechumen/remembrance in church/holy church + saints’ sin-entreaty; cosmos→church application dominates the whole paragraph.
- ¶43: Although it uses ‘living soul’ vocabulary, the paragraph operates in intercessory/forgiveness-of-sins practice (“in the name of that person”), i.e., applied community soteriology.
- ¶44: Contains a clean cosmological ascent-to-Light clause embedded in a reciprocal-intercession argument.
- ¶45: Direct charge to catechumens to perform alms/remembrances; explicit transmigration count; dialogue attribution (“Then speaks the apostle…”) and strong institutional practice.
- ¶46: Formulaic closing response: praise/obeisance/gratitude to the master—hagiographic closure.

## Chapter 116
### Concerning why if a [Nail] is cut the Person shall not be sick; but if a Limb is cut he shall immediately be pained.

*This chapter is carried almost entirely by a dialogue frame (disciple question → apostle answer → closing reaction). The only recoverable older teaching substrate is the correspondential/analogical explanation in ¶5 (body~earth; limbs~trees; regrowing vs non-regrowing kinds). No NT overlay appears; the frame is hagiographic/compilational rather than Christian-citation.*

Happen you know th[at the] body is like the earth, wh[ile these] limbs als[o ...] of it are like the trees. There are varieties [...] of tree you shall find growing on the earth. They ar[is]e and grow again and stand firm. They have not [... ...] Again, there are others that if they are cut shall not [grow ...] and they grow, as they are li[ke ...] which shall be cut and grow [...] and [...] they too that occur [...] they shall be cut and not grow, resembling [... neither] grow nor shall they [... ...] it [sh]all please him on [...] and grows [...] in impurity [... ...] from him. This flesh that is corrupt [... ⟨p.282⟩ ...] in the liv[in]g flesh and they [... ...] are somewhat sick.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue setup: disciple questions the apostle; this is the Kephalaia’s hagiographic frame voice.
- ¶2: Direct-address question to “my master” in Q&A format; despite containing the problem statement (hair/nails vs limbs), it functions as framed petition rather than impersonal systematic exposition.
- ¶3: Explicit beseeching formula (“I besee[ch you], my master”) typical of the editorial Q&A container.
- ¶4: Dialogue attribution (“Then the a[postle] speaks…”) is a layer-2 frame marker; not part of the oldest substrate.
- ¶5: Core substrate appears as an impersonal correspondential mapping within a natural/cosmic-style microcosm (body~earth; limbs~trees; kinds that regrow vs kinds that do not), without institutional application or Gospel citation. The paragraph is MIXED only because it is introduced by explicit Q&A self-reference.
- ¶6: Concluding ‘lesson delivered’ formula (“Behold, [I have tau/ght you]…”) is editorial closure rather than core exposition.
- ¶7: Audience reaction/scene management (“disciple heard… he sat down”) is narrative frame.

The [sec]ond [ ... ] if he shall fall [ ... ... ] one [ ... ... ] one hour [ ... ... ] others who shall [ ... ... ] and they remain. They shall delay until they [ ... ... ] he is freed, and they delay to come toward [ ... ... ]

## Chapter 119
### Chapter 119

*Core substrate survives mainly as a cosmological/hierarchical depiction of the First Man’s commissioning, steadfastness in the worlds of darkness, and the purifying/separating work against the Enemy. A later sermonic/pastoral layer intensifies in the second half (esp. ¶6, ¶9), turning the cosmology into direct exhortation and introducing church categories (notably “catechumen”) as an interpretive graft. No explicit NT citation formula appears; the Christian-sounding titles (“only begotten”, “righteous”) function here as internal honorifics rather than Gospel overlay.*

⟨p.285⟩[ ... ] and the [righte]o[usness ... ] is more than [the ... ] the voice through them.

This one, whom they called “approved” [ ... ... ] they approved him in the midst of the ae[ons of] light. These numerous multitudes set him a[part], and he came forth against the Enemy. They gave this name [ ... ... ] this matter [ ... ... ] who are numerous, that are in the aeons of light and who [are without] measure or number.

This one, [whom they called] “righteous”, because he is the first righteous one who had no sin amongst all of the aeons that belong to the light and the thought [ ... ] they knew him, so that [ ... ] his Father, who had sent him with five sons [ ... ] perdition.

This one also [whom they called] “only begotten”, because he was established [ ... ] pour [ ... ] Enemy.

Again, just as the only begotten ones from your [only] begotten race and you [ ... ] according to flesh [ ... ... o]nly begot-ten also from gold and silver and all the [ ... ] of the universe.

⟨p.286⟩Just as your father, [the F]irst Man, himself stood firm in the [midst] of all the worlds of darkness. He did not love [the] worlds of darkness, nor did he lust for them! Rather, h[is] thought and love for the household of his people is in h[i]m. Anything else that the Enemy [ ... ] with his violent de[e]ds. Behold, (the First Man) toils and struggles; so that (the Enemy) would be separat[e from] them and be cast out from them. And he purifies them and ma[ke]s them f[r]ee, and makes them as they were from the beginning.

[ ... ] may they discover the remembrance of the household of his peo[ple], as he remains in this [place ni]ght and day.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Highly uncertain due to heavy lacunae and low lexical signals; no overt frame/pastoral markers remain, and the fragment reads like part of an impersonal teaching sequence rather than dialogue frame.
- ¶2: Dialogue attribution with “The a[postle] speaks [to] them …” functions as compilation frame (Layer 2); no recoverable substrate teaching is preserved in the surviving words.
- ¶3: Impersonal, cosmological commissioning scene: aeons of light, innumerable multitudes, being “set apart” and sent against the Enemy—cosmos→cosmos description without institutional application.
- ¶4: Core logic is cosmic (primordial righteousness across the aeons; Father sending with five sons against perdition), but the ‘first catechumen’ phrase crosses into institutional present and reads like an editorial application inserted mid-sentence.
- ¶5: Title-explication remains internal to the mythic commissioning; despite Christian resonance, there is no NT citation vector and the function is cosmological (a named role/epithet within the system).
- ¶6: Strong application voice (“I tell you, my brothers and my limbs”), moral modeling, and explicit community categories (catechumens/rebels). This is sermonic exhortation using the myth as precedent—editorial/pastoral layer, especially consistent with chapter’s fatigue drift.
- ¶7: Correspondential/cosmological comparison (race/flesh alongside gold/silver/universe) with no direct exhortation; reads as internal teaching about ‘only-begotten’ status across registers.
- ¶8: Cosmic-process description (steadfastness in darkness; separation and purification; restoration ‘as from the beginning’). The possessive ‘your father’ sounds sermonic, but the substance remains cosmos→cosmos mechanics rather than institutional application.
- ¶9: Direct second-person exhortation (“You too… stand firm in this body… and all its lusts”) applies the myth to audience ascetic practice—application layer rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶10: Fragmentary continuation of the ‘household of his people’ motif; likely still within the First Man mythic situation (‘remains… night and day’). The optative ‘may they’ could be rhetorical, but no clear institutional marker survives.
- ¶11: Formulaic closing: disciples rejoice, give glory, praise, obeisance—hagiographic frame wrapping the teaching.

## Chapter 120
### Concerning the Two Essences

*This chapter is dominated by polemical address, moral examples, and Christianized/judgment language (strong application drift in the second half). The only clear recoverable substrate fragment is the bare cosmological axiom of the Two Essences (Light/Darkness) embedded in a hagiographic-looking frame sentence.*

the] two essences that are present at the beginning [ ... ] the Light and the Darkness, that which is good and that which is evi[], life and] death.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The extracted clause is a compact, impersonal cosmological axiom (Two Essences at the beginning) characteristic of the dualist substrate; the surrounding visionary-narrative and speech-introduction is frame material.
- ¶2: Second-person address and argumentative setup against monotheists ("you... among you... they say") anchors the discourse in a contemporary dispute rather than cosmos→cosmos exposition.
- ¶3: Catalog of social/moral evils (lying, slander, adultery, theft, idol-worship, Mammon, etc.) functions as polemical illustration in the editor’s present world, not as a systematic cosmological mapping.
- ¶4: Direct exhortation/imperatives and threat logic ("why do you annul... do them!... woes") is application-voice ethical polemic, not substrate cosmology.
- ¶5: Argumentation is tied to Christianized authority language ("beloved son", "apostles") and a tree/fruit proof-text trajectory; this enters the Kephalaia through Mani’s/Manichaean Christian synthesis rather than the older impersonal teaching layer.
- ¶6: Explicitly Christian Trinitarian-style phrasing ("beloved son... holy spirit") and the five-trees/fruit saying marks a New-Testament-shaped proof-text layer (even if paraphrased rather than introduced by a formal citation formula).
- ¶7: Eschatological condemnation scene with "beloved son", "holy spirit", "last day", "advent" is Christian overlay in exhortation mode.
- ¶8: Continues judgment/exhortation ("I have recounted to you... Judge of truth") in application voice; framed as impending adjudication rather than cosmological mechanism, consistent with late overlay/paraenesis.

## Chapter 121
### Concerning the Sect of the Basket

*This kephalaion is front-loaded with hagiographic scene-setting and anti-sectarian/application material about “the basket” (¶1–4). The cosmological-teaching substrate appears only in the broken fragments of ¶5–6 (pillars, angels, cosmic supports, Living Spirit garments, Porter). ¶5 is especially unstable: a brief institutional phrase (“holy church”) intrudes into an otherwise cosmological enumeration, consistent with the chapter-level fatigue signal.*

The [...] and the great [...] and the great Mind that is [...] this Youth that is with the [...] thirty-six angels [...] twenty-two angels [...] but [...] of the twenty-two [...] ⟨p.290⟩ the seven pillars that stan[d ...] the sea that surrounds the universe [...] four angels that are distributed to the [...] universe, supporting the mightiness [...] below, which is the earth of all the heavens [...] these other five gods, the sons of the Man, they that [...] the universe is stood firm [...] the Summons and the Obedience [...] the splendour of the [...] and the Adamant of Light [...] they are become enemies [...] they guide it.

The third also, [...] below, there are twelve powers standing firm there. However, the number of the twelve images [...] the three great pillars of mightiness that [...] pillars supporting the disk [...] the other three [...] they are the garments that the Living Spirit [...] he put them at that time [...] that threw them, together with [...] which is displayed therein [...] this [...] perfect before [...] great Porter [...] in his power [...] these are the three great [...] against [...] are in the midst of people [...] knows [...] entrusts his soul to them [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic narrative container: setting in Babylon, arrival of a presbyter, labels like “our enlightener” and “apostle”; biographical scene-setting rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Sect-descriptive/etiological explanation of a contemporary group-name (“sect of the ‘basket’”) with mundane fruit-picking imagery; not a cosmos→cosmos mapping but a present-world referent (how they name/handle fruit).
- ¶3: Dialogue attribution (“The apostle speaks…”) plus explicit application to a sectarian identity claim and alms-language. The universe-as-tree analogy is used polemically (“how then do you call yourself…”) and anchors to institutional practice (“alms”), so it functions as editorial application rather than substrate cosmology.
- ¶4: Blessing/exhortation about alms and relocation of goods into “the basket, which is the holy church!”—explicit institutional mapping (cosmic metaphor used to point to church practice).
- ¶5: Underlying content is enumerative cosmological architecture (Mind/Youth, angelic counts, seven pillars, encircling sea, four supporting angels, earth-of-heavens, sons of the Man, Summons/Obedience). The brief “holy church” phrase is a register-crossing intrusion consistent with late editorial application/fatigue, so the paragraph is treated as a core list with a small institutional graft.
- ¶6: Impersonal, structural cosmology: twelve powers/images; three great pillars; cosmic disk; ‘garments’ of the Living Spirit; Great Porter—i.e., a world-support mechanism list. No citation formulae or institutional identifications; the mention of ‘people/soul’ remains within soteriological mechanics rather than a contemporary church rule.

## Chapter 122
### Concerning the 'Assent' and the 'Amen'

*This chapter is dominated by late liturgical/institutional application (assent/amen in congregational prayer, sealing, alms, healing, temptation among the elect), with only a small cosmological substrate explaining the divine archetypes (Summons/Obedience as "portals" redeeming the First Man). The editorial fatigue signal matches the text: after the initial cosmological explanation, the chapter drifts into church practice and exempla. A brief Christian/historical notice about "apostles" appears near the end.*

The assent and the amen have come about from these great ones of the powers. They were given the name as the assent belongs to the summons and the amen belongs to the answer.

Just as the summons and the obedience are the ones that opened the portals of mercy and entreaty for the First Man, so they also became portals for the Mother of Life and the Father of the living. The First Man sought and found his redeemer. And also the Mother of Life and the Father of the living sought [...] this runner, who came forth from them, who is the First Man; but the summons and the obedience became the portals for them.

⟨p.293⟩
Now, in this way they have become portals for the aeons of greatness towards the First Man, and the soul that was conjoined with death. They became helpers [...] of the First Man during his ascent. They gave him [...] the Father of Life and the Mother of Life [...]. They are purifiers of the living soul, being helpers and bestowers of remembrance for it, be it either in [...] in the tree or in the creation of flesh [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Disciple-to-master question formula (hagiographic dialogue frame) with direct address "my master" and request to be taught.
- ¶2: Underlying content is cosmological etiology (assent/amen grounded in "great ones of the powers"; mapped onto Summons/Answer), but introduced by a clear dialogue-frame formula.
- ¶3: Impersonal, process/cosmology exposition: Summons/Obedience as cosmic mechanisms (“portals”) enabling redemption; named cosmic beings (First Man, Mother of Life). No institutional address or moral exhortation.
- ¶4: Opens as continuation of the same cosmological mechanism (portals/helpers/purifiers; ascent; aeons), then shifts into explicit church/institution vocabulary (“holy church”, “teacher”), crossing cosmos→community.
- ¶5: Liturgical instruction about uttering a formula over psalm/prayer and its effect on the blesser; institutional prayer practice rather than cosmic architecture.
- ¶6: Congregational response formula (“verily and amen”) sealing an entreaty; purely church practice.
- ¶7: Direct address (“Happen you know… all of you”), communal benefit language (“came to you profitably”), and daily rebirth motif tied to the congregation; application voice rather than impersonal cosmology.
- ¶8: Mechanism depends on crowds uttering assent/amen; includes explicit institutional virtues (“alms”) and depicts liturgical efficacy (sound sculpting an image). Cosmos-language is harnessed to practice, so the whole paragraph is late application.
- ¶9: Prayer-for-the-sick and bodily danger; practical ritual efficacy rather than systemic cosmological mapping.
- ¶10: Continuation of healing/help motif from assent/amen in applied setting.
- ¶11: Explicit community category (“elect”), moral/temptation counseling, charity request, confession; institutional soteriology and pastoral care.
- ¶12: Applied prayer practice over a person; therapeutic effect language (help/power).
- ¶13: Uses cosmic imagery (“go up to the ships”) but still as a function of the assent/amen uttered in practice; not a standalone cosmological exposition.
- ¶14: Extended applied scenario (danger/enemies/goods/judgement; being saved when assent/amen are called over him). Application-voice density confirms situational exemplum.
- ¶15: Direct address/imperative tone (“Behold… your own cry”), continuing applied exhortation about responding with amen.
- ¶16: Historical/authority anchoring in "apostles" and "first fathers" reflects Christian-heritage overlay and community legitimation rather than the impersonal substrate.
- ¶17: Formulaic closing response (“he glorified…”), characteristic of the redactional frame.
